Method: VOICE uses contrastive learning over 23 million cells to align cell-centered H&E morphology embeddings (from a pathology foundation model) with single-cell expression embeddings (from a transcriptome foundation model). It then predicts expression through two branches: (1) direct regression from morphology, and (2) retrieval of measured expression from similar reference cells to recover genes lacking morphological signal. The two branches are fused with per-gene weights to account for varying morphological predictability.

Result: VOICE generalizes to held-out patients, slides, and partially overlapping Xenium gene panels, and consistently outperforms prior single-cell expression prediction methods on seven metrics.

Conclusion: Predicting single-cell gene expression from routine H&E images is a feasible and scalable approach that can bring molecular analysis to large tissue archives, with VOICE demonstrating strong generalization and performance.

Abstract: Spatial transcriptomics can resolve gene expression at single-cell resolution, but it is costly, limited to targeted panels of a few hundred to a few thousand genes, and applicable to only a small number of samples. H&E imaging, by contrast, is cheap and collected routinely at scale. This makes predicting single-cell expression directly from morphology a practical way to bring molecular analysis to large tissue archives. We therefore present VOICE, a multimodal foundation model that predicts single-cell gene expression from H&E images using paired Xenium data. VOICE first aligns cell centered H&E morphology from a pathology foundation model with single-cell expression embeddings from a transcriptome foundation model, trained using contrastive learning over 23 million cells. Next it predicts expression through two branches. One branch directly regresses expression from morphology. The other branch retrieves measured expression from similar reference cells, recovering genes that do not have morphological signal. Because genes vary in morphological predictability, VOICE fuses the two branches with a per-gene weight. After training, VOICE generalizes to heldout patients, slides, and partially overlapping gene panels from Xenium, and it consistently outperforms prior single-cell expression prediction methods on seven metrics.

</details>


### [93] [PARAGraph: Pathology-Anatomy-Aware Hierarchical Graph for Diabetic Retinopathy Grading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08368)
*Ziyang Zhang,Yuankai Huo,Yalin Zheng,He Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PARAGraph is a Pathology-Anatomy-Aware Hierarchical Graph framework for diabetic retinopathy (DR) severity grading that models clinically grounded evidence (lesion types and spatial relations) via a three-level hierarchical graph, using an optic disc-fovea-anchored coordinate frame for scale/rotation normalization and a dual-fusion strategy to handle noisy lesion segmentation. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods on Messidor-2, APTOS, and DDR while remaining clinically interpretable and robust.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Most deep learning models for DR grading treat it as image-level classification and fail to explicitly model clinically grounded evidence such as lesion types and their spatial relations, limiting interpretability and clinical reliability.

Method: PARAGraph builds a three-level hierarchical graph per image with lesion-level nodes, intermediate category/region nodes, and global anatomical/semantic nodes. It uses an optic disc-fovea-anchored coordinate frame for scale- and rotation-normalized retinal reference, encodes lesion nodes with category, normalized area, and anatomical coordinates, and employs a dual-fusion strategy combining global visual context into a graph semantic node and a decision-level prediction branch to mitigate noisy lesion segmentation.

Result: PARAGraph achieves consistent DR grading performance over state-of-the-art methods on Messidor-2, APTOS, and DDR, with interpretability and robustness analyses confirming clinically grounded predictions and robustness to lesion segmentation noise.

Conclusion: Explicitly incorporating pathology and anatomy priors via hierarchical graph modeling improves both DR grading accuracy and clinical interpretability, offering a robust alternative to pure image-level classification approaches.

Abstract: Diabetic retinopathy (DR) remains a leading cause of vision loss among working-age adults worldwide, making reliable severity grading clinically important. Despite strong performance, most deep models formulate DR grading as image-level classification and do not explicitly model clinically grounded evidence, such as lesion types and spatial relations. In this paper, we propose PARAGraph, a Pathology-Anatomy-Aware Hierarchical Graph framework for DR grading. PARAGraph represents each image as a three-level hierarchical graph with lesion-level nodes, intermediate category and region nodes, and global anatomical and semantic nodes. To incorporate medical priors into nodes, we construct an optic disc-fovea-anchored coordinate frame that provides a scale- and rotation-normalized retinal reference system. Within this frame, lesion nodes are encoded with category, normalized area, and anatomical coordinates. To mitigate noisy lesion segmentation, PARAGraph uses a dual-fusion strategy that introduces global visual context into a graph semantic node and a decision-level prediction branch, improving robustness when lesion evidence is unreliable. Extensive experiments on Messidor-2, APTOS, and DDR show that PARAGraph achieves consistent DR grading performance over state-of-the-art methods. Interpretability and robustness analyses further demonstrate that its predictions are clinically grounded, closely associated with lesion evidence and robust to lesion segmentation noise.

</details>


### [94] [Gated Spatial Redundancy Projection for Pathology Transformer Attentions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08374)
*Zhiyuan Yang,Jiahao Cheng,Vincent Quoc-Huy Trinh,Mahdi S. Hosseini*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper identifies local spatial redundancy in whole-slide images (WSIs) as a pathology-specific failure mode of self-attention in transformer models, and proposes Gated Spatial Redundancy Projection (Gated SRP), a lightweight drop-in module that projects attention outputs onto local redundancy axes and applies a learned signed gate to correct redundancy-aligned components. The method achieves state-of-the-art results on five TCGA survival cohorts and five slide-level classification datasets with negligible parameter overhead (+0.02%).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: WSIs differ fundamentally from natural images in that neighbouring patches often contain highly similar tissue type, stain, texture, and cellular composition. This local spatial redundancy causes self-attention to repeatedly mix dominant neighbourhood features into patch tokens, weakening subtle diagnostic or prognostic signals. The paper aims to address this pathology-specific failure mode of self-attention.

Method: Proposes Gated Spatial Redundancy Projection (Gated SRP), a lightweight drop-in correction module for self-attention layers. For each patch token and attention head, it: (1) estimates a local redundancy axis from neighbouring value vectors, (2) projects the attention output onto this axis, and (3) applies a learned signed gate to geometrically correct the redundancy-aligned component.

Result: Across five TCGA survival cohorts, Gated SRP achieves the highest mean C-index among compared attention variants in all cohorts, with an average improvement over base attention while adding only +0.02% parameters. Across five slide-level classification datasets, it improves base attention on 12 of 16 reported metrics and achieves the best AUC on three datasets.

Conclusion: Gated SRP effectively mitigates the local spatial redundancy problem in pathology WSIs with minimal parameter overhead, improving both survival analysis and classification performance across multiple benchmark datasets. Code is publicly available.

Abstract: Transformer models are increasingly used for whole-slide image analysis in computational pathology. Yet, WSIs differ fundamentally from natural images: neighbouring patches often contain highly similar tissue type, stain, texture, and cellular composition. We identify this local spatial redundancy as a pathology-specific failure mode of self-attention, where dominant neighbourhood features can be repeatedly mixed into patch-tokens and weaken subtle diagnostic or prognostic deviations. We propose Gated Spatial Redundancy Projection (Gated SRP), a lightweight drop-in correction module for self-attention layers. For each patch token and attention head, Gated SRP estimates a local redundancy axis from neighbouring value vectors, projects the attention output onto this axis, and applies a learned signed gate to correct the redundancy-aligned component geometrically. Across five TCGA survival cohorts, Gated SRP obtains the highest mean C-index among the compared attention variants in all cohorts, with an average improvement over the base attention, while adding only +0.02% parameters. Across five slide-level classification datasets, it improves the base attention on 12 of 16 reported metrics and achieves the best AUC on three datasets. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/AtlasAnalyticsLab/GatedSRP.

</details>


### [95] [DoRF++: Spherical Representation Learning over Doppler Radiance Fields for Robust Wi-Fi Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08381)
*Navid Hasanzadeh,Shahrokh Valaee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DoRF/DoRF++ brings neural radiance fields (NeRF) from computer vision into Wi-Fi sensing by modeling Doppler velocity projections extracted from CSI as virtual-camera views of human motion, recovering a latent 3D motion sequence, and applying spherical Transformers for activity classification, achieving state-of-the-art cross-user generalization in Wi-Fi-based human activity recognition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wi-Fi CSI-based human activity recognition (HAR) has grown rapidly due to the IEEE 802.11bf standardization effort, and Doppler velocity projections have shown robustness and generalization benefits. However, reliable generalization under real-world variability remains a major challenge, hindering real-world adoption of Wi-Fi sensing.

Method: DoRF models Doppler velocity projections from Wi-Fi CSI as sparse, diverse virtual-camera views of human motion, infers a latent 3D motion sequence whose projections along learned effective Doppler directions explain the CSI-derived Doppler observations, and projects the recovered motion onto an equiangular grid on the unit sphere. DoRF++ extends this with a spherical-learning design using spherical Transformers for activity classification.

Result: Experiments on a collected hand-gesture dataset show DoRF++ significantly outperforms state-of-the-art Wi-Fi-based HAR methods in cross-user generalization accuracy, especially for difficult gestures with a single multi-antenna receiver access point (AP).

Conclusion: Bringing NeRF concepts into Wi-Fi sensing with spherical representations enables stronger cross-user generalization for Wi-Fi-based HAR, advancing the practicality of device-free, privacy-preserving Wi-Fi sensing.

Abstract: Motivated by the IEEE 802.11bf effort to standardize advanced WLAN sensing, interest in Wi-Fi Channel State Information (CSI) for passive, device-free, and privacy-preserving activity and gesture recognition has grown rapidly. Recent studies have shown that Doppler velocity projections extracted from CSI, which directly reflect human-motion velocity, enable more robust human activity recognition (HAR) and stronger generalization across users and unseen conditions. Nevertheless, reliable generalization under real-world variability remains a major challenge, hindering the adoption of Wi-Fi sensing in real-world applications. To address this challenge, we introduce Doppler Radiance Fields (DoRF), bringing the concept of neural radiance fields (NeRF) from computer vision into Wi-Fi sensing. DoRF models Doppler velocity projections extracted from Wi-Fi CSI as sparse and diverse virtual-camera views of human motion. It then infers a latent 3D motion sequence whose projections along learned effective Doppler directions explain the CSI-derived Doppler observations. The recovered motion is subsequently projected onto an equiangular grid of directions on the unit sphere, producing a spherical representation of the underlying motion. Since DoRF naturally defines the Doppler representation on spheres, we further introduce DoRF++, a spherical-learning design that applies spherical Transformers for activity classification. Experiments on our collected hand-gesture dataset show that DoRF++ significantly outperforms state-of-the-art Wi-Fi-based HAR methods in cross-user generalization accuracy, especially for difficult gestures in settings with a single multi-antenna receiver access point (AP).

</details>


### [96] [Anatomically Consistent Cross-Contrast Super-Resolution of Anisotropic Brain T2w MRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08401)
*Mengqi Shen,Haicheng Wang,Meghna Trivedi,Tony J. Wang,Yuanguang Xu,Yingyan Zeng,Yading Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VIPP-SR is a cross-contrast guided super-resolution framework that restores inter-plane resolution of anisotropic T2w MRI volumes without isotropic ground-truth T2w, using a view-independent patched generator trained on T1c-to-T2w correspondence and a projection-based optimization for inter-plane consistency, improving downstream segmentation Dice scores on both in-distribution (BraTS-MET) and cross-cohort (BraTS-GLI) data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: T2w brain MRI is important for neuro-oncology but acquired with anisotropic voxels, causing blurring and stair-stepping on non-axial views that obscures small structures and weakens downstream 3D analysis. Existing methods require isotropic ground-truth T2w which is often unavailable.

Method: VIPP-SR trains a view-independent patched generator (VIP-GAN) that learns local T1c-to-T2w anatomical correspondence from high-resolution axial slices. The generator is applied to axial, coronal, and sagittal views of the T1c volume to produce three orthogonal T2w estimates. Shape-preserving patching and deepest-skip removal prevent view-specific shortcuts, enabling zero-shot inter-plane transfer. A projection-based optimization enforces anatomical consistency across the three views by balancing inter-plane self-consistency and per-view data fidelity.

Result: VIPP-SR improves downstream segmentation over the real anisotropic T2w baseline, raising mean-label Dice from 0.330 to 0.465 on BraTS-MET and, zero-shot, from 0.473 to 0.563 on BraTS-GLI. Ablation studies identify inter-plane self-consistency as the main source of gain.

Conclusion: The cross-contrast guided approach enables super-resolution of anisotropic T2w volumes without isotropic ground truth, generalizes across cohorts, and improves downstream analysis through inter-plane consistency enforcement.

Abstract: T2-weighted (T2w) brain MRI provides fluid-sensitive soft-tissue contrast that is important for neuro-oncology and radiotherapy planning. However, T2w scans are acquired with anisotropic voxels and appear blurred or stair-stepped on coronal and sagittal views, which obscures small structures and weakens any downstream 3D analysis. We propose VIPP-SR (View-Independent Patched Projection Super-Resolution), a cross-contrast guided super-resolution framework that restores the inter-plane resolution of an existing anisotropic T2w volume without an isotropic ground-truth T2w. VIPP-SR first trains a view-independent patched generator (VIP-GAN) to learn local T1c-to-T2w anatomical correspondence from high-resolution axial slices. The trained generator is then applied to axial, coronal, and sagittal views of the T1c volume to generate three orthogonal T2w estimates. Shape-preserving patching and deepest-skip removal reduce view-specific shortcuts, thereby constraining the generator to learn patch-local representations and enabling the zero-shot inter-plane transfer. Central to VIPP-SR, a projection-based optimization then enforces anatomical consistency across the three view-specific volumes, fusing them by balancing inter-plane self-consistency against per-view data fidelity. The generator is trained on BraTS-MET and evaluated on both the held-out BraTS-MET testing set and the BraTS-GLI cohort without retraining, assessing the cross-cohort generalizability. The results validate that VIPP-SR improves downstream segmentation over the real anisotropic T2w baseline, raising mean-label Dice from 0.330 to 0.465 on BraTS-MET and, zero-shot, from 0.473 to 0.563 on BraTS-GLI and ablation studies identify inter-plane self-consistency as the main source of the gain.

</details>


### [97] [Agentic AI-powered flexible fiber-bundle endoscopy for high-resolution NIR-II fluorescence imaging in vivo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08402)
*Yanzhao Shi,Yuanhua Liu,Sixin Xu,Wayne Jason Li,Yuyuan Chen,Danyang Xu,Zhisheng Wu,Hanze Yu,Ian Yu-Hong Wong,Simon Ying-Kit Law,Hongjie Dai,Liangqiong Qu,Feifei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents an AI-powered flexible endoscopy platform using optical-computational co-design to overcome long-standing limitations of fiber-bundle endoscopy (low resolution, honeycomb artifacts, inter-core crosstalk). The system combines optimized ultrathin fiber bundles enabling high-fidelity imaging from visible to NIR-II wavelengths with an Agent-Guided Mixture-of-Experts (GAME) pipeline using a vision-language model to dynamically route images to restoration experts, achieving fourfold resolution improvement beyond the Nyquist-Shannon limit and demonstrating in vivo imaging in mice and human-tissue models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fiber-bundle endoscopy has been limited since the 1950s by low spatial resolution, honeycomb artifacts, and inter-core crosstalk, which become more pronounced at NIR-II wavelengths (1000-3000 nm) — a spectral window offering superior contrast, resolution, and tissue penetration. The goal is to overcome these longstanding optical and computational constraints to enable high-fidelity clinical fluorescence imaging through natural orifices.

Method: The method employs optical-computational co-design: (1) optimizing ultrathin fiber bundles to reduce crosstalk-induced image blur and enable high-fidelity image transmission across visible-to-NIR-II spectra; (2) developing an Agent-Guided Mixture-of-Experts (GAME) pipeline that uses a vision-language model to dynamically route diverse biomedical images to suitable restoration experts for honeycomb-artifact removal and image restoration.

Result: The GAME pipeline provides a unified restoration entry point for diverse biomedical images (cell, mouse, human samples), achieving a fourfold resolution improvement beyond the Nyquist-Shannon sampling limit. Utility is demonstrated through in vivo NIR-II imaging of anatomical structures in mice, as well as DMD-projected human gastric tube and lymphatic system imaging.

Conclusion: The AI-powered endoscopy platform successfully overcomes the classic limitations of fiber-bundle endoscopy by combining optimized ultrathin fiber bundles with an agent-guided AI restoration pipeline, enabling high-resolution visible-to-NIR-II fluorescence imaging and paving the way for future clinical translation.

Abstract: Fiber-bundle endoscopy offers a compact and flexible route for clinical fluorescence imaging through natural human orifices, but since its first report in the 1950s, it has remained limited by low spatial resolution, honeycomb artifacts, and inter-core crosstalk. The crosstalk becomes more pronounced at near-infrared-II wavelengths (NIR-II, 1000-3000 nm), a spectral window that offers superior contrast, resolution, and tissue penetration depth for biomedical imaging. Here, we present an AI-powered flexible endoscopy platform that overcomes these constraints through optical-computational co-design: optimizing ultrathin fiber bundles to mitigate crosstalk-induced image blur and enable high-fidelity image transmission across the visible-to-NIR-II spectral range, and developing an Agent-Guided Mixture-of-Experts (GAME) pipeline for honeycomb-artifact removal and image restoration. GAME provides a single restoration entry point for diverse biomedical images acquired with our endoscope, spanning cell, mouse and human samples. It dynamically routes each input to suitable restoration experts via a vision-language model, facilitating image reconstruction with a fourfold resolution improvement beyond the NyquistShannon sampling limit. The utility of our endoscope is demonstrated through in vivo NIR-II imaging of anatomical structures in mice, as well as imaging of the digital micromirror device (DMD)-projected human gastric tube and lymphatic system, paving the way for future clinical translation.

</details>


### [98] [Learning Deep Modality-Shared Self-Expressiveness for Image Clustering with Textual Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08418)
*Xianghan Meng,Wei He,Zhiyuan Huang,Chun-Guang Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DeepMORSE is a principled approach for image clustering that uses a modality-shared self-expressive model to discover cross-modal structures while learning structured representations conforming to modality-specific subspaces, achieving state-of-the-art results on six benchmarks and showing strong transferability to downstream tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLM-based image clustering methods align multimodal representations by directly enforcing cross-modal agreement (e.g., maximizing image-text similarity), which fails to explicitly model intrinsic structure within each modality, potentially yielding unreliable alignment or distorting modality-specific structures crucial for clustering.

Method: DeepMORSE proposes a deep modality-shared self-expressive model that discovers cross-modal structures via a shared self-expressive coefficient while simultaneously learning structured representations that conform to a union of modality-specific subspaces. It also provides theoretical justification that the modality-shared self-expressive coefficients suppress inter-class noise toward a subspace-preserving solution, and shows mini-batch optimization introduces implicit regularization.

Result: Evaluated on six image clustering benchmarks, DeepMORSE achieves performance improvements exceeding 3% on UCF-101, DTD-47, and ImageNet-Dogs. It also achieves state-of-the-art performance on downstream tasks including image retrieval and zero-shot classification without task-specific losses or post-processing, demonstrating strong transferability.

Conclusion: DeepMORSE demonstrates that explicitly modeling modality-specific subspace structures while sharing cross-modal self-expressive structures leads to more reliable clustering and better transferable representations than direct cross-modal alignment strategies.

Abstract: Leveraging textual information for image clustering has emerged as a promising direction, largely owing to the powerful representations learned by Vision-Language Models (VLMs). Existing approaches typically retrieve a textual counterpart for each image and then refine multimodal representations by directly enforcing cross-modal agreement, e.g., maximizing image-text similarity inherited from pretrained VLMs. However, such a strategy aligns heterogeneous representations across modalities without explicitly modeling the intrinsic structure within each modality and thus might yield unreliable alignment or distort modality-specific structures that are crucial for clustering. In this paper, we propose a simple but principled approach, termed deep modality-shared self-expressive model (DeepMORSE), which discovers cross-modal structures via a modality-shared self-expressive model and simultaneously learns structured representations that conform to a union of modality-specific subspaces. Moreover, we theoretically justify that the modality-shared self-expressive coefficients suppress inter-class noise towards a subspace-preserving solution, and show that mini-batch optimization procedure introduces an implicit regularization onto the self-expressive model. We evaluate our DeepMORSE on six widely used image clustering benchmarks and observe performance improvements exceeding 3% on the UCF-101, DTD-47, and ImageNet-Dogs datasets. In addition, we demonstrate the strong transferability of the learned representations by achieving state-of-the-art performance on downstream tasks such as image retrieval and zero-shot classification---without requiring any task-specific losses or post-processing. The code is available at: https://github.com/mengxianghan123/DeepMORSE.

</details>


### [99] [FreCast: Refining Radar Echo Intensity via Phase-Preserving Amplitude Residual Diffusion for Precipitation Nowcasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08436)
*Heping Fang,Zihuai Yin,Kaicheng Mao,Peiguang Zhang,Peng Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FreCast is a two-stage framework for precipitation nowcasting: the first stage generates an initial radar echo forecast, and the second stage uses the spatial structure of that forecast to correct intensity biases at individual locations, achieving consistent improvements across three datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing deep learning nowcasting models capture overall spatial distribution of precipitation but produce substantial intensity biases at individual locations. Rather than regenerating entire sequences without spatial constraints, the paper proposes a targeted structure-guided refinement strategy to directly address echo intensity biases.

Method: FreCast employs a two-stage framework: Stage 1 generates an initial forecast of future radar echoes; Stage 2 uses the spatial structure of the initial forecast as a constraint to correct intensity biases at individual locations in the first-stage prediction.

Result: Experiments on three datasets show consistent improvements across forecast skill metrics. Qualitative results show FreCast better preserves rainband continuity and intense precipitation structures at longer lead times.

Conclusion: The structure-guided refinement approach effectively reduces location-specific intensity biases in precipitation nowcasting, improving both quantitative forecast skill and qualitative preservation of precipitation structures.

Abstract: Precipitation nowcasting predicts the spatiotemporal evolution of future radar echoes from historical radar echo sequences, thereby estimating the occurrence, development, and movement of precipitation over the near term. In recent years, deep learning has become an important approach to precipitation nowcasting. Although state-of-the-art models can generally capture the overall spatial distribution of future precipitation, their predictions still exhibit substantial biases in radar echo intensity at individual locations. This observation motivates a more targeted strategy for reducing forecast errors. Instead of regenerating an entire radar echo sequence without spatial constraints, the predicted precipitation structure can be used to guide the refinement of echo intensities at individual locations. This structure-guided refinement directly targets echo intensity biases. Accordingly, we propose FreCast, a two-stage framework for radar echo prediction. The first stage generates an initial forecast of future radar echoes. The second stage uses the spatial structure of the initial forecast as a constraint to further correct intensity biases at individual locations in the first-stage prediction. Experiments on three datasets demonstrate that FreCast achieves consistent improvements across forecast skill metrics. Qualitative results further show that FreCast better preserves rainband continuity and intense precipitation structures at longer lead times.

</details>


### [100] [InstructionCrafter: Generating Consistent and High-Fidelity Visual Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08460)
*Shun Okamoto,Satoshi Iizuka,Kazuhiro Fukui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: InstructionCrafter is a diffusion-based framework for generating step-by-step visual instructions from text, using spatial-freeze training and instruction-aware adapters to simultaneously optimize step faithfulness, cross-image consistency, and per-frame visual quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Generating step-by-step visual instruction sequences requires satisfying three properties simultaneously: step faithfulness, cross-image consistency, and per-frame quality. Existing text-to-image approaches fail because independent sampling breaks consistency, finetuning on low-quality video degrades per-frame quality, and frozen backbones lack multi-step understanding.

Method: Built on a pretrained video diffusion backbone with (1) spatial-freeze training that freezes spatial layers controlling per-frame detail while updating only temporal and text-conditioning pathways, reducing trainable parameters by ~50%, and (2) two lightweight adapters: a Consistent Adapter that aggregates textual cues across the instruction sequence and neighboring steps, and a Context-Aware Temporal Adapter that converts cross-attention outputs into biases for temporal self-attention to propagate inter-frame relations.

Result: State-of-the-art overall performance on step faithfulness, cross-image consistency, and per-frame visual quality across two benchmark datasets, with significant reduction in noise, blur, and spurious subtitles.

Conclusion: Separating temporal/instructional alignment optimization from per-frame quality through spatial-freeze training and lightweight adapters effectively preserves generative priors while learning instruction semantics and inter-step relations, providing a promising direction for visual instruction generation.

Abstract: Given textual task instructions, generating step-by-step visual instructions as an image sequence requires the simultaneous satisfaction of multiple properties, specifically step faithfulness, cross-image consistency, and per-frame visual quality. Existing text-to-image generation approaches rarely meet all three properties, owing to independent sampling that breaks consistency, finetuning on low-quality video that degrades per-frame quality, and frozen backbones that lack multi-step understanding. In this work, we propose InstructionCrafter, a diffusion-based framework with the key idea of separating the optimization of temporal and instructional alignment from per-frame visual quality via (1) spatial-freeze training and (2) instruction-aware adapters. Built on a pretrained video diffusion backbone, InstructionCrafter freezes the spatial layers that control per-frame detail and updates only temporal and text-conditioning pathways to learn instruction semantics and inter-step relations, which preserves the generative prior for per-frame quality and reduces trainable parameters by about 50 percent compared with full finetuning. We also introduce two lightweight adapters that enhance the model's understanding of instructional context. The Consistent Adapter aggregates textual cues from the entire instruction sequence and from neighboring steps to keep object identity and attributes consistent across frames, and the Context-Aware Temporal Adapter converts cross-attention outputs into biases for temporal self-attention, explicitly propagating inter-frame relations. Extensive experiments on two benchmark datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art overall performance on step faithfulness, cross-image consistency, and per-frame visual quality while significantly reducing noise, blur, and spurious subtitles. Our code and trained models will be publicly available.

</details>


### [101] [RayLift: Lifting Complementary Ray-Wise Evidence with 3D Geometry Priors for Semantic Scene Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08476)
*Meng Wang,Hongxia Yu,Wenzhe He,Xingdong Song,Huilong Pi,Jiapeng Zhang,Ruihui Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RayLift is a stereo-based 3D semantic scene completion framework that treats stereo geometry as a metric reference while adaptively incorporating complementary ray evidence to handle depth uncertainty, achieving competitive performance on SemanticKITTI and SSCBench-KITTI-360.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing stereo-based SSC methods treat depth estimates as deterministic geometric constraints, causing depth uncertainty and local correspondence errors to propagate directly into voxel representations, degrading 3D scene understanding.

Method: RayLift consists of three key components: (1) a Complementary Context Encoder that extracts geometry-aware priors from a frozen 3D vision foundation model, (2) a Depth Ray Evidence Lifter that jointly models geometric dissimilarity, depth confidence, and spatial uncertainty to adaptively sample and weight candidate surface locations along each camera ray, and (3) a Semantic-Aware Voxel Integrator that injects ray evidence into voxel features by modeling their spatial support.

Result: Extensive experiments on SemanticKITTI and SSCBench-KITTI-360 demonstrate that RayLift achieves competitive performance and consistently outperforms existing methods.

Conclusion: Using stereo geometry as a metric reference while adaptively incorporating complementary ray evidence can effectively recover reliable 3D structures and improve semantic scene completion performance over prior approaches.

Abstract: Camera-based 3D semantic scene completion (SSC) provides comprehensive scene understanding for autonomous driving and robotics. However, existing methods often treat stereo depth estimates as deterministic geometric constraints, causing depth uncertainty and local correspondence errors to propagate directly into voxel representations. To address this issue, we propose RayLift, a framework that uses stereo geometry as a metric reference while incorporating complementary ray evidence to recover reliable 3D structures adaptively. RayLift first employs a Complementary Context Encoder that extracts geometry-aware priors from a frozen 3D vision foundation model, thereby enriching the scene context. It then introduces a Depth Ray Evidence Lifter module that jointly models geometric dissimilarity, depth confidence, and spatial uncertainty to adaptively sample and weight candidate surface locations along each camera ray. Finally, a Semantic-Aware Voxel Integrator injects the resulting ray evidence into voxel features by explicitly modeling their spatial support. Extensive experiments on SemanticKITTI and SSCBench-KITTI-360 demonstrate that RayLift achieves competitive performance and consistently outperforms existing methods.

</details>


### [102] [RenderMatte: Exact-Alpha Rendering and Group-Relative Alignment for Image Matting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08487)
*Zecheng Ren,Yafei Hu,Jianing Zhao,Ruichen Cong,Qun Jin,Yiren Song*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RenderMatte is a trimap-guided image matting framework built on FLUX.1 Kontext via full-parameter fine-tuning, introducing an alpha-edge objective for boundary supervision and group-relative alpha alignment with matting-specific rewards, trained on a large-scale synthetic RGBA dataset (RenderMatte dataset) with strand-level alpha annotations, achieving SOTA performance across all benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise alpha estimation in open-world scenes remains challenging due to diverse foreground appearances and opacity patterns, causing semantic ambiguity and fine-grained opacity variation issues—especially in sparse boundary regions that are difficult to supervise.

Method: RenderMatte adapts FLUX.1 Kontext via full-parameter fine-tuning as a trimap-guided matting framework. It uses an alpha-edge objective that preserves latent flow-matching signals while strengthening pixel-space boundary supervision. It introduces group-relative alpha alignment for post-training, comparing multiple mattes sampled under the same trimap condition using matting-specific rewards (alpha accuracy, boundary fidelity, trimap compliance, compositional consistency). A large-scale synthetic dataset with 3D-rendered RGBA foregrounds and strand-level alpha annotations is constructed for training.

Result: State-of-the-art performance is achieved across all benchmarks, demonstrating a scalable path toward high-fidelity matting in open-world scenes.

Conclusion: RenderMatte demonstrates that leveraging image editing priors from diffusion models, combined with specialized boundary supervision and reward-based post-training on high-quality synthetic data, provides a scalable and effective solution for open-world image matting.

Abstract: Image matting is an essential enabling technology for modern visual content production, where foreground extraction determines the realism and editability of downstream creation workflows. However, precise alpha estimation in open-world scenes remains challenging because real foregrounds exhibit highly diverse appearances and opacity patterns. This makes existing methods struggle with semantic ambiguity and fine-grained opacity variation, especially in sparse boundary regions that are fragile and difficult to supervise. To address this gap, we present RenderMatte, a trimap-guided matting framework that adapts FLUX.1 Kontext through full-parameter fine-tuning, leveraging image editing priors for structure-preserving alpha prediction. During supervised adaptation, an alpha-edge objective preserves the latent flow-matching signal while strengthening pixel-space boundary supervision. We further introduce group-relative alpha alignment for post-training. It compares multiple mattes sampled under the same trimap condition using matting-specific rewards for alpha accuracy, boundary fidelity, trimap compliance, and compositional consistency. To overcome the lack of precise edge annotations, we construct the RenderMatte dataset, a large-scale synthetic dataset combining 3D-rendered RGBA foregrounds with diverse multi-source assets. It features exact strand-level alpha annotations and diverse background composites. Experiments show state-of-the-art performance across all benchmarks, demonstrating a scalable path toward high-fidelity matting in open-world scenes.

</details>


### [103] [Linguistically-Aligned and Visually-Grounded Preference Optimization for Clinically-Augmented Medical Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08494)
*Qiang Hu,Yuxuan Luo,Yingjie Guo,Hao Wang,Qimei Wang,Qiang Li,Zhiwei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DPO-Clin is a novel post-training framework for Medical Report Generation that enhances reliability by focusing Direct Preference Optimization on clinical findings and cross-modal alignment, using entity-level factual diagnosis, retrieval-augmented multi-modal preference optimization, and counterfactual data augmentation to reduce factual errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DPO-based MRG methods use naive preference construction that pairs model-generated reports with ground truth, which entangles critical clinical findings with irrelevant linguistic characteristics and lacks explicit vision-language alignment, limiting factual reliability.

Method: DPO-Clin introduces three components: (1) Entity-level Clinical Diagnostic (ECD) module for precise entity-level factual diagnosis that guides generation of linguistically-aligned preference pairs; (2) M^2DPO, a retrieval-augmented multi-modal DPO variant enforcing textual preference inversion triggered by visual context switches for fine-grained cross-modal alignment; (3) counterfactual modification on correct yet highly uncertain predicted entities to construct targeted preference data for latent risk mitigation.

Result: Experiments on MIMIC-CXR, IU X-Ray, and an in-house endoscopy dataset demonstrate significant improvements over SFT baselines on clinical-aware metrics, with superior performance over existing DPO-based MRG methods and robust generalizability across different baseline architectures and medical imaging modalities.

Conclusion: Focusing preference optimization on clinical findings and cross-modal alignment, rather than naive report-level pairing, substantially improves factual reliability in medical report generation across diverse datasets and modalities.

Abstract: Despite significant advances in Medical Report Generation (MRG), the reliability remains constrained by the prevalence of factual errors. While Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) has emerged as a promising post-training paradigm to enhance the performance of Supervised Fine-Tuned (SFT) MRG models, existing DPO-based MRG methods typically adopt a naive preference construction that directly pairs model-generated reports with ground truth reports. This strategy inadvertently entangles critical clinical findings with clinically irrelevant linguistic characteristics, and fundamentally lacks explicit vision-language alignment. To address these challenges, we propose DPO-Clin, a novel post-training framework that focuses preference optimization on clinical findings and cross-modal alignment. First, we introduce the Entity-level Clinical Diagnostic (ECD) module to perform a precise entity-level factual diagnosis. ECD guides the generation of linguistically-aligned report preference pairs, isolating clinical discrepancies from linguistic variations. Second, to achieve fine-grained cross-modal alignment, we develop M$^2$DPO, a retrieval-augmented multi-modal DPO variant that enforces textual preference inversion triggered by visual context switches. Third, we locate correct yet highly uncertain predicted entities and apply counterfactual modifications to construct targeted preference data for latent risk mitigation, thereby further enhancing the model reliability. Extensive experiments on two public chest X-ray datasets (MIMIC-CXR and IU X-Ray) and an in-house endoscopy dataset demonstrate that DPO-Clin significantly improves the SFT baselines on clinical-aware metrics. Furthermore, it achieves superior performance over existing DPO-based MRG methods, exhibiting robust generalizability across distinct baseline architectures and diverse medical imaging modalities.

</details>


### [104] [Towards Adaptive Super-Resolution and Quality Assessment via Test-Time Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08508)
*Ajeet Kumar Verma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This doctoral research addresses video super-resolution (VSR) robustness under unknown degradations from heterogeneous devices, codecs, and networks. The work proposes test-time adaptation (TTA) as a unified paradigm that improves robustness and perceptual quality without retraining or high-resolution supervision, comprising three components: a TTA-based no-reference video quality assessment framework, a transformer-based architecture for screen-content super-resolution, and a region-aware TTA strategy for selectively refining text and non-text regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video super-resolution methods fail to generalize under unknown degradations arising from heterogeneous devices, codecs, and network environments, limiting their practical deployment in real-world conditions.

Method: The work introduces a TTA-based paradigm with three contributions: (1) a TTA framework for no-reference VQA providing perceptual guidance for VSR under unseen distortions; (2) a transformer-based architecture for screen-content super-resolution preserving text clarity and structural fidelity; (3) a region-aware TTA strategy that selectively refines text and non-text regions without requiring HR ground truth.

Result: Experimental results across diverse benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements in perceptual quality and readability compared to existing approaches.

Conclusion: The findings support TTA as an effective unified paradigm for robust and perceptually-aware VSR in real-world conditions, with ongoing work toward fully adaptive video enhancement systems that generalize across unseen domains.

Abstract: This paper presents doctoral research on adaptive video super-resolution and perceptual quality modeling under real-world conditions. Existing video super-resolution (VSR) methods struggle to generalize under unknown degradations arising from heterogeneous devices, codecs, and network environments. We address this challenge through test-time adaptation (TTA), a unified paradigm that improves robustness and perceptual quality without retraining or high-quality supervision. Specifically, we: 1) propose a TTA-based framework for no-reference video quality assessment (VQA), where adapted quality predictions provide perceptual guidance for VSR under unseen distortions; 2) develop a transformer-based architecture for screen-content super-resolution that preserves text clarity and structural fidelity; and 3) introduce a region-aware TTA strategy that selectively refines text and non-text regions without requiring high-resolution ground truth. Experimental results across diverse benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements in perceptual quality and readability. We also outline ongoing work toward fully adaptive video enhancement systems capable of generalizing across unseen domains.

</details>


### [105] [eBIRD: Event-based Intensity Image Reconstruction Using Controllable Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08519)
*Ignacio Bugueno-Cordova,Fabian Valderrama,Rodrigo Verschae*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: eBIRD is an event-guided intensity-image reconstruction framework combining a Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Model (DDPM) with ControlNet-based conditioning. Tested on N-MNIST (digits) and RGBE-Gaze (faces) datasets with 33ms event windows, it finds that a generic diffusion model performs best on N-MNIST (MSE 0.0052, SSIM 0.8982, PSNR 23.34dB), while a specialized model excels on RGBE-Gaze (MSE 0.0161, SSIM 0.7605, PSNR 19.08dB), demonstrating that the optimal learning strategy depends on the reconstruction domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reconstructing intensity images from event streams is challenging due to the binary, sparse, and asynchronous nature of event data, motivating the need for a robust framework that can effectively guide the reconstruction process.

Method: The proposed eBIRD framework combines a DDPM with ControlNet-based conditioning for event-guided reconstruction. The paper analyzes two learning strategies: a generic diffusion model and a specialized diffusion model, evaluated on handwritten digit (N-MNIST) and face (RGBE-Gaze) reconstruction tasks using 33ms event windows.

Result: On N-MNIST, the generic model achieves the best reconstruction quality (MSE 0.0052, SSIM 0.8982, PSNR 23.34dB), while on RGBE-Gaze, the specialized model performs best (MSE 0.0161, SSIM 0.7605, PSNR 19.08dB).

Conclusion: Controllable diffusion models are a promising approach for event-guided intensity-image reconstruction, but the preferred learning strategy (generic vs. specialized) depends on the reconstruction domain.

Abstract: Intensity-image reconstruction from event streams remains a challenging problem due to the binary, sparse, and asynchronous nature of event data. This work proposes eBIRD, an event-guided reconstruction framework that combines a DDPM with ControlNet-based conditioning. We analyze generic and specialized diffusion learning strategies for handwritten digit (N-MNIST) and face (RGBE-Gaze) reconstruction using 33ms event windows. On N-MNIST, the general model achieves the best reconstruction quality (MSE 0.0052, SSIM 0.8982, PSNR 23.34dB), whereas the specialized model performs best on RGBE-Gaze (MSE 0.0161, SSIM 0.7605, PSNR 19.08dB). These preliminary results suggest that controllable diffusion models are a promising approach for event-guided intensity-image reconstruction, while highlighting that the preferred learning strategy depends on the reconstruction domain.

</details>


### [106] [A Combined Feature-Based Framework for Disguise and Spoofing Detection in Face Recognition Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08521)
*Sangiya Pararajasingham*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses two complementary failure modes in face recognition—spoofing (impostor presentation attacks) and disguise (legitimate user rejection due to appearance variation)—by proposing and comparing five classical feature-extraction and classification pipelines that handle both problems in a single unified framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Face recognition systems typically address spoofing and disguise as separate, disjoint problems, yet both are critical security vulnerabilities. There is a need for a unified framework that can handle both failure modes simultaneously, which existing classical approaches do not adequately provide.

Method: Five pipelines are proposed and compared: PM (PCA + Minimum Euclidean Distance MED), LPM (LBP + PCA + MED), HPM (HOG + PCA + MED), SM (SURF + MED), and HM (Harris corners + MED). All follow a common two-phase pipeline of pre-processing, feature extraction, feature filtering, and classification. Models are trained on 115 subjects from the FEI, Disguised Faces Database, and NUAA databases and evaluated across six test conditions.

Result: The HOG-based HPM pipeline achieved the most consistent performance, with 94.59% accuracy on mixed-appearance disguise, 81.5-93.2% across pose and illumination variants, and 91.67% on spoofing. The LBP-based LPM pipeline achieved the highest spoofing-detection accuracy (93.2%) but showed weaker robustness to pose changes.

Conclusion: The results reveal a measurable trade-off between spoof sensitivity and disguise robustness among classical feature representations, motivating the exploration of deep-learning and cross-database extensions discussed in the concluding sections.

Abstract: Face recognition systems face two distinct, commonly-separated failure modes: spoofing, where an impostor presents a photograph or video of an authorized user, and disguise, where a legitimate user is rejected because their appearance differs from their enrolled template due to accessories, facial hair, illumination, or pose. This paper proposes and compares five combined feature-extraction and classification pipelines that address both problems within a single framework: PM (PCA and Minimum Euclidean Distance, MED), LPM (Local Binary Patterns with PCA and MED), HPM (Histogram of Oriented Gradients with PCA and MED), SM (Speeded-Up Robust Features with MED), and HM (Harris corner features with MED). Each pipeline follows a common two-phase process comprising pre-processing, feature extraction, feature filtering, and classification. The methods were trained on 115 subjects drawn from the FEI, Disguised Faces Database, and NUAA databases and evaluated on six test conditions covering mixed appearances, frontal faces, dark illumination, left- and right-turned poses, and photo-spoofing attempts. The HOG-based pipeline (HPM) achieved the most consistent performance across conditions, with 94.59% accuracy on mixed-appearance disguise, 81.5-93.2% across pose and illumination variants, and 91.67% on spoofing, while the LBP-based pipeline (LPM) achieved the highest spoofing-detection accuracy (93.2%) but weaker robustness to pose change. These results reveal a measurable trade-off between spoof sensitivity and disguise robustness among classical feature representations, motivating the deep-learning and cross-database extensions discussed in the concluding sections.

</details>


### [107] [ERF-GS: Reconstructing Fast Motion from Disjoint Event-RGB Viewpoints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08531)
*Xiaoyang Bai,Zhenyang Li,Weiwei Xu,Edmund Y. Lam,Yifan Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ERF-GS is a framework that integrates event camera data into both the optimization and densification stages of 3D Gaussian splatting to reconstruct fast-moving dynamic scenes with high accuracy, outperforming existing baselines on realistic datasets with blur and disjoint viewpoints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dynamic 3D scene reconstruction of fast-moving objects remains challenging because conventional frame-based video methods struggle with scenarios like sports events and animal videography, which involve high motion speeds, low frame rates, and motion blur.

Method: ERF-GS fuses event information into both the optimization and densification stages of the Gaussian splatting pipeline, leveraging high-frame-rate event sensors. It uses realistic simulation settings and makes event-based learning detached from RGB inputs, enabling applicability to natural video with complex layouts.

Result: ERF-GS outperforms the 4DGS baseline and concurrent E-D3DGS method on multiple variants of Neu3D and Nvidia datasets, including blurry RGB frames and disjoint RGB-event viewpoints.

Conclusion: By integrating event information into the full Gaussian splatting pipeline and detaching event-based learning from RGB inputs, ERF-GS demonstrates superior performance in reconstructing fast-moving dynamic scenes in realistic, challenging settings, with code made publicly available.

Abstract: Deep learning-driven representations such as neural radiance fields (NeRFs) and 3D Gaussian splatting (3DGS) have revolutionized the field of dynamic 3D scene reconstruction with improved visual precision and scalability. However, the reconstruction of fast-moving objects remains a challenge; existing methods based on conventional frame-based videos often struggle in scenarios such as sports events and animal videography. We propose an event-RGB fusion Gaussian splatting (ERF-GS) framework that integrates event information into both optimization and densification stages of the Gaussian splatting pipeline, taking advantage of novel event sensors with high frame-rate. Unlike many other event-assisted scene reconstruction methods, ERF-GS was developed using realistic simulation settings and realizes event-based learning detached from RGB inputs. This design enables its application beyond straightforward synthetic data into the realm of natural video with complex layout, low frame rates and severe motion blur. Our experiments show that ERF-GS outperforms both the 4DGS baseline and the concurrent E-D3DGS on different variants of the Neu3D and Nvidia datasets which include blurry RGB frames and disjoint RGB-event viewpoints. Our code is available at https://github.com/andrewbxy/ERF-GS.

</details>


### [108] [Rethinking Attention Locality in Spiking Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08541)
*Zeqi Zheng,Zizheng Zhu,Yuping Yan,Wenxuan Pan,Zhaofei Yu,Yaochu Jin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper identifies that existing locality-enhanced Spiking Self-Attention (SSA) methods do not consistently induce true spatial locality across layers and architectures, and proposes SCLA-BCP (Spatially Contiguous Local Attention with Boundary Continuity Pathway) along with a hierarchical locality deployment strategy for Spiking Transformers, achieving consistent improvements on seven datasets including COCO and ADE20K.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spiking Transformers with Softmax-free Spiking Self-Attention (SSA) lack spatially localized token interactions. Existing locality-enhanced SSA methods improve accuracy, but it is unclear whether they consistently induce spatial locality; MAD analysis reveals that computational locality does not translate into spatial locality, and uniform enhancement overlooks architecture-dependent requirements.

Method: Two key components: (1) SCLA computes attention within non-overlapping regions of spatially adjacent tokens, and BCP enables cross-boundary information exchange via a lightweight convolutional pathway. (2) A hierarchical locality deployment strategy is developed to apply SCLA-BCP appropriately across the two major Spiking Transformer architectures.

Result: Extensive experiments on seven static and neuromorphic datasets (classification, detection, segmentation) show consistent improvements with limited parameter and energy overhead. Notably, mAP@50 improves by up to 9.50% on COCO 2017 and mIoU by up to 3.42% on ADE20K.

Conclusion: The approach is validated by visualizations, MAD analysis, and ablation studies, confirming that SCLA-BCP with hierarchical deployment effectively induces spatial locality and improves performance across Spiking Transformer architectures.

Abstract: Spiking Transformers provide a promising paradigm for efficient visual processing with spike-driven computation, yet their Softmax-free Spiking Self-Attention (SSA) struggles to establish spatially localized token interactions. Although existing locality-enhanced SSA methods improve accuracy, it remains unclear whether they consistently induce spatial locality across layers and different Spiking Transformer architectures. Through Mean Attention Distance (MAD) analysis, we reveal that computational locality does not necessarily translate into spatial locality and show that uniformly applying the same locality enhancement overlooks architecture-dependent deployment requirements. Motivated by these observations, we propose Spatially Contiguous Local Attention with Boundary Continuity Pathway (SCLA-BCP). SCLA computes attention within non-overlapping regions of spatially adjacent tokens, while BCP facilitates cross-boundary information exchange through a lightweight convolutional pathway. Furthermore, we develop a hierarchical locality deployment strategy to effectively apply SCLA-BCP across the two major Spiking Transformer architectures. Extensive experiments on seven static and neuromorphic datasets covering classification, detection, and segmentation demonstrate consistent improvements with limited parameter and energy overhead. Notably, our approach improves mAP@50 by up to 9.50% on COCO 2017 and mIoU by up to 3.42% on ADE20K. Visualizations, MAD analysis, and ablation studies further validate its effectiveness.

</details>


### [109] [MotionCraft: Latent World Modeling with Sparse Attention for Visual Upscaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08553)
*Rong Fu,Chunlei Meng,Yangchen Zeng,Xiaowen Ma,Yongtai Liu,Wangyu Wu,Shuo Yin,Zijian Zhang,Sicheng Li,Yingrui Ji,Chenhao Wang,Simon Fong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MotionCraft is a controllable video super-resolution framework that formulates restoration as motion-aware latent state prediction, combining robust motion fusion, a Latent World Transformer with adaptive sparse attention, and a compact conditional decoder to achieve temporally consistent, high-quality reconstructions under streaming constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VSR approaches trade off among local-detail fidelity, long-range spatio-temporal modeling, perceptual realism, and efficiency. Convolutional alignment struggles with large motion or complex degradations, transformer-based methods are computationally heavy, and latent/diffusion-based generators need specialized temporal constraints. A framework that balances all these aspects while offering user control is needed.

Method: MotionCraft formulates VSR as motion-aware latent state prediction inspired by world models. It integrates robust motion fusion, a Latent World Transformer with adaptive sparse attention balancing locality and targeted non-local interactions, and a compact conditional decoder, plus an explicit user-accessible control interface.

Result: Empirical evaluations show MotionCraft achieves strong reconstruction and perceptual performance while enabling predictable trade-offs between temporal smoothness and reconstruction fidelity.

Conclusion: MotionCraft demonstrates that a world-model-inspired latent state prediction approach with adaptive sparse attention and user control can deliver high-quality, temporally consistent video super-resolution under streaming constraints, with controllable trade-offs between smoothness and fidelity.

Abstract: Video super-resolution (VSR) aims to recover high-fidelity high-resolution videos from low-resolution inputs and is central to applications ranging from mobile capture to streaming and archival restoration. Existing approaches trade off among local-detail fidelity, long-range spatio-temporal modeling, perceptual realism, and efficiency: convolutional alignment techniques preserve local structure but suffer when motion is large or degradations are complex; transformer-based methods capture long-range dependencies yet require architectural or algorithmic adaptations to remain computationally feasible; and recent latent or diffusion-based generators synthesize rich texture but require specialized temporal constraints to maintain coherence. We present MotionCraft, a controllable VSR framework that formulates restoration as motion-aware latent state prediction inspired by world models and integrates adaptive sparse attention with an explicit user-accessible control interface. MotionCraft combines robust motion fusion, a Latent World Transformer that balances locality and targeted non-local interactions, and a compact conditional decoder to deliver temporally consistent, high-quality reconstructions under streaming constraints. Empirical evaluations show that MotionCraft achieves strong reconstruction and perceptual performance while enabling predictable trade-offs between temporal smoothness and reconstruction fidelity.

</details>


### [110] [SC-Diff: Semantically Calibrated Diffusion for Visible-to-Infrared Image Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08555)
*Junyin Zhang,Siyu Huang,Jianxiong Ye,Haowei Gong,Ruicheng Zhang,Deyu Meng,Chenqiang Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SC-Diff is a semantically calibrated latent diffusion framework for visible-to-infrared image translation that uses semantic priors for both external conditional guidance and internal self-attention calibration. It leverages SAM3 to extract category-specific masks, converts them into token-level semantic labels, and applies Semantic-Guided Self-Attention Calibration (SGSC) to reduce cross-category interference while retaining global context, improving both perceptual quality and downstream detection performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing diffusion-based visible-to-infrared translation methods use semantic priors only as external conditions without regulating token interactions inside the denoising network, failing to preserve object locations, shapes, and semantic layouts needed for reliable annotation reuse.

Method: SC-Diff uses a pretrained SAM3 model with predefined text prompts to extract category-specific semantic masks from visible images. These masks are merged into a semantic map and fused with the visible image as input condition. The same map is converted into token-level semantic labels that calibrate self-attention via Semantic-Guided Self-Attention Calibration (SGSC), which applies adaptive positive biases to query-key pairs of the same category based on attention dispersion and per-category attention strength, with the original attention scores modulating the bias magnitude.

Result: Extensive experiments show that SC-Diff improves perceptual quality of generated infrared images and produces more effective synthetic training data for downstream infrared object detection.

Conclusion: Soft semantic calibration of self-attention within the denoising network effectively reduces cross-category interference while retaining global contextual interactions, thereby improving semantic consistency and utility of generated infrared images for downstream tasks.

Abstract: Visible-to-infrared image translation provides a practical way to expand infrared training data using abundant visible images. Diffusion models are promising for this task because of their strong generative performance. However, existing diffusion-based methods typically use semantic priors only as external conditions, without explicitly regulating token interactions within the denoising network. Consequently, they struggle to preserve object locations, shapes, and semantic layouts required for reliable annotation reuse. We propose SC-Diff, a semantically calibrated latent diffusion framework that uses semantic priors for both conditional guidance and internal self-attention calibration. A pretrained SAM3 model with predefined text prompts first extracts category-specific semantic masks from visible images. These masks are merged into a semantic map and fused with the visible image as the input condition. The same map is converted into token-level semantic labels to calibrate self-attention in the denoising network. Based on these labels, we introduce Semantic-Guided Self-Attention Calibration (SGSC), which adaptively applies positive biases to query-key pairs of the same category. The query-wise calibration strength depends on the dispersion of attention across semantic categories and the attention assigned to the query's own category. The original attention scores further modulate the bias, giving greater calibration to same-category keys with stronger responses. This soft calibration reduces cross-category interference while retaining global contextual interactions, thereby improving semantic consistency in generated infrared images. Extensive experiments show that SC-Diff improves perceptual quality and produces more effective synthetic training data for downstream infrared object detection.

</details>


### [111] [On-Device Multi-Species Malaria Detection with Uncertainty-Calibrated Slide-Level Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08566)
*Idaya Seidu,Ahmed Tahiru Issah,Charles B. Delahunt,Carine Mukamakuza*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a clinically-driven malaria diagnosis pipeline deployed on an edge device that addresses six non-obvious clinical constraints often ignored in ML malaria research, including stopping criteria, human-in-the-loop review, multi-species discrimination, thick film detection, uncertainty estimation, and offline edge deployment. Using YOLOv13n via TensorFlow Lite, the system detects four parasite species and white blood cells from Giemsa-stained thick blood smears, achieving strong detection (mAP@0.5 = 0.863) and slide-level parasitemia correlation (r = 0.951) entirely on-device.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Malaria remains a leading cause of mortality in resource-limited settings with scarce expert microscopists. While ML-based diagnosis has strong potential, existing ML malaria literature typically ignores critical real-world clinical constraints required for actual deployment. In consultation with a national health center, the authors identified six key clinical requirements and developed a system addressing them.

Method: The pipeline uses YOLOv13n deployed via TensorFlow Lite on an edge device for fully offline inference. It detects four Plasmodium species and white blood cells from Giemsa-stained thick blood smear images. The system incorporates: (i) stopping criteria to reduce acquisition time, (ii) human-in-the-loop review functionality, (iii) multi-species discrimination, (iv) thick film detection, (v) computationally-efficient uncertainty calculations, and (vi) on-device edge deployment. Per-image detections are aggregated into slide-level parasitemia using WHO-standard quantification.

Result: Evaluated on 2,739 annotated images across all four species, the system achieves mAP@0.5 of 0.863, per-image parasite count correlation of r = 0.812, and slide-level correlation of r = 0.951 (with soft counting over 10 images/slide). The pipeline runs entirely offline with a processing time of 10.27 ± 1.65 seconds per image.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates that clinically-informed ML systems can meet real-world constraints for malaria diagnosis in resource-limited settings, bridging the gap between academic ML research and practical clinical deployment requirements.

Abstract: Malaria remains a leading cause of mortality in resource-limited settings, where expert microscopists are scarce. Automated diagnosis based on microscopy images thus has strong potential to improve care delivery. But for an algorithm to deploy, a necessary requirement is that it meet a suite of non-obvious (from a machine learning (ML) perspective) clinical constraints. Therefore, in close consultation with a national health center we developed a malaria diagnosis pipeline which addresses key requirements listed by the health care center but typically ignored in the ML malaria literature. In particular, it includes: (i) stopping criteria (to reduce image acquisition and time-to-result); (ii) human-in-the-loop functionality (for review and accountability); (iii) multi-species discrimination (since treatment varies by species); (iv) thick film detection (standard for microscopy); (v) computationally-efficient uncertainty calculations (to aid clinician review); and (vi) an edge device platform (since internet can be spotty in this catchment area). The mobile system performs all inference on-device using YOLOv13n deployed via TensorFlow Lite. It detects four species and white blood cells from Giemsa-stained thick blood smear images, aggregating per-image detections into slide-level parasitemia with World Health Organization (WHO)-standard quantification. This paper highlights these various clinical constraints and offers methods to address them. Evaluated on 2,739 annotated images across all four species, the system achieves mAP@0.5 of 0.863, per-image parasite count correlation of r = 0.812, slide-level r = 0.951 (soft counting, 10 images/slide), and runs entirely offline with a pipeline time of 10.27 +- 1.65 s per image.

</details>


### [112] [CDGC-Net: 3D Medical Image Segmentation with Cooperative Dual-Scale Self-Attention and Grouped Channel Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08575)
*Zheyang Jing,Qin Lu,Jianwang Li,Yujie Yang,Chen Yi,Shaofeng Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CDGC-Net is a 3D medical image segmentation network that combines cooperative dual-scale spatial self-attention (CDSA) with grouped hierarchical channel attention (GHCA) within a unified block, achieving state-of-the-art performance across four benchmark datasets while reducing parameters and FLOPs by ~40% relative to UNETR++.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D segmentation methods model global and local features in separate modules, leading to semantic mismatch between global context and local boundaries, insufficient channel relationship modeling, weak spatial-channel interaction, and redundant representations.

Method: CDGC-Net introduces CDGC blocks containing: (1) Cooperative Dual-Scale Self-Attention (CDSA) with parallel local-window and global-sparse attention heads capturing fine details and long-range context at the same feature level; (2) Grouped Hierarchical Channel Attention (GHCA) organizing channels into r groups to model within-group and cross-group dependencies; (3) shared key projection between CDSA and GHCA for consistent feature reference; and (4) residual feature alignment integrating refined features with the original representation.

Result: On Synapse, ACDC, BraTS, and LA datasets, CDGC-Net achieved mean DSC of 86.96%, 92.91%, 82.56%, and 93.52%, surpassing next-best results by 0.39, 0.47, 0.17, and 0.32 percentage points. The model has 25.83M parameters and 28.62G FLOPs (for 64x128x128 input), reducing these by 39.87% and 40.30% compared to UNETR++.

Conclusion: CDGC-Net demonstrates that jointly modeling dual-scale spatial attention and grouped hierarchical channel attention within the same feature level achieves a favorable trade-off between segmentation accuracy and computational complexity, outperforming existing methods across multiple 3D medical segmentation benchmarks.

Abstract: Accurate 3D medical image segmentation requires the integration of long-range anatomical context with fine boundary detail. Existing methods often model global and local features in separate modules or feature levels and perform channel recalibration independently. This may cause semantic mismatch between global context and local boundaries, insufficient channel relationship modeling, weak spatial-channel interaction, and redundant representations. We propose CDGC-Net, a 3D medical image segmentation network that combines cooperative dual-scale spatial attention with grouped hierarchical channel modeling. With-in each CDGC block, Cooperative Dual-Scale Self-Attention (CDSA) assigns attention heads to parallel local-window and global-sparse branches. The two branches capture fine spatial details and long-range anatomical context at the same feature level. Their outputs are concatenated into an $N\times C$ spatial representation and directly passed to Grouped Hierarchical Channel Attention (GHCA). GHCA organizes the channels into $r$ groups and models both within-group and cross-group dependencies. CDSA and GHCA reuse a shared key projection to maintain a consistent feature reference. Residual feature alignment subsequently integrates the refined features with the original representation. On the Synapse, ACDC, BraTS, and LA datasets, CDGC-Net achieved mean DSC values of 86.96\%, 92.91\%, 82.56\%, and 93.52\%, respectively, exceeding the next-highest reported values by 0.39, 0.47, 0.17, and 0.32 percentage points. CDGC-Net contains 25.83M parameters and 28.62G FLOPs for an input size of $64\times128\times128$, reducing these quantities by 39.87\% and 40.30\%, respectively, relative to UNETR++. These results indicate a favorable trade-off between segmentation accuracy and computational complexity.

</details>


### [113] [Where Is the Bee? Detecting Tiny Pollinators with a Single Collaborative-Head Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08580)
*Junsu Kim,Seungryul Baek*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper describes the winning solution for the CVPPA@ECCV 2026 BuzzSpot Challenge, which detects bees, bumblebees, hoverflies, and moths in field keyframes. The authors select Co-DINO with a Swin-L backbone based on a comparison of 10 detector configurations, and address the severe class imbalance (bees = 80% of labels) via a crop-mosaic training pool that rebalances rare classes and a class-weighted simplex ETF loss. Without ensembling or test-time augmentation, the system achieves 0.5062 mAP@[.5:.95], ranking first on FinalTest.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge exhibits two annotation difficulties: extremely small objects (median box occupies only 0.16% of a 1920x1080 frame) and severe class imbalance (bees dominate 80% of labels). These require systematic detector selection and specialized training strategies to handle small boxes and rare classes effectively.

Method: (1) Compare 10 recorded detector configurations on held-out keyframes, selecting plain Co-DINO with a Swin-L backbone based on highest mAP. (2) Fine-tune on a crop-mosaic pool that raises the combined annotation share of the 3 rare classes from 19.9% to 55.1%. (3) Apply a class-weighted simplex equiangular tight frame (ETF) loss that pulls projected states of matched decoder queries toward fixed class directions. The training schedule spans 12+3+2 epochs.

Result: The system ranks first on the FinalTest set with 0.5062 mAP@[.5:.95], achieved without inference-time ensembling or test-time augmentation.

Conclusion: A careful detector selection combined with data rebalancing (crop-mosaic) and a class-weighted ETF loss effectively addresses both small-object detection and class imbalance in the BuzzSpot insect detection challenge, achieving state-of-the-art results.

Abstract: The CVPPA@ECCV 2026 BuzzSpot Challenge asks us to detect bees, bumblebees, hoverflies, and moths in 1920x1080 field keyframes. Its annotations carry 2 difficulties: the median box occupies 0.16% of a frame, and bees account for 80% of the labels. To cope with the small boxes, we compare 10 recorded detector configurations on held-out keyframes; plain Co-DINO with a Swin-L backbone has the highest mAP in this comparison, so we select it. Training then addresses the bee dominance in 2 ways: fine-tuning on a crop-mosaic pool in which the combined annotation share of the 3 rare classes rises from 19.9% to 55.1%, and a class-weighted simplex equiangular tight frame (ETF) loss that pulls the projected states of matched decoder queries toward fixed class directions. The full schedule spans 12+3+2 epochs. Without inference-time ensembling or test-time augmentation, we rank first on FinalTest at 0.5062 mAP@[.5:.95].

</details>


### [114] [EvTrajGS: Accurate and Efficient 3D Gaussian Splatting from Unposed Event Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08585)
*Zixuan Chen,Jiakai Zhang,Junhao Dong,Guangcong Wang,Jianhuang Lai,Yew-Soon Ong,Xiaohua Xie*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EvTrajGS is a 3D Gaussian Splatting framework for unposed event streams that enables accurate and efficient joint pose-scene optimization by parameterizing camera motion as a continuous-time trajectory initialized from coarse pose priors, achieving 3.8 dB higher PSNR and over 40% lower ATE RMSE than state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional reconstruction methods using off-the-shelf pose estimates are efficient but produce low-fidelity results due to cumulative errors from inaccurate pose initialization, while SLAM-style methods achieve higher fidelity but with considerable computational overhead. There is a need to address this trade-off.

Method: EvTrajGS parameterizes camera motion as a continuous-time trajectory initialized from discrete camera poses for unified pose refinement. It aggregates adjacent trajectory states into a temporally coupled pose to promote temporally consistent pose updates during joint optimization, and introduces a loss-reweighted event sampling strategy to adaptively emphasize temporally under-reconstructed intervals.

Result: Extensive experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show EvTrajGS outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving 3.8 dB higher PSNR, 0.1 higher SSIM, and over 40% lower ATE RMSE while retaining high computational efficiency.

Conclusion: EvTrajGS successfully resolves the trade-off between reconstruction fidelity and computational efficiency for event-based dense 3D reconstruction by enabling reliable joint pose-scene optimization from coarse pose priors without computationally expensive SLAM-style pipelines.

Abstract: Event cameras, with high temporal resolution, high dynamic range, and asynchronous sensing characteristics, have shown great potential for dense 3D reconstruction. Traditional reconstruction methods based on off-the-shelf pose estimates achieve high efficiency but produce low-fidelity results, as inaccurate pose initialization introduces cumulative reconstruction errors. In contrast, recent SLAM-style methods stabilize joint pose-scene optimization through incremental tracking and mapping, yielding higher reconstruction fidelity at the expense of considerable computational overhead. To address this trade-off, this paper presents EvTrajGS, an accurate and efficient 3D Gaussian Splatting framework for unposed event streams.
