To facilitate fine-grained text-driven facial modeling, we first construct FaME-G2E, a large-scale multimodal dataset containing detailed text--mesh annotations and paired text--blendshape samples for unified 3D facial generation and editing. Based on this dataset, we propose RAGMesh, a retrieval-augmented framework that leverages text-correlated geometric priors to improve high-fidelity facial synthesis and editing. Specifically, the Multi-Scale Retrieval Fusion (MSRF) module retrieves semantically consistent global and regional facial priors and fuses them in the blendshape space, suppressing conflicting local deformations while preserving coherent deformation patterns. Furthermore, we introduce Adaptive RAG-guided Supervision (AdaRAGS), a region-aware constraint that explicitly aligns textual semantics with corresponding facial regions, enhancing regional controllability and editing accuracy. Extensive experiments on FaME-G2E demonstrate that RAGMesh achieves superior performance over state-of-the-art methods in local geometric accuracy, text-guided controllability, regional editing precision, and inference efficiency. Video demo is available at https://youtu.be/Yr0_XkpWcNk, and the source code and dataset will be released upon paper acceptance.

</details>


### [183] [NBA_Streaming: A Large-Scale Benchmark for Fine-Grained Basketball Commentary Generation in Continuous Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09200)
*Lifang Wu,Yuyang Wu,Yangdong Gao,Fengyu Liu,Ya Jing,Liang Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces NBA_Streaming, a large-scale benchmark with 307 hours of basketball broadcasts and ~35K temporally aligned events annotated with event boundaries, player identities, fine-grained actions, event chains, and natural-language commentary, enabling unified evaluation of online commentary generation. A causal two-stage framework combining completion-first localization with ball-centric semantic grounding is proposed and shown to outperform existing baselines, though a significant performance gap remains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing basketball commentary generation methods are designed for pre-segmented clips or complete videos and are unsuitable for continuous live streams. Existing datasets also lack supervision for player identities, fine-grained actions, event attributes, and coherent event chains, limiting the factual richness of generated commentary.

Method: The paper proposes NBA_Streaming, a large-scale benchmark for online fine-grained basketball commentary generation, along with a causal two-stage framework that combines completion-first localization (detecting when events are complete from observed streams) with ball-centric semantic grounding (organizing scene, event, identity, and action cues) for commentary generation under causal constraints.

Result: The benchmark reveals that existing baselines struggle with online timing, factual grounding, and fine-grained description. The proposed framework consistently outperforms strong alternatives, though a notable performance gap remains, indicating the benchmark's value as a challenging testbed.

Conclusion: NBA_Streaming serves as a valuable benchmark for streaming sports video understanding and generation, advancing research toward online, causally-constrained, factually grounded basketball commentary generation.

Abstract: Live basketball commentary generation requires determining when an event is sufficiently observable and describing it before subsequent events unfold. However, existing methods are primarily designed for pre-segmented clips or complete videos, making them unsuitable for continuous streams. Existing datasets also provide limited supervision for player identities, fine-grained actions, event attributes, and coherent event chains, restricting the factual richness of generated commentary. To address these limitations, we introduce NBA_Streaming, a large-scale benchmark for online fine-grained basketball commentary generation. It contains 307 hours of basketball broadcasts and approximately 35K temporally aligned events, with annotations of event boundaries, player identities, fine-grained actions, event chains, and natural-language commentary. By moving from isolated clips to continuous streams, NBA_Streaming enables unified evaluation of event localization, response reliability, factual grounding, and commentary quality under causal constraints. We further propose a causal two-stage framework that combines completion-first localization with ball-centric semantic grounding, enabling the system to identify complete events from observed streams and organize scene, event, identity, and action cues for commentary generation. Extensive experiments reveal the difficulty of NBA_Streaming, where existing baselines struggle with online timing, factual grounding, and fine-grained description. Our framework consistently improves over strong alternatives, while the remaining gap highlights NBA_Streaming as a valuable benchmark for streaming sports video understanding and generation.

</details>


### [184] [PatchHead: Learning Spatial Patch Evidence for Generalizable AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09223)
*Shengbo Qi,Hongyi Fang,Benjia Zhou,Rui Mao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PatchHead is a lightweight spatial aggregation head that preserves the 2D organization of DINO patch tokens to improve cross-dataset generalization of AI-generated image detectors, achieving +3.0 average accuracy and +6.9 worst-case accuracy improvements over prior methods with minimal parameter/FLOP overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AI-generated image detectors fail to generalize across different generators or datasets. Although vision foundation models like DINO produce rich spatial representations, existing detectors only use the globally aggregated CLS token, which obscures spatially distributed generation traces.

Method: PatchHead is a lightweight spatial aggregation head that preserves the 2D organization of DINO patch tokens and aggregates evidence across neighboring regions. The pretrained DINO backbone is frozen, with only LoRA adapters, PatchHead, and an auxiliary projection head optimized during training.

Result: Across nine cross-dataset benchmarks, PatchHead ranks first on seven datasets and second on the remaining two. It improves average balanced accuracy from 91.6% to 94.6% (+3.0 points) and worst-case accuracy from 82.4% to 89.4% (+6.9 points), with only 8.6% more trainable parameters and 0.08% additional FLOPs.

Conclusion: Qualitative analysis shows PatchHead reduces class-conditional domain discrepancy and redirects representations from content-dominated saliency toward spatially distributed authenticity evidence, providing a representation-level explanation for why spatial patch aggregation transfers more reliably than CLS-based global representations.

Abstract: AI-generated image detectors generalize poorly when their training and test images originate from different generators or datasets. Despite the rich spatial representations produced by vision foundation models like DINO, existing detectors typically classify images using only the globally aggregated CLS token. We hypothesize that globally aggregating DINO features into a single CLS token obscures spatially distributed generation traces. To test this hypothesis, we introduce PatchHead, a lightweight spatial aggregation head that preserves the two-dimensional organization of DINO patch tokens and integrates evidence across neighboring regions. During training, we freeze the pretrained DINO backbone and optimize only the inserted LoRA adapters, PatchHead, and auxiliary projection head. Across nine cross-dataset benchmarks spanning manually curated and in-the-wild settings, PatchHead ranks first on seven datasets and second on the remaining two. It improves the strongest prior method from 91.6% to 94.6% in average balanced accuracy (+3.0 points) and raises the worst-case accuracy from 82.4% to 89.4% (+6.9 points), while introducing only 8.6% more trainable parameters and 0.08% additional FLOPs. Further qualitative analysis suggests that PatchHead (i) reduces class-conditional domain discrepancy, and (ii) redirects the representation from content-dominated saliency toward spatially distributed authenticity evidence. Together, these observations provide a representation-level account of why spatial patch aggregation transfers more reliably across generators and datasets than a single CLS-based global representation. Our code and models will be made available upon acceptance.

</details>


### [185] [RL-Native Distillation: Exploiting Scored Trajectories for Few-Step Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09226)
*Yuhan Li,Fangao Zeng,Sicong Kang,Mengfei Xu,Hao Zhou,Wei Li,Pipei Huang,Bingbing Ni*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: REST is a single-stage framework that jointly performs RL-based reward alignment and few-step distillation for text-to-image diffusion models, using an advantage-modulated distillation loss over the teacher's evolving rollout trajectories to achieve few-step CFG-free inference matching a 40-step RL teacher with under 25% additional training cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional text-to-image pipelines perform RL reward alignment and few-step distillation sequentially, which increases training cost and risks losing reward gains during the compression phase. The authors propose viewing diffusion RL natively as already producing reward-scored finite-step trajectories whose intermediate states can serve as distillation supervision, avoiding the need for separate distillation data or extra rollouts.

Method: REST attaches a decoupled student to an arbitrary RL teacher within a single-stage co-training framework. The student learns segment-wise from the teacher's evolving rollout trajectories while the teacher's optimization remains unchanged. An Advantage-Modulated Distillation (AMD) mechanism converts rollout advantages into signed weights over a base distillation loss, enhancing supervision from high-reward trajectories and mildly repelling the student from low-reward ones.

Result: Experiments on compositional generation, visual text rendering, and human-preference alignment show REST enables few-step CFG-free inference matching or surpassing its 40-step RL teacher, with overall additional training cost below 25% over pure RL. REST improves DrawBench PickScore over RTDMD by 0.82 while requiring only one-fifth of the training iterations.

Conclusion: REST demonstrates that RL and distillation can be unified in a single efficient stage, providing a lightweight, plug-and-play framework that requires no extra image rollouts, no separate distillation dataset, and no adversarial training, while preserving or improving reward alignment performance.

Abstract: Efficient text-to-image generation requires both reinforcement-learning (RL)-based reward alignment and few-step distillation, yet these procedures are typically performed sequentially, increasing training cost and risking the loss of reward gains during compression. We instead take an RL-native perspective: diffusion RL already generates reward-scored finite-step trajectories, whose intermediate states provide a natural source of distillation supervision rather than a disposable byproduct of sampling. Based on this insight, we propose REST (Reward-Enhanced Scored-Trajectory Distillation), a single-stage RL-distillation co-training framework that attaches a decoupled student to an arbitrary RL teacher. The student learns segment-wise from the teacher's evolving rollout trajectories while leaving the original teacher optimization unchanged. To prevent uniform imitation from preserving undesirable low-reward behaviors, we further introduce Advantage-Modulated Distillation (AMD), which transforms rollout advantages into signed weights over a base distillation loss. AMD strengthens supervision from preferred trajectories and mildly repels the student from low-reward ones. The resulting framework is lightweight and plug-and-play, requires no extra image rollouts, no separate distillation dataset, and no adversarial training. Experiments on compositional generation, visual text rendering, and human-preference alignment show that REST enables few-step CFG-free inference that matches or surpasses its 40-step RL teacher, with an overall additional training cost below 25% over pure RL. REST improves DrawBench PickScore over RTDMD by 0.82 while requiring only one-fifth of the training iterations.

</details>


### [186] [BAG: Budget-Aware Gating for Diffusion Caching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09231)
*Tong Zhao,Mingkun Lei,Yucheng Han,Chi Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BAG (Budget-Aware Gating) is a novel caching policy for Diffusion Transformers that unifies global budget pacing with dynamic, instance-adaptive feature reuse, using a lightweight gating network trained via offline-to-online schedule distillation to decide when to execute full computation versus reuse cached features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing diffusion caching paradigms face a fundamental trade-off: online heuristics lack global budget awareness, while static schedules lack instance adaptivity and cannot flexibly adapt to varying runtime budget constraints.

Method: BAG employs a lightweight gating network that dynamically decides whether to execute full computation or reuse cached features at each step by jointly conditioning on the budget state and local trajectory feedback. The policy is trained via offline-to-online schedule distillation, transferring decision-making from offline-searched schedules into a compact online gate.

Result: Extensive experiments on FLUX.1-dev and Wan2.1 demonstrate that BAG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art caching methods across various speedup tiers while remaining robust across different resolutions, seeds, and guidance scales.

Conclusion: BAG bridges the gap between global budget awareness and instance adaptivity in diffusion caching, providing a unified and robust solution that outperforms existing methods across multiple benchmarks.

Abstract: Diffusion caching is a lightweight strategy that accelerates Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) by reusing intermediate features across denoising steps, but existing paradigms face a fundamental trade-off: online heuristics lack global budget awareness, whereas static schedules lack instance adaptivity and fail to flexibly adapt to varying runtime budget constraints. To bridge this gap, we present BAG (Budget-Aware Gating), a novel caching policy that unifies global budget pacing with dynamic, instance-adaptive feature reuse. Rather than relying on hand-crafted rules, BAG employs a lightweight gating network that dynamically decides whether to execute a full computation or reuse cached features at each step by jointly conditioning on the budget state and local trajectory feedback. We train this policy via offline-to-online schedule distillation, transferring the decision-making of offline-searched schedules into a compact online gate. Extensive experiments on FLUX.1-dev and Wan2.1 demonstrate that BAG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art caching methods across various speedup tiers while remaining robust across different resolutions, seeds, and guidance scales. Code will be released.

</details>


### [187] [RealDenseFace: Real-time Monocular 3D Face Reconstruction from Dense UV-space Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09238)
*Linzhou Li,Tianjia Shao,Kun Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RealDenseFace is a real-time optimization-based monocular 3D face reconstruction method that fits a 3DMM to dense UV-space network predictions using a tailored Gauss-Newton solver converging in only a few iterations. It achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on NeRSemble SVFR, runs at 80+ FPS for online tracking, and is 20x faster than prior optimization-based baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing optimization-based monocular 3D face reconstruction methods achieve high fidelity by fitting 3DMMs to dense network priors, but the optimization stage is computationally expensive, taking tens of seconds per image—which limits practical use.

Method: The method has two stages: (1) a network predicts two dense UV-space maps from a single RGB image—a correspondence map for UV-to-image alignment and a relative-depth map for geometric constraints; (2) a tailored Gauss-Newton solver fits per-vertex targets sampled from these maps at vertex UV coordinates, formulated as a nonlinear least-squares problem. The solver supports single-image fitting, offline sequence reconstruction, and online tracking.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on the NeRSemble SVFR benchmark. The online tracker runs at 80+ FPS, and offline sequence reconstruction is over 20 times faster than previous optimization-based baselines.

Conclusion: RealDenseFace demonstrates that optimization-based 3D face reconstruction can be made real-time while maintaining high fidelity, closing the gap between accuracy and speed in this domain.

Abstract: Recent monocular 3D face reconstruction methods achieve high fidelity by fitting a 3D Morphable Model (3DMM) to dense priors predicted by networks, but the optimization stage is computationally expensive, often taking tens of seconds per image. We present RealDenseFace, a real-time optimization-based 3D face reconstruction method with dense UV-space network predictions. Our key idea is to formulate 3DMM fitting as a nonlinear least-squares problem and solve it with a tailored Gauss-Newton solver that converges in only a few iterations. The reconstruction is conducted in two stages. In the first stage, the network predicts two dense UV-space maps from a single RGB image: a correspondence map for UV-to-image alignment, and a relative-depth map for geometric constraints along the viewing direction. In the second stage, the solver fits per-vertex targets sampled from these maps at the vertex UV coordinates. The solver supports all three reconstruction settings: single-image fitting, offline sequence reconstruction, and online tracking. Our method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on the NeRSemble SVFR benchmark. The online tracker runs at 80+ FPS, and the offline sequence reconstruction is over 20 times faster than previous optimization-based baselines.

</details>


### [188] [In-Loop Model Adaptation with Coupled Latent-Noise Guidance for High-Fidelity Subject-Driven Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09244)
*Yushun Tang,Weiming Chen,Siyi Liu,Yi Zhang,Feng Wu,Zhihai He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes In-Loop Model Adaptation (IMA), a novel approach for subject-driven text-to-image generation that adapts the core diffusion model during the actual generation process using a DDIM inversion chain and a coupled latent-noise loss, eliminating the need for pre-training or fine-tuning on the reference image.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Subject-driven generation faces the challenge of efficiently adapting diffusion models to different visual contexts while maintaining subject identity when reference images change. Existing methods require either large domain-specific datasets for training or hundreds of fine-tuning iterations on the reference image before generation, both of which are computationally expensive and inefficient.

Method: IMA adapts the diffusion model at each generation step during image generation without pre-training. It establishes two chains: a DDIM inversion chain mapping the reference image to a sequence of latents, and a text-to-image generation chain generating from text only. A masked latent consistency loss and a noise regularization loss characterize the latent-noise difference between the model and these chains at each generation step, guiding in-loop adaptation to preserve subject identity while maintaining text alignment.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that IMA significantly improves subject-driven text-to-image generation performance, achieving high-fidelity generation while preserving subject identity without the computational overhead of pre-training or fine-tuning.

Conclusion: In-loop model adaptation during generation is an effective alternative to pre-training and fine-tuning approaches for subject-driven text-to-image generation, enabling efficient adaptation while maintaining both subject identity and text prompt alignment.

Abstract: Text-to-image diffusion models have achieved remarkable success in generating high-quality images from a given text prompt. Subject-driven generation aims to synthesize customized images to mimic the appearance of subjects in given reference images within different visual contexts specified by the text prompts. The central challenge here is that, when the reference image changes, the diffusion model cannot efficiently adapt to different visual contexts while consistently maintaining the subject identity. Existing methods either train the model with a large domain-specific dataset or fine-tune the model using the reference image for hundreds of iterations before actual image generation. In this work, we explore a new approach, called \textit{In-Loop Model Adaptation} (IMA), which adapts the core diffusion model at each generation step during the actual process of image generation, without being trained on the reference image before the generation process. To this end, we establish a DDIM inversion chain that maps the reference image to a sequence of latent, as well as a text-to-image generation chain which generates the image from the text prompt only. We then introduce a masked latent consistency loss and a noise regularization loss to characterize the latent-noise difference between the diffusion model and these two chains at each generation step. This coupled latent-noise loss is used to guide the in-loop model adaptation to preserve the subject identity specified by the reference image while maintaining accurate alignment with the text prompt, resulting in high-fidelity text-to-image generation. Our extensive experiments demonstrate that our proposed IMA method significantly improves the performance of subject-driven text-to-image generation.

</details>


### [189] [Task-Adaptive 3D Cross-Field MRI Translation via Field-Conditioned Content-Style Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09264)
*Haowen Pang,Yingqi Hao,Pengli Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 3D unpaired cross-field MRI translation framework for the MRIxFields 2026 challenge, which addresses magnetic field strength-induced domain shift in MRI across 0.1T to 7T. The framework uses field-conditioned content-style pretraining to disentangle anatomical content from field-dependent contrast, enabling controllable translation across three challenge tasks (Any-to-7T, 0.1T-to-High, and Any-to-Any synthesis) while preserving subject-specific anatomy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetic field strength causes significant domain shift in MRI, affecting signal-to-noise ratio, tissue contrast, spatial detail, and anatomical boundary visibility. Cross-field MRI translation is challenging because paired acquisitions of the same subject across multiple field strengths are rarely available for training.

Method: The framework employs a 3D content encoder, a 3D style encoder, a field-conditioned style generator, an AdaIN-modulated decoder, and a multi-field discriminator. It first learns controllable field-to-field translation by disentangling anatomical content from field-dependent contrast via pretraining, then adapts the backbone to task-specific target domains. Training uses adversarial learning with cycle-consistency, identity, content, style, and diversity constraints.

Result: Evaluation on MRIxFields data spanning five field strengths and three MRI modalities shows the framework can adapt to all three challenge settings while preserving three-dimensional anatomical structure in synthesized volumes, as demonstrated on paired test data.

Conclusion: The proposed field-conditioned content-style pretraining approach effectively enables unpaired cross-field MRI translation, providing a practical solution for multi-field MRI synthesis with anatomical fidelity across the three MRIxFields challenge tasks.

Abstract: Magnetic field strength is a major source of domain shift in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), affecting signal-to-noise ratio, tissue contrast, spatial detail, and the visibility of anatomical boundaries. The MRIxFields 2026 challenge investigates this problem through cross-field MRI translation across acquisitions at 0.1T, 1.5T, 3T, 5T, and 7T. Its three tasks, Any-to-7T, 0.1T-to-High, and Any-to-Any synthesis, require the generation of target-field image characteristics while preserving subject-specific anatomy. This problem is particularly challenging because paired acquisitions of the same subject across multiple field strengths are rarely available for training. We propose a 3D unpaired cross-field MRI translation framework based on field-conditioned content-style pretraining. The proposed framework first learns controllable field-to-field translation across all available field strengths by disentangling anatomical content from field-dependent contrast characteristics. The pretrained backbone is then adapted to task-specific target domains. Our model comprises a 3D content encoder, a 3D style encoder, a field-conditioned style generator, an AdaIN-modulated decoder, and a multi-field discriminator. Adversarial learning encourages realistic target-field appearance, while cycle-consistency, identity, content, style, and diversity constraints promote anatomical fidelity and controllable translation. We evaluate the proposed method on MRIxFields data spanning five field strengths and three MRI modalities. Experiments on paired test data demonstrate that the framework can adapt to the three challenge settings while preserving three-dimensional anatomical structure in the synthesized volumes. The implementation code is publicly available at https://github.com/Idea89560041/3D-MRI-Field-Translation.

</details>


### [190] [Did the Grid Erase the Event? EndoClock for Auditing Medical World-Model Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09266)
*Yarin Udi,Tom Sharon-Shahak,Roee Masad,Dan Pri-Tal*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper identifies a critical failure mode in medical world models where synchronizing multimodal data onto a fixed-rate grid can erase task-relevant evidence when observation clocks are endogenous. It introduces a four-regime taxonomy (sampled values, grid-cell update patterns, native timing, external acquisition channel) to characterize where evidence survives, and presents EndoClock, a conservative pretraining audit tool that reports the lowest witness-bearing representation or flags the case as unresolved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical world models typically synchronize multimodal recordings onto a fixed-rate grid, resampling native streams onto a shared time axis. When observation clocks depend on latent or acquisition state (endogenous clocks), this synchronization is not neutral and can destroy task-relevant evidence before the model ever sees the data—a problem that has not been systematically characterized or audited.

Method: The paper proposes (1) a four-regime taxonomy that classifies where evidence needed to distinguish a target event or state survives: in sampled values, grid-cell update patterns, native timing, or an external acquisition channel; and (2) EndoClock, an executable pretraining audit that operationalizes this taxonomy, conservatively reporting the lowest witness-bearing representation supported by available evidence, or marking the case unresolved.

Result: The paper demonstrates the failure mode in echocardiography: during pulsed-wave Doppler acquisition, B-mode video write-outs cease while the corresponding measurement events exist only in an external acquisition log. The audit illustrates how synchronization would erase the evidence needed for the task.

Conclusion: This preliminary work serves as a failure alert and executable audit. Its practical recommendation is to preserve the native observation process long enough to determine whether synchronization has erased information required by the intended task, rather than blindly assuming fixed-rate resampling is neutral.

Abstract: Medical world models commonly learn from multimodal recordings synchronized onto a fixed-rate grid. This preprocessing resamples each native stream onto a shared time axis. Each stream has an observation clock that governs when observations are emitted or updated. When this clock depends on the latent or acquisition state, it is endogenous. In such settings, synchronization may not be neutral and can erase task-relevant evidence before the model sees the data. We introduce a four-regime taxonomy that characterizes where the evidence needed to distinguish a target event or state survives. The relevant witness may remain in the sampled values, in grid-cell update patterns, in native timing, or only in an external acquisition channel. EndoClock operationalizes this taxonomy as a conservative pretraining audit. It reports the lowest witness-bearing representation supported by the available evidence, or unresolved when no regime can be established. We illustrate this failure in echocardiography, where B-mode video write-outs cease during pulsed-wave Doppler acquisition while the corresponding measurement events remain recorded only in an external acquisition log. This work is a preliminary failure alert and executable audit. Its practical message is to preserve the native observation process long enough to determine whether synchronization has erased information required by the intended task.

</details>


### [191] [GRASP: Granularity-Aware Region Alignment and Semantic Prototype Learning for Fine-Grained Cross-Modal Understanding in Drone Views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09270)
*Jiahui Cui,Yan Zhao,Kan Wei,Enze Zhu,Peirong Zhang,Lei Wang,Yiru Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GRASP is a learning framework for fine-grained cross-modal understanding in drone views, addressing two key challenges: Cross-Modal Focus Misalignment (background clutter) and Visual Isomorphism (ambiguous similar candidates), via Region-Focused Alignment (RFA) and Semantic Perturbation Enhanced Matching (SPEM) with a Semantic Prototype Codebook (SPC).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Drone-view aerial vision-language navigation suffers from two distinct challenges: (1) at the macro level, overwhelming background clutter causes Cross-Modal Focus Misalignment where models prioritize global environment similarities over object details; (2) at the micro level, Visual Isomorphism creates ambiguity where candidates share similar geometric structures but differ only in subtle attributes.

Method: The proposed GRASP framework uses two synergistic strategies: (1) Region-Focused Alignment (RFA) for object-centric cross-modal alignment while suppressing background interference; (2) Semantic Perturbation Enhanced Matching (SPEM), which uses a foreground-purified Semantic Prototype Codebook (SPC) to construct semantically perturbed negatives for fine-grained semantic discrimination.

Result: Extensive experiments on the GeoText-1652 benchmark and the unseen ERA dataset demonstrate that GRASP achieves competitive performance in drone-view fine-grained image-text retrieval.

Conclusion: GRASP validates the effectiveness of granularity-aware region alignment and semantic prototype learning for cross-modal understanding in aerial scenarios, and the code is publicly available.

Abstract: Fine-grained cross-modal understanding in drone views is essential for aerial vision-language navigation. However, the inherent wide field of view and overhead perspective of drone scenarios impose dual challenges on vision-language understanding. At the macro level, overwhelming background clutter in visual representations leads to Cross-Modal Focus Misalignment, where the model prioritizes global environmental similarities over specific object details. At the micro level, Visual Isomorphism creates ambiguity, where candidates share similar geometric structures yet differ only in subtle attributes. To address these challenges, we propose the Granularity-Aware Region Alignment and Semantic Prototype (GRASP) learning framework, enhancing discriminative capability through two synergistic strategies. Specifically, we introduce Region-Focused Alignment (RFA) to promote object-centric cross-modal alignment while suppressing background interference. Concurrently, to tackle visual isomorphism, we propose Semantic Perturbation Enhanced Matching (SPEM), which leverages a foreground-purified Semantic Prototype Codebook (SPC) to construct semantically perturbed negatives for fine-grained semantic discrimination. Extensive experiments on the GeoText-1652 benchmark and the unseen ERA dataset demonstrate that GRASP achieves competitive performance in drone-view fine-grained image-text retrieval, validating its effectiveness for cross-modal understanding in aerial scenarios. Our code implementation is available at https://github.com/UCAS-JC/GRASP.

</details>


### [192] [UniDFKD: A Unified Semantic Prior Framework for Architecture-Agnostic Data-Free Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09287)
*Xuewan He,Tong Chu,Zihan Cheng,Yuchen Su,Qianxin Xia,Guoming Lu,Jielei Wang,Wen Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniDFKD is a unified data-free knowledge distillation framework that replaces architecture-specific statistical priors with explicit, architecture-agnostic semantic priors to enable effective DFKD for both CNNs and Vision Transformers, achieving over 20% average improvement over existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DFKD methods rely heavily on architecture-specific statistical priors (e.g., Batch Normalization statistics) to guide data synthesis, which are often absent in modern architectures like Vision Transformers, leading to degraded semantic quality of synthesized data and catastrophic performance degradation.

Method: UniDFKD governs the entire synthesis-distillation pipeline along three dimensions: (1) Categorical Semantic Conditioning (CSC) defines what to synthesize by modulating the generator with language-derived embeddings; (2) Spatial Semantic Anchoring (SSA) anchors the teacher's spatial attributions to a Gaussian prior to dictate where evidence belongs; (3) Spatial Semantic Distillation (SSD) aligns teacher-student spatial evidence alongside predictions to control how knowledge is transferred.

Result: Extensive experiments across CNNs and ViTs demonstrate that UniDFKD establishes a new state-of-the-art, outperforming existing methods by an average absolute margin of over 20% in both homogeneous and heterogeneous settings.

Conclusion: UniDFKD successfully addresses the architecture-dependency limitation of existing DFKD methods by replacing architecture-specific statistics with explicit semantic priors, enabling robust data-free knowledge distillation across diverse architectures including both CNNs and ViTs.

Abstract: Data-Free Knowledge Distillation (DFKD) transfers knowledge from a pretrained teacher model to a compact student model by synthesizing semantically informative data, eliminating the need for access to the original training dataset. Existing DFKD methods rely heavily on architecture-specific statistical priors (e.g., Batch Normalization statistics) to guide data synthesis, however, such architecture-dependent priors are often absent in modern architectures such as Vision Transformers (ViTs), resulting in degraded semantic quality of the synthesized data and consequently catastrophic performance degradation. In this paper, we propose \emph{UniDFKD}, a unified data-free knowledge distillation framework that replaces architecture-specific statistics with explicit, architecture-agnostic semantic priors. \emph{UniDFKD} governs the entire synthesis-distillation pipeline along three dimensions: (1) Categorical Semantic Conditioning (CSC) defines \emph{what} to synthesize by persistently modulating the generator with language-derived embeddings to capture semantic diversity; (2) Spatial Semantic Anchoring (SSA) dictates \emph{where} evidence belongs by anchoring the teacher's spatial attributions to a Gaussian prior; and (3) Spatial Semantic Distillation (SSD) controls \emph{how} knowledge is transferred by explicitly aligning teacher-student spatial evidence alongside predictions. Extensive experiments across CNNs and ViTs demonstrate that UniDFKD establishes a new state-of-the-art, outperforming existing methods by an average absolute margin of over 20\% in both homogeneous and heterogeneous settings.

</details>


### [193] [Bootstrapping Vision-Language Model for Hysteroscopic Surgical Scene Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09302)
*Jun Huang,Meiyi Chen,Zijie Yue,Yuhang Xiao,Fang Li,Hanli Wang,Xiaowen Tong,Yi Guo,Miaojing Shi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes VLM-hyster, the first vision-language model-based hysteroscopic surgical scene segmentation method that performs pixel-wise localization for fifteen categories, using a segmentation backbone with a transformer decoder, category-specific text prompts, and a masked distillation branch, validated on a large multicentric dataset and showing superior performance over state-of-the-art models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hysteroscopic surgical scene segmentation is crucial for computer-assisted interventions but faces challenges from high morphological similarity among lesions and artifacts like specular reflections, motion blur, and fluid occlusions in surgical videos.

Method: The method uses a VLM-based segmentation backbone with a pretrained image encoder for visual feature extraction and a transformer-based decoder for dense prediction. It incorporates category-specific text prompts and a masked distillation branch that filters out low-correlation visual features, focusing the model on category-specific regions. A large multicentric dataset of 4,020 high-resolution images with mask annotations was collected for training and evaluation.

Result: VLM-hyster substantially outperforms state-of-the-art AI models, with robustness and generalizability confirmed through gynecologist assessments and multicentre/prospective validations.

Conclusion: VLM-hyster shows considerable potential for enabling AI-assisted localization of surgical instruments and lesions in hysteroscopic surgeries.

Abstract: Hysteroscopic surgical scene segmentation plays a pivotal role in understanding the hysteroscopic intraoperative environment as well as computer-assisted intervention. However, this task presents unique challenges due to the high morphological similarity among different lesions and the presence of artifacts such as specular reflections, motion blur, and fluid occlusions in surgical videos. In this work, we propose the first vision-language model (VLM)-based hysteroscopic surgical scene segmentation method, which performs pixel-wise localization for fifteen representative categories in hysteroscopic surgical scenes. Our VLM-hyster has a segmentation backbone that utilizes the pretrained image encoder for robust visual feature extraction, coupled with a transformer-based decoder for dense prediction. Moreover, we design category-specific text prompts and incorporate a masked distillation branch to filter out visual features with low correlation to the text prompts, enabling the model to focus more effectively on category-specific image regions and thereby enhancing segmentation performance. We collect a large multicentric hysteroscopic surgical scene dataset, containing 4,020 high-resolution images with detailed mask annotations, for model training and evaluation. Experimental results demonstrate that VLM-hyster substantially outperforms state-of-the-art AI models. Furthermore, extensive assessments by gynecologists, as well as multicentre and prospective validations, demonstrate VLM-hyster's robustness and generalizability. The results suggest that VLM-hyster earns considerable potential in enabling AI-assisted localization of surgical instruments and lesions in hysteroscopic surgeries. Code is available at https://github.com/viscom-tongji/VLM-hyster.

</details>


### [194] [Degraded Infrared Small Object Detection via Degradation-Adapted Physics-Guided Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09311)
*Xinkai Lu,Wenjun Chen,Yi Li,Yi Chang,Luxin Yan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes DAISOD, a degradation-adapted infrared small object detection framework that identifies degradation type/severity, adapts processing via dedicated branches, and uses physics-guided restoration to avoid over-restoration that could erase small targets. A new degraded infrared dataset is also constructed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Infrared small object detection becomes challenging under degradations like fog and nonuniformity which suppress target-background contrast. Existing preprocessing-based restoration methods are designed for specific degradation types and fail to generalize across varying degradation conditions.

Method: DAISOD first identifies the type and severity of degradations, then adapts processing through dedicated branches, and finally fuses results for subsequent detection. A physics-guided restoration mechanism explicitly estimates degradation parameters and removes degradation effects via physical models to prevent excessive restoration that could erase small targets.

Result: A degraded infrared small object detection dataset covering diverse degradation types and levels was constructed. Extensive experiments show DAISOD outperforms state-of-the-art methods under various degradation conditions.

Conclusion: DAISOD provides a robust degradation-adapted framework for infrared small object detection, achieving superior performance over existing methods under diverse degradation scenarios while avoiding over-restoration issues.

Abstract: Infrared small object detection has made significant progress in recent years. However, degradations such as fog and nonuniformity can suppress target-background contrast, substantially increasing detection difficulty. Existing methods mainly rely on image restoration as preprocessing, but they are typically designed for specific degradation types and fail to generalize to varying degradations. To alleviate this, we propose DAISOD, a degradation-adapted infrared small object detection framework for robust detection under different degradations. DAISOD first identifies the type and severity of degradations, then adapts the processing via dedicated branches, and finally fuses the results for subsequent detection. Moreover, a physics-guided restoration mechanism is incorporated to explicitly estimate degradation parameters and remove degradation effects through physical models, avoiding excessive restoration that may erase small targets. Moreover, we construct a degraded infrared small object detection dataset covering diverse degradation types and levels. Extensive experiments show that DAISOD outperforms state-of-the-art methods under various degradation conditions.

</details>


### [195] [MemeMind: Reference-Guided Trace Construction for Offline Context Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09316)
*Run Yang,Weihang Wang,Boheng Sheng,Yuchen He,Jielei Zhang,Pengyu Chen,Zhiyu Wu,Qiang Sun,Huyang Sun,Longwen Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MemeMind is a context optimization framework that recovers missing successful tool-use experience when an agent only has failed rollouts. It uses offline reference answers to construct verified tool traces via text search, image retrieval, and visual grounding, then summarizes them into per-tool instructions and a shared guide. Evaluated on a 1,000-meme benchmark (MemeX), it outperforms strong context optimization baselines by up to 22% under GPT-5 judging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Offline context optimization typically needs successful rollouts to learn from, but in challenging multi-modal settings the agent may fail all rollouts for a query, leaving the optimizer with no example of correct tool usage. The paper addresses this gap by leveraging offline reference answers to synthesize the missing successful tool-use experience.

Method: MemeMind has two components: (1) TraceBuilder, which extracts evidence required by a reference answer, executes text search, image retrieval, and visual grounding to construct a tool trace, and verifies it before adding to the adaptation buffer; (2) ToolGuide, which summarizes collected traces into a shared guide and individual tool-specific instructions. The reference answers and traces are used only during adaptation; inference uses the learned guides with a frozen model.

Result: On the MemeX benchmark (1,000 expert-annotated memes from Anime, Comic, and Game domains), MemeMind improves over the strongest context optimization baseline by 22.0% and 21.1% on Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B, and by 8.1% and 8.0% on Qwen3-VL-235B-A22B under GPT-5 judging. Ablations show constructing successful tool use for failed groups provides the largest component gain.

Conclusion: MemeMind demonstrates that offline reference answers can effectively recover missing successful tool-use experience for failed rollout groups, significantly improving context optimization in demanding multi-modal interpretation tasks. The approach produces more effective evidence acquisition at inference time, with tool-trace construction from failures being the dominant source of gain.

Abstract: Offline context optimization improves an agent by revising its instructions and examples while keeping the model frozen. This approach learns from rollouts on an adaptation set, but some queries produce only failed rollouts. In these cases, the optimizer sees no successful example of how the available tools can reach the correct answer. We introduce MemeMind, which uses an offline reference answer to recover this missing experience. TraceBuilder identifies the evidence required by the reference, executes text search, image retrieval, and visual grounding, and verifies the resulting tool trace before adding it to the adaptation buffer. ToolGuide then summarizes the collected traces into a shared guide and separate instructions for each tool. The reference answers and constructed traces are used only during adaptation, while inference uses the learned guides with a frozen model. We study this problem through Anime, Comic, and Game meme interpretation. These memes combine edited and ambiguous visual content, overlaid text, long tail franchise knowledge, and culture specific references. Their interpretation can require coordinated visual grounding, image retrieval, and text search, making them a demanding setting in which native rollout groups may fail together. We evaluate MemeMind on MemeX, a benchmark of 1,000 such memes annotated by experts. Across two Qwen3-VL models, two language partitions, and two independent judges, MemeMind improves over the strongest context optimization baseline by 22.0% and 21.1% on Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B, and by 8.1% and 8.0% on Qwen3-VL-235B-A22B under GPT-5 judging. Ablations and held out traces show that constructing successful tool use for failed groups provides the largest component gain and produces more effective evidence acquisition at inference time.

</details>


### [196] [Warp-free Cross-view Geo-localization via Feature-space Consensus Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09321)
*Zhuo Song,Lian Xu,Runqing Jiang,Yongjian Zhang,Kunhong Li,Ye Zhang,Yulan Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a joint-view consensus-guided learning framework for cross-view geo-localization that avoids explicit geometric warping. It mines cross-view semantic consensus directly in feature space via an auxiliary joint-view pathway during training, uses global pattern probes as a semantic dictionary to align heterogeneous feature spaces, and distills consensus-mining capability into single-view encoders via a consensus-mediated contrastive objective. The method achieves SOTA performance on four benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing cross-view geo-localization methods rely on geometric warping to expose co-visible cues, but such transformations depend on restrictive spatial assumptions and introduce severe visual distortions under view-dependent visibility, leading to noisy supervision and fragile correspondences.

Method: The framework (1) uses an auxiliary joint-view pathway during training to enable direct cross-view interaction and aggregate corroborative evidence into a unified consensus representation; (2) introduces global pattern probes acting as a semantic dictionary to project heterogeneous single/joint-view streams into a strictly aligned metric space; (3) employs a consensus-mediated contrastive objective to pull single-view embeddings toward the joint-view anchor, distilling consensus-mining into single-view encoders for inference.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art performance across four standard cross-view geo-localization benchmarks.

Conclusion: Discovering cross-view semantic consensus—rather than forcing geometric alignment—is crucial for reliable geo-localization, and the proposed distillation approach enables robust single-view retrieval at inference without explicit warping.

Abstract: Cross-view geo-localization is challenging due to drastic viewpoint changes and large appearance discrepancies between street-level and satellite imagery. Although existing methods often use geometric warping to expose co-visible cues, such transformations rely on restrictive spatial assumptions and inevitably introduce severe visual distortions under view-dependent visibility, yielding noisy supervision and fragile correspondences. To overcome this, we propose a novel joint-view consensus-guided learning framework that entirely bypasses explicit geometric warping. Instead of forcing rigid spatial alignment, we dynamically mine and adaptively strengthen a semantic consensus directly within the feature space. Specifically, an auxiliary joint-view pathway during training enables direct cross-view interaction, allowing each view to selectively aggregate corroborative evidence into a unified consensus representation. To resolve feature heterogeneity among the single- and joint-view streams, we introduce global pattern probes acting as a semantic dictionary to project divergent modalities into a strictly aligned metric space. Guided by a consensus-mediated contrastive objective, single-view embeddings are explicitly pulled toward the joint-view anchor during training, distilling this consensus-mining capability into the single-view encoders for robust retrieval at inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across four standard benchmarks, underscoring the importance of discovering cross-view semantic consensus for reliable geo-localization.

</details>


### [197] [Diffusion Image Editing via Asynchronous Token Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09322)
*Yang Shi,Liangsi Lu,Minzhe Guo,Yifeng Xie,Yanhui Chen,Jingchao Wang,Xuhang Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ATDEdit is an inference-time framework for text-guided diffusion image editing that uses asynchronous token decoding with token-indexed condition switching to modify semantic attributes while preserving identity, layout, and background, achieving state-of-the-art preservation metrics on PIE-Bench.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Naively switching text conditions during diffusion sampling causes global drift, as denoising dynamics propagate changes across tokens and disrupt unedited regions. Existing methods either require external masks or model fine-tuning to address this, motivating a mask-free, training-free solution for background preservation during local edits.

Method: ATDEdit treats each sampler step as a parallel update of a globally coupled token matrix, enabling token-indexed condition switching with differentiated update policies. It estimates editable locations via token-wise conditional surprisal, applies target-conditioned corrections only to selected tokens, supplies source key/value memory at keep-token positions, and projects selected keep-token latent rows back to their source values—all without external masks or fine-tuning.

Result: On PIE-Bench, ATDEdit achieves the strongest reported preservation metrics, including 27.44 dB PSNR and 0.055 LPIPS, while retaining competitive semantic alignment.

Conclusion: ATDEdit demonstrates that asynchronous, token-level condition switching can effectively combine local editing with background preservation without requiring spatial masks or model fine-tuning, setting a new state of the art in preservation metrics.

Abstract: Text-guided diffusion image editing aims to modify semantic attributes of an image while preserving its identity, layout, and background. However, naïvely switching the text condition during sampling often causes global drift, as denoising dynamics propagate changes across tokens and can disrupt unedited regions. To address this issue, we propose \textbf{A}synchronous \textbf{T}oken \textbf{D}ecoding \textbf{Edit} (ATDEdit), an inference-time framework that views each sampler step as a parallel update of a globally coupled token matrix and enables token-indexed condition switching with differentiated update policies. Instead of applying synchronous target-conditioned updates to all tokens, ATDEdit estimates editable locations using token-wise conditional surprisal and applies target-conditioned corrections to the selected token set. It supplies source key/value memory at keep-token positions and projects selected keep-token latent rows back to their source values; these operations promote background preservation but do not constitute a pixel-level invariance guarantee. This approach combines local editing and background preservation without external or user-provided spatial masks and without model fine-tuning. On PIE-Bench, ATDEdit achieves the strongest reported preservation metrics, including 27.44~dB PSNR and 0.055 LPIPS, while retaining competitive semantic alignment.

</details>


### [198] [Revisiting the Current Frame: Physical-Trace-Guided Network Output Correction for Video Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09342)
*Yifeng Lin,Liuxiang Qiu,Guangming Ren,Tiesong Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ANCHOR is a model-agnostic framework that uses the low-quality current frame as a temporally aligned anchor to correct video restoration outputs. It estimates a spatial trust field from heterogeneous physical-trace evidence and adaptively balances the restoration proposal with the original observation, improving results on HDR video reconstruction and video deraining across various state-of-the-art models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Video restoration methods rely on temporal information but reference frames can introduce inconsistent degradation, content discrepancy, or reconstruction errors due to physical variations, occlusion, and imperfect temporal aggregation. Existing work focuses on improving restoration networks while neglecting the reliability of generated outputs at different spatial locations.

Method: ANCHOR revisits the low-quality current frame as a temporally aligned anchor for correction. It estimates a spatial trust field from heterogeneous physical-trace evidence and adaptively balances the restoration proposal against the original observation based on this trust field.

Result: Experiments on HDR video reconstruction and video deraining demonstrate consistent improvements across various state-of-the-art restoration models, validating the effectiveness of reliability-aware output correction.

Conclusion: Reliability-aware output correction, embodied by the spatial trust field framework, is an effective and model-agnostic approach to improve video restoration performance.

Abstract: Video restoration methods exploit temporal information to recover information missing from degraded observations. However, reference frames within the sequence may introduce inconsistent degradation, content discrepancy, or reconstruction errors due to physical image-formation variations, occlusion, and imperfect temporal aggregation. Existing approaches mainly focus on improving restoration networks, while the reliability of the generated outputs at different spatial locations remains largely unexplored. In this work, we propose ANCHOR, a model-agnostic framework that revisits the low-quality current frame as a temporally aligned anchor for video restoration correction. Specifically, ANCHOR estimates a spatial trust field from heterogeneous physical-trace evidence and adaptively balances the restoration proposal with the original observation. Experiments on High Dynamic Range video reconstruction and video deraining demonstrate consistent improvements across various state-of-the-art restoration models, validating the effectiveness of reliability-aware output correction for video restoration.

</details>


### [199] [Beyond Global Editing: Per-Instance Disentangled Subspaces for Training-Free Hallucination Mitigation in LVLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09344)
*Ali Cheraghian,Hamidreza Dastmalchi,Hamed Barzamini,Morteza Saberi,Mojtaba Golzan,Shafin Rahman,Hossein Rahmani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a training-free hallucination mitigation framework for large vision-language models (LVLMs) that constructs multiple disentangled hallucination subspaces, each capturing a distinct hallucination mode, and dynamically combines them per-input at test time to selectively suppress the most probable hallucination directions while preserving image-grounded semantics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LVLMs suffer from hallucinations where generated text inaccurately describes visual input. While fine-tuning mitigates this, it is computationally expensive and requires large curated datasets. Existing model-editing approaches use a single global subspace to correct hallucinations, failing to capture diverse hallucination modes across different inputs.

Method: The framework is training-free and works at test time. It first constructs a set of Disentangled Hallucination Subspaces, each isolating a distinct hallucination mode. During inference, the model adaptively computes weights reflecting each input's relationship to these subspaces, guiding a dynamically combined projection that selectively suppresses the most probable hallucination directions while preserving image-grounded semantics.

Result: Extensive experiments across multiple vision-language benchmarks and LVLM families demonstrate consistent improvements, highlighting the robustness, generalizability, and efficiency of the approach.

Conclusion: The proposed dynamic, per-instance hallucination suppression framework outperforms existing model-editing approaches by better capturing diverse hallucination modes, offering a more efficient, stable, and generalizable solution to LVLM hallucination mitigation.

Abstract: Recent advances in large vision-language models (LVLMs) have enabled powerful multimodal reasoning by integrating visual encoders with large language models (LLMs). However, their reliability is frequently undermined by hallucinations, where generated text inaccurately describes the visual input. Although fine-tuning can mitigate this problem, it is computationally expensive and requires large, curated datasets, making training-free alternatives attractive. Among these, model editing is more promising than decoding-based approaches: decoding methods adapt outputs per input but introduce computational overhead and instability, whereas model editing modifies internal representations offline, providing a more efficient and stable solution. However, existing model-editing techniques typically rely on a single global subspace to correct hallucinations, treating all test samples identically and failing to capture diverse hallucination modes across inputs. To address this limitation, we propose a training-free hallucination mitigation framework for dynamic, per-instance suppression at test time. Our method first constructs a set of Disentangled Hallucination Subspaces, each isolating a distinct hallucination mode. During inference, the model adaptively calculates weights reflecting each input's relationship to these subspaces, guiding a dynamically combined projection that selectively suppresses the most probable hallucination directions while preserving image-grounded semantics. Extensive experiments across multiple vision-language benchmarks and LVLM families demonstrate consistent improvements, highlighting the robustness, generalizability, and efficiency of our approach.

</details>


### [200] [One-Time Training for All Grains: Open-Set Grain Recognition and Quantitative Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09345)
*Qihe Su,Mengyu Sun,Yuxi Ke,Zhuoyan Jiang,Wanneng Yang,Chenglong Huang,Ziyuan Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GROW is a framework for open-set grain recognition and quantitative analysis that performs class-agnostic grain localization, builds extensible fused descriptors in a GrainBank, and recognizes query grains via rank-similarity weighted top-k retrieval—allowing new varieties to be added without model retraining, reducing registration time from 4153s to 39s while maintaining competitive performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing grain recognition methods are trained on a fixed variety set and require data collection and model retraining when new varieties are introduced, which is inefficient given the growing number of grain varieties from crop breeding advances.

Method: GROW first performs class-agnostic grain localization to convert mixed-grain images into instances for counting and phenotypic measurement. It then fuses visual embeddings with morphological descriptors into grain descriptors stored in an extensible GrainBank. Recognition is done via rank-similarity weighted top-k retrieval, and new varieties are added by simply appending descriptors without retraining deployed models.

Result: Extensive experiments under progressive variety expansion, varying grain densities, and background domain shifts demonstrate scalability, robustness, and adaptability. GROW reduced average category-registration time from 4153s to 39s compared to joint retraining while maintaining competitive recognition performance.

Conclusion: GROW provides an efficient and maintainable solution for extensible grain recognition, counting, and phenotypic analysis without repeated model retraining.

Abstract: Advances in crop breeding have introduced an increasing number of grain varieties, creating a growing demand for efficient variety recognition and quantitative analysis. However, existing methods are typically trained on a fixed variety set, and incorporating newly introduced varieties requires additional data collection and model retraining. To address this limitation, we propose GROW, a framework for Grain Recognition and quantitative analysis in Open sets Without retraining. GROW first performs class-agnostic grain localization, converting mixed-grain images into individual instances for variety-wise counting and phenotypic measurement. It then combines visual embeddings and morphological descriptors into fused grain descriptors stored in an extensible GrainBank. Query grains are recognized through rank-similarity weighted top-k retrieval, and newly introduced varieties are incorporated by appending their descriptors without updating the deployed models. Extensive experiments under progressive variety expansion, varying grain densities, and background domain shifts demonstrate the scalability, robustness, and adaptability of GROW. Compared with joint retraining, GROW reduced the average category-registration time from 4153 s to only 39 s while maintaining competitive recognition performance. These results demonstrate that GROW provides an efficient and maintainable solution for extensible grain recognition, counting, and phenotypic analysis without repeated model retraining.

</details>


### [201] [Alpha as an Efficiency Signal: Visibility-Routed RGBA Image-to-Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09355)
*Zhe Li,Honghao Qiao,Zhixin Xu,Qijie Wang,Bo Peng,Dawei Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces GameAlpha-2.4K, a game-style RGBA video dataset, and trains a reference-conditioned RGBA video generator that jointly produces RGB frames and alpha mattes in a single pass. A visibility router skips transparent tokens during DiT denoising for efficiency, achieving 1.2x speedup with minimal quality loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Generating high-quality RGBA animations for games is challenging due to (1) existing RGBA datasets being dominated by photorealistic content with limited game asset coverage, and (2) traditional generate-then-matte pipelines producing unstable alpha outputs due to background blur in semi-transparent regions.

Method: The method includes: (1) building GameAlpha-2.4K, a 2.4K-clip game-style RGBA video dataset with matte-friendly synthesis, multi-hypothesis alpha recovery, and compositing-based quality gates; (2) training a reference-conditioned RGBA video generator that jointly outputs RGB frames and alpha mattes; (3) proposing a visibility router that identifies transparent tokens early and bypasses their later DiT updates, with x_0-lock guiding them along the flow-matching schedule toward self-predicted endpoints.

Result: The model achieves lower FVD than traditional two-stage pipelines. The visibility router skips 35% of token evaluations in the final two DiT denoising steps, providing a 1.2x backbone speedup with negligible quality degradation compared to dense inference.

Conclusion: Jointly modeling RGB and alpha in a single pass with a dedicated game-style dataset and an efficient visibility router provides both better quality (lower FVD) and improved efficiency for game RGBA video generation.

Abstract: RGBA videos combine RGB appearance with an alpha channel, enabling animated assets to be applied across arbitrary backgrounds, which are heavily used in gaming industry. However, generating high-quality RGBA animations for games remains challenging for two reasons. First, most existing RGBA video datasets are dominated by photorealistic content, with limited coverage of game assets. Second, the traditional generate-then-matte pipelines estimate alpha only after RGB synthesis, so semi-transparent regions are often blurred by background, resulting in unstable matting outputs. More recently, many methods have begun to model RGB and alpha jointly, but existing approaches are mostly text-conditioned, and still have unresolved issues in efficiency and quality. To address these challenges, we introduce GameAlpha-2.4K, a 2.4K-clip game-style RGBA video dataset built with matte-friendly synthesis, multi-hypothesis alpha recovery, and compositing-based quality gates. Using this dataset, we train a reference-conditioned RGBA video generator that jointly produces RGB frames and alpha mattes in a single pass. To improve efficiency, we propose a visibility router that identifies transparent tokens in an early stage and bypasses their later DiT updates, while x_0-lock guides them along the original flow-matching schedule toward self-predicted endpoints. Our model obtains lower FVD than traditional two-stage pipelines, and the visibility router skips 35% of token evaluations in the final two DiT denoising steps, providing a 1.2x backbone speedup with negligible quality degradation compared to dense inference.

</details>


### [202] [ControlRadio: Prompt-Driven Controllable Diffusion for Cross-Modal Radio Map Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09357)
*Kangjun Liu,Xiying Pan,Shuhang Zhang,Xiang Xiang,Ke Chen,Yaowei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ControlRadio is a controllable generative framework that creates radio maps from natural-language descriptions and environmental layouts, achieving state-of-the-art accuracy with computation time reduced by over four orders of magnitude compared to conventional simulation-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional radio map construction requires dense measurements or expensive physical simulations, limiting scalability and real-time deployment. Existing generative AI approaches lack fine-grained control and physical consistency in real-world wireless environments.

Method: A controllable generative framework that uses joint semantic and spatial conditioning from natural-language descriptions and environmental layouts (building structures, transmitter locations). It incorporates a controlled latent prior and layout-aware conditioning to ensure stability and structural consistency, producing interpretable and propagation-plausible radio maps.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and strong generalization across diverse urban scenarios, while reducing computation time by more than four orders of magnitude compared with conventional simulation-based methods.

Conclusion: The results suggest a new paradigm for scalable and controllable wireless environment modeling, with broad implications for next-generation communication systems and data-driven radio sensing.

Abstract: Radio maps describe how wireless signals propagate across space and are essential for wireless communication, sensing, and network planning. However, constructing accurate radio maps traditionally requires either dense measurements or computationally expensive physical simulations, which limits scalability and real-time deployment. Recent advances in generative artificial intelligence offer a promising alternative, but existing approaches lack fine-grained control and physical consistency when applied to real-world wireless environments. Here we present \textbf{ControlRadio}, a controllable generative framework that produces radio maps from natural-language descriptions and environmental layouts, including building structures and transmitter locations. Joint semantic and spatial conditioning enables interpretable, propagation-plausible generation, while a controlled latent prior and layout-aware conditioning improve stability and structural consistency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ControlRadio achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and strong generalization across diverse urban scenarios, while reducing computation time by more than four orders of magnitude compared with conventional simulation-based methods. Such results suggest a new paradigm for scalable and controllable wireless environment modeling, with broad implications for next-generation communication systems and data-driven radio sensing.

</details>


### [203] [Deep Learning based Detection of Fishing Vessels and Fishing Monitoring using Nightlight Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09360)
*Shantakar Mohanty,Prasun Kumar Gupta,Raian Vargas Maretto*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study develops a dual-branch YOLO11 deep learning model to detect small-scale fishing vessels using SDGSAT-1 nighttime light imagery along India's western coast, achieving high accuracy (F1=0.96) and identifying 24,379 potential "dark vessels" (77.3%) lacking AIS transmission out of 31,525 detected instances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Maritime surveillance requires monitoring fishing vessel activities, particularly "dark vessels" that operate without AIS transmission. There is a need for novel approaches to detect these vessels, especially small-scale fishing vessels that may evade traditional monitoring systems.

Method: A dual-branch YOLO11 architecture was developed using 10-meter panchromatic and 40-meter RGB nighttime light imagery from SDGSAT-1 satellite. The model features parallel convolutional backbones processing both modalities before concatenation, optimized for small object detection in NTL imagery. Cross-matching with AIS data and spatio-temporal analysis were performed.

Result: The dual-branch YOLO11 achieved precision of 0.99, recall of 0.93, F1-score of 0.96, and mAP@50 of 0.96, outperforming single-branch YOLOv5s, YOLOv8s, and standard YOLO11s. It detected 31,525 vessel instances (2022-23), with 24,379 (77.3%) identified as potential dark vessels. Peak fishing activity occurred during January-April, within 50-100 km of the coastline.

Conclusion: Nighttime light satellite imagery combined with deep learning is effective for fishing vessel detection, providing valuable insights into fishing patterns and regulatory compliance issues in Indian waters, thereby enhancing maritime surveillance capabilities.

Abstract: The demand for maritime surveillance has given rise to the need for monitoring fishing vessel activities, particularly in addressing the challenge of "dark vessels" that operate without Automatic Identification System (AIS) transmission. This study presents a novel approach for detecting small-scale fishing vessels using nighttime light (NTL) imagery from the SDGSAT-1 satellite, combined with deep learning techniques to enhance fishing monitoring awareness along the western coast of India. A dual-branch YOLO11 architecture was developed to exploit both the 10-meter panchromatic and 40-meter RGB imagery from SDGSAT-1. The custom model architecture was specifically optimized for small object detection in NTL imagery, featuring parallel convolutional backbones that process both modalities before concatenation for enhanced feature extraction. The dual-branch YOLO11 model demonstrated optimal performance with a precision of 0.99, recall of 0.93, F1-score of 0.96, and mAP@50 of 0.96, significantly outperforming single-branch implementations of YOLOv5s, YOLOv8s, and standard YOLO11s architectures. When applied to the western coast of India, the model detected 31525 vessel instances across the temporal dataset spanning 2022-23. Cross-matching analysis with AIS data revealed that only 7146 (22.7%) of detected vessels had corresponding AIS transmissions, while 24379 (77.3%) were identified as potential dark vessels. Spatio-temporal analysis showed peak fishing activity during January-April, with a primary activity corridor parallel to the coastline within 50-100 km, corresponding to productive continental shelf areas. This research contributes to maritime surveillance capabilities by highlighting the effectiveness of nighttime lights satellite imagery for fishing vessel detection and provides valuable insights into fishing patterns and potential regulatory compliance issues in Indian waters.

</details>


### [204] [FeedbackTrack: Visual-Cortex-Inspired Cross-Frame Feedback for Transformer Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09369)
*Yueyang Cang,Xiaoteng Zhang,Zhiyuan Ning,Yuchen He,Li Shi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FeedbackTrack introduces a visual-cortex-inspired framework that adds sparse, group-level layer-aligned cross-frame feedback to pretrained Transformer trackers, caching previous-frame intermediate states and returning them via Query Feedback (token-level) and Gate Feedback (feature modulation) pathways, improving tracking performance across multiple backbones with less than 1% added parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Most Transformer trackers rely on predominantly feed-forward feature extraction, and existing temporal mechanisms only update templates, prompts, queries, or prediction states—but intermediate representations are rarely reused to modulate corresponding processing stages. This misses the recurrent feedback principles observed in the visual cortex.

Method: FeedbackTrack introduces sparse, group-level layer-aligned cross-frame feedback into pretrained Transformer trackers. Previous-frame intermediate states are detached, cached, and returned to corresponding Transformer groups in the current frame via two lightweight pathways: Query Feedback (token-level query modulation) and Gate Feedback (context-dependent feature modulation). It requires only a fixed-size one-frame cache and preserves the original tracking pipeline.

Result: Across SPMTrack and ARTrackV2, FeedbackTrack improves five backbone configurations on LaSOT and GOT-10k, achieving 83.4 AO and 79.1 AUC with SPMTrack-G while adding less than 1% parameters. Cross-frame feedback outperforms same-frame modulation by 1.8-3.2 AO points.

Conclusion: The gains come mainly from recurrent historical information rather than same-frame modulation, and analysis reveals a non-uniform depth-dependent organization of learned feedback strengths, highlighting the effectiveness of recurrent feedback for Transformer tracking.

Abstract: Visual object tracking requires effective temporal integration, yet most Transformer trackers still rely on predominantly feed-forward feature extraction. Existing temporal mechanisms typically update templates, prompts, queries, or prediction states, while intermediate representations are rarely reused to modulate corresponding processing stages. We propose \textbf{FeedbackTrack}, a visual-cortex-inspired framework that introduces sparse, group-level layer-aligned cross-frame feedback into pretrained Transformer trackers. Previous-frame intermediate states are detached, cached, and returned to corresponding Transformer groups in the current frame through two lightweight pathways: Query Feedback for token-level query modulation and Gate Feedback for context-dependent feature modulation. FeedbackTrack preserves the original tracking pipeline with only a fixed-size one-frame cache. Across SPMTrack and ARTrackV2, FeedbackTrack consistently improves five backbone configurations on LaSOT and GOT-10k, achieving 83.4 AO and 79.1 AUC with SPMTrack-G while adding less than 1\% parameters. Controlled comparisons show that cross-frame feedback outperforms same-frame modulation by 1.8--3.2 AO points, demonstrating that the gains mainly come from recurrent historical information. Further analysis reveals a non-uniform depth-dependent organization of learned feedback strengths, highlighting the effectiveness of recurrent feedback for Transformer tracking.

</details>


### [205] [Preserve More Details: Mitigating Content Drift in Real-World Image Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09373)
*Chunxiao Liu,Wei Liu,Anbin Xiong,Erli Meng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FSP-Diff is a one-step diffusion model for real-world image super-resolution that uses a dual-pathway architecture (Detail-Conditioned Pathway and Detail-Modulated Semantic Pathway) to address content drift caused by unclear low-quality inputs, outperforming existing one-step diffusion methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Real-ISR methods using Stable Diffusion priors overlook that insufficient clarity of LQ inputs causes content drift in generated HQ images, manifesting as visual detail degradation and textual semantic shift, which compromises fidelity and perceptual quality.
