Method: FSP-Diff is a one-step diffusion model with a dual-pathway architecture: (1) a Detail-Conditioned Pathway that injects structured details to recover fine structures, and (2) a Detail-Modulated Semantic Pathway that refines semantic guidance using structured details to mitigate semantic deviations.

Result: Extensive experiments on standard Real-ISR benchmarks show that FSP-Diff surpasses existing one-step diffusion methods in both quantitative and qualitative metrics.

Conclusion: FSP-Diff effectively addresses content drift in one-step Real-ISR by coupling detail recovery with semantic refinement, achieving superior fidelity and perceptual quality compared to prior one-step diffusion methods.

Abstract: Real-world image super-resolution (Real-ISR) aims to reconstruct high-quality (HQ) images from low-quality (LQ) inputs subject to diverse real-world degradations. Recent advances have leveraged the LQ inputs and natural image priors learned by Stable Diffusion models to achieve impressive results. However, existing methods often overlook insufficient clarity of LQ inputs inevitably induce content drift in the generated HQ images. This manifests primarily as visual detail degradation and textual semantic shift, severely compromising both fidelity and perceptual quality. To address this challenge, we propose FSP-Diff, a novel one-step diffusion model featuring a dual-pathway architecture. This architecture comprises a Detail-Conditioned Pathway for injecting structured details to recover fine structures, and a Detail-Modulated Semantic Pathway that refines semantic guidance using structured details to mitigate semantic deviations. Extensive experiments on standard Real-ISR benchmarks demonstrate that FSP-Diff surpasses existing one-step diffusion methods in both quantitative and qualitative metrics.

</details>


### [206] [Efficient Human-Contact Representation for Human-Scene Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09388)
*Nghia Vu,Tuong Do,Binh X. Nguyen,Erman Tjiputra,Anh Nguyen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces efficient sparse contact masks for human-scene interaction, paired with sparse operators that replace dense ones in deep networks, achieving better reconstruction accuracy and at least 12x speedup over state-of-the-art baselines on three benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite progress in network architectures for human-scene interaction, efficient representation of contact between humans and environments remains an open challenge due to redundancy in high-dimensional contact data.

Method: Introduces sparse contact masks that selectively pick essential contact information to reduce redundancy, and proposes a suite of sparse operators that replace traditional dense operators in deep network layers for faster computation and better precision.

Result: Outperforms state-of-the-art models in reconstruction accuracy and achieves at least 12x computation speedup over recent baselines across contact prediction and scene synthesis tasks on three public benchmarks.

Conclusion: Sparse contact representation combined with sparse operators provides both efficiency and accuracy improvements for human-scene interaction tasks.

Abstract: Human-scene interaction is an active research topic with several industrial applications in virtual reality, gaming, robotics, and surveillance. Despite significant progress in network architectures to improve the results or optimize models' parameters for fast inference speed, the efficient representation of contact between humans and their environments remains an open challenge. In this paper, we propose a new efficient human-contact representation for human-scene interaction. Our primary contribution is the introduction of sparse contact masks that strategically select essential contact information, significantly reducing redundant data in high-dimensional inputs. Leveraging this efficient contact representation, we propose a suite of sparse operators to replace traditional dense operators within deep network layers for faster computation. Our approach not only enhances computational speed but also filters out non-essential contact data, thereby improving the precision of human-scene interaction models. To validate the effectiveness of our method, we conduct intensive experiments across three public benchmark datasets, focusing on two critical tasks for human-scene interaction: contact prediction and scene synthesis. The experimental results show that our approach outperforms state-of-the-art models in reconstruction accuracy and achieves a computation speed-up of at least 12 times over recent baselines.

</details>


### [207] [CoInS-Net: A Continuous Position-Aware Network for Joint Medical Image Interpolation and Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09391)
*Yujia Sun,Ningfeng Que,Peiting Shi,Rongrong Fu,Yingying Yang,Xinhang Li,Yin Dai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes CoInS-Net, a continuous position-aware interaction network for joint frame interpolation and lesion segmentation in anisotropic medical volumes, using a shared Swin encoder with continuous spatial coordinate queries, prototype-based task interaction, and a multi-scale task-cooperative decoder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical image interpolation and segmentation are typically performed independently, causing redundant computation and failing to exploit complementary cross-slice structural information. Anisotropic volumes with sparse through-plane sampling suffer from structural discontinuity and boundary blur.

Method: A joint framework with (1) a shared Swin encoder with continuous spatial coordinate queries, (2) a spatially continuous position interpolation module generating target-position features at each scale from relative coordinates and physical spacing, (3) a prototype-based task mutual interaction module exchanging global structure via shared prototypes, and (4) a multi-scale task-cooperative decoder separating shared and task-specific components.

Result: Outperforms conventional single-task schemes on four public medical imaging datasets with diverse modalities and anatomical regions, demonstrating mutual promotion between interpolation and segmentation.

Conclusion: Joint optimization of interpolation and segmentation effectively enables mutual promotion between tasks, providing a reliable and universal technical scheme for intelligent clinical medical image analysis.

Abstract: Accurate medical image interpolation and anatomical structure segmentation are fundamental for computer-aided diagnosis and treatment planning. Anisotropic medical volumes with sparse through-plane sampling often suffer from structural discontinuity and boundary blur, hindering reliable clinical image analysis. Most existing methods implement interpolation and segmentation independently, which introduces redundant computation and fails to fully exploit complementary cross-slice structural information between sequential slices. To address these issues, we propose a continuous position-aware interaction network, termed CoInS-Net, for joint frame interpolation and lesion segmentation. Unlike conventional cascaded interpolation-then-segmentation paradigms, the framework enables bidirectional interaction under a shared Swin encoder with continuous spatial coordinate queries. A spatially continuous position interpolation module generates target-position features at every scale from the relative coordinate and physical spacing, and a prototype-based task mutual interaction module lets the segmentation and interpolation branches exchange global structure through a small set of shared prototypes rather than dense feature mixing. A multi-scale task-cooperative decoder further separates each scale into shared and task-specific components, so the two tasks reinforce common anatomy while preserving their distinct requirements down to the boundary level, without extra annotations. Experiments on four public medical imaging datasets with diverse modalities and anatomical regions demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms conventional single-task schemes. The joint optimization framework effectively realizes mutual promotion between interpolation and segmentation tasks, providing a reliable and universal technical scheme for intelligent clinical medical image analysis.

</details>


### [208] [CableDex: Cable Length Estimation on Industrial Reels Using a Handheld Device](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09392)
*Francisco Guillén,Ricardo Almeida,Bruno Silva,João C. Neves*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CableDex is a mobile-based computer vision system that estimates cable length on industrial reels from a single photo, combining camera calibration, instance segmentation, pose estimation, and volumetric calculation. It achieves 99.5% mAP50 with 5.66ms inference and 4.90% MAPE on 75 reels across five types, within the 10% industrial error tolerance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Manual measurement of cable length on industrial reels is time-consuming and inaccurate, motivating the need for an automated, computer vision-based solution using a single mobile photograph.

Method: The system integrates camera calibration, instance segmentation (trained on 1,000 annotated images), pose estimation, and volumetric calculation to estimate cable length across five reel types and various cable sizes. The pipeline runs end-to-end through a mobile application from label scanning to image capture, segmentation, and length estimation.

Result: Achieves 99.5% mAP50 with 5.66 ms inference time per image, and a MAPE of 4.90% evaluated on 75 reels across five reel types, staying within the 10% industrial error tolerance.

Conclusion: CableDex provides an accurate, fast, and practical automated alternative to manual cable length measurement on industrial reels, meeting industrial accuracy requirements through a fully integrated mobile pipeline.

Abstract: CableDex is a computer vision system that addresses the time-consuming and inaccurate manual measurement of cable length on industrial reels from a single photograph captured with a mobile phone. The system combines camera calibration, instance segmentation, pose estimation, and volumetric calculation to estimate the cable length across five different reel types and various cable sizes. This system is based on an instance segmentation model trained on 1,000 manually annotated images, achieving 99.5\% mAP50 with an inference time of 5.66 ms per image. Evaluated on 75 reels across five reel types, the system achieves a MAPE of 4.90\%, within the 10\% error tolerance commonly accepted in industrial cable-reel measurement. The demonstration presents the end-to-end pipeline, from reel label scanning and image capture to segmentation and length estimation, through the mobile application.

</details>


### [209] [Sign Language Recognition Using Original and Synthetic Depth Image Based Point Cloud Data Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09400)
*Rustem Ozakar,Eyup Gedikli*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates using synthetic depth images generated from RGB images via the Depth Anything V2 network for sign language recognition. Using three sign language datasets (Real-time ASL Fingerspelling, KArSL, AUTSL), point cloud data from both original and synthetic depth images were classified using various PointNet architectures with different data models (frame-based, Point Gesture Map, LSTM). Results show both approaches perform acceptably, with original depth-based models generally better but synthetic depth-based models outperforming in some cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sign language recognition primarily relies on RGB images since depth-based sign language datasets are limited. This scarcity of depth data motivates exploring whether synthetic depth images generated from RGB images can serve as a viable alternative for point cloud-based sign language recognition.

Method: Synthetic depth images were generated from RGB images using the Depth Anything V2 network. Three sign language datasets containing both RGB and depth images were used. Point cloud data was created from both original and synthetic depth images, then classified using various PointNet architectures. Three data modeling approaches were compared: frame-based, Point Gesture Map, and Long Short Term Memory (LSTM) models.

Result: Both original and synthetic depth-based point clouds achieved acceptable performance in most models. Original depth-based point cloud models generally performed better than synthetic ones, though in some specific models the synthetic depth-based models outperformed the originals.

Conclusion: Synthetic depth images generated from RGB images show promise as an alternative for sign language recognition using point clouds, though original depth data still generally provides better classification performance.

Abstract: Research regarding the sign language recognition mostly relies on RGB images, whileas sign language datasets that provide depth images are limited. Point clouds obtained from depth images can be used for sign language recognition with neural networks like PointNet. In recent years, various neural networks are used for generating realistic depth images from monocular RGB images. In this work, synthetic depth images were created from RGB images using Depth Anything V2 network. For this purpose, three sign language datasets (Real-time ASL Fingerspelling, KArSL, AUTSL) which contain both RGB and depth images were used. Classification accuracies of the point cloud data created from both original and synthetic depth images using various PointNet architectures were measured for sign language recognition. From the original and synthetic point clouds, frame based, Point Gesture Map and Long Short Term Memory data models were used for classification and their performances were compared. In the results, both original and synthetic based data achieved acceptable performance in most models. In general, original depth based point cloud models performed better than synthetic ones, however in some models synthetic depth based models performed better than the originals.

</details>


### [210] [One Model to Magnify Them All: Efficient Scale-Invariant Histopathology via Conditional Normalization and Continuous Magnification Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09403)
*Agnieszka Florkowska,Henning Müller,Marek Wodzinski*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The authors propose Conditional Layer Normalization (CLN), a lightweight mechanism that generates affine normalization parameters from input pixel size via a small MLP, integrated into standard CNN architectures for WSI classification and segmentation. This allows models to generalize to arbitrary, previously unseen magnification scales at test time, collapsing a five-model ensemble into a single network and reducing training/inference cost roughly 4-5 times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Whole slide images are acquired at discrete magnification levels but clinical deployment involves continuously varying acquisition magnifications that rarely align with fixed training resolutions. Existing magnification-invariant methods rely on multi-scale architectures at predefined discrete resolutions or costly ensembles of magnification-specific models, leaving intermediate scales poorly covered.

Method: The authors propose Conditional Layer Normalization (CLN), which generates affine normalization parameters from input pixel size via a small MLP. This is integrated into standard CNN architectures for both WSI classification and segmentation. Models are trained on patches sampled continuously across a range of pixel sizes, decoupling inference from scanner-dependent magnification.

Result: On the PANDA prostate cancer dataset, the approach matches or exceeds independently trained single-magnification models on average and ranks among the top three performers at every evaluated magnification, including those unseen during training. It collapses a five-model ensemble into a single network, reducing training and inference cost roughly 4-5 times while leaving multiply-accumulate count unchanged.

Conclusion: Conditional Layer Normalization effectively decouples inference from scanner-dependent magnification, generalizing to arbitrary unseen scales at test time with a single network, substantially reducing computational cost compared to magnification-specific ensembles.

Abstract: Whole slide images (WSIs) in digital histopathology are acquired at discrete magnification levels encoding complementary diagnostic information from global tissue architecture to fine-grained cellular morphology. Yet, deep learning models remain sensitive to scale variation. Existing magnification-invariant methods rely on multi-scale architectures at predefined discrete resolutions, while in clinical deployment the acquisition magnification varies continuously, rarely aligns with a model's fixed training resolution, and intermediate scales are common, so robust coverage otherwise demands a costly ensemble of magnification-specific models. We propose Conditional Layer Normalization (CLN), a lightweight mechanism that generates affine normalization parameters from input pixel size via a small MLP, integrated into standard CNN architectures for both WSI classification and segmentation. Trained on patches sampled continuously across a range of pixel sizes, the model decouples inference from scanner-dependent magnification and generalizes to arbitrary, previously unseen scales at test time. On the PANDA prostate cancer dataset, our approach on average matches or exceeds independently trained single-magnification models and ranks among the top three performers at every evaluated magnification, including those unseen during training. This collapses a five-model ensemble into a single network and reduces training, and inference cost roughly 4-5 times, while leaving the multiply-accumulate count unchanged. The code is available at: https://github.com/aflorkowska/OneModelToMagnifyThemAll.

</details>


### [211] [MeanSR: Restoration Trajectory Learning for One-Step Perceptual Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09405)
*Axi Niu,Jiawei Kou,Kang Zhang,Qingsen Yan,Jinqiu Sun,Yanning Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MeanSR is a one-step perceptual super-resolution method that learns an LR-conditioned average velocity field to directly model the finite-time transition from degraded inputs to high-resolution outputs, avoiding costly distillation while outperforming CTMSR on perceptual metrics with reduced computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing diffusion-based SR methods require costly iterative denoising; one-step distillation approaches reduce inference time but depend on expensive pretrained teachers, while CTMSR avoids distillation but does not explicitly model the restoration dynamics from LR to HR images.

Method: MeanSR learns an LR-conditioned average velocity field to directly capture finite-time transitions from degraded/noisy inputs to plausible HR outputs, reformulates distribution trajectory matching for average-velocity generation, and introduces a Stage-Aware Temporal Sampling strategy to improve trajectory learning.

Result: MeanSR outperforms CTMSR on CLIPIQA, MUSIQ, and MANIQA benchmarks while substantially reducing FLOPs and inference latency, and reconstructs sharper structures and more realistic textures with fewer perceptual artifacts.

Conclusion: MeanSR demonstrates that a distillation-free, one-step perceptual SR approach with explicit restoration dynamics modeling can achieve superior perceptual quality and efficiency compared to existing consistency-training-based methods.

Abstract: Diffusion-based super-resolution (SR) achieves strong perceptual quality but requires costly iterative denoising. Existing one-step distillation methods reduce inference time but depend on expensive pretrained teachers, whereas CTMSR avoids distillation through PF-ODE consistency training yet does not explicitly model the restoration dynamics from low-resolution (LR) inputs to high-resolution (HR) images. We propose MeanSR, a one-step perceptual SR method that learns an LR-conditioned average velocity field to directly capture the finite-time transition from degraded or noisy inputs to plausible HR outputs. We further reformulate distribution trajectory matching for average-velocity generation and introduce a Stage-Aware Temporal Sampling strategy to improve trajectory learning. Experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks show that MeanSR outperforms CTMSR on CLIPIQA, MUSIQ, and MANIQA while substantially reducing FLOPs and inference latency. MeanSR also reconstructs sharper structures and more realistic textures with fewer perceptual artifacts.

</details>


### [212] [Foundation Models are Implicit Deepfake Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09427)
*Stefan Smeu,Dragos-Alexandru Boldisor,Elisabeta Oneata,Dan Oneata*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper reveals that fake media consistently produces lower-magnitude pretrained representations than real media across models, datasets, and domains. This simple feature-magnitude statistic can be used for anomaly-detection-based deepfake detection, is driven primarily by semantic shifts, and improves with larger foundation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While pretrained self-supervised representations are widely used in deepfake detection, it is unclear which properties of these representations make real and fake media distinguishable. The authors aim to uncover the underlying mechanism and exploit it for simpler, more efficient detection.

Method: The authors identify that fake samples systematically produce lower-magnitude representations than real ones across multiple pretrained models and datasets. They formulate deepfake detection as an anomaly detection problem using simple statistics of feature magnitude, and analyze the origin of the effect by separating semantic shifts from low-level generative fingerprints.

Result: Simple feature-magnitude statistics achieve competitive performance compared to more sophisticated deepfake detection methods. The reduced feature magnitude is primarily linked to semantic shifts from fake content rather than low-level generative artifacts, and the discriminative signal strengthens with larger foundation models, yielding stronger zero-shot deepfake detectors.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that a surprisingly simple feature-magnitude signal in pretrained representations is highly effective for deepfake detection, and that advances in representation learning (larger models) naturally lead to stronger zero-shot detectors. This provides both practical benefits and insights into why representations separate real from fake media.

Abstract: Pretrained self-supervised representations have emerged as a core component of current deepfake detection methods, yet it remains unclear which of their properties make real and fake media distinguishable. In this work, we uncover a surprisingly consistent phenomenon: across multiple pretrained models, datasets, and both image and video domains, fake samples systematically produce lower-magnitude representations than their real counterparts. Motivated by this finding, we formulate deepfake detection as an anomaly detection problem and show that simple statistics of feature magnitude achieve competitive performance with far more sophisticated deepfake detection methods. We further investigate the origin of this effect and demonstrate that reduced feature magnitude is primarily associated with semantic shifts introduced by fake content, while low-level generative fingerprints play a comparatively smaller role. Finally, we show that this discriminative signal strengthens as the size of the underlying foundation model grows, suggesting that advances in representation learning naturally translate into stronger zero-shot deepfake detectors.

</details>


### [213] [Unveiling the Secret of AdaLN-Zero in Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09438)
*Jie Zhu,Mingyu Ding,Boqiang Duan,Leye Wang,Jingdong Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates the adaLN-Zero conditioning mechanism in Diffusion Transformers (DiT), identifies zero-initialization as the most influential factor, proposes a novel initialization strategy (adaLN-Gaussian) and an improved conditioning mechanism (SE-adaLN-Zero), validated through extensive experiments on image generation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: DiT is an emerging architecture for image generation with rapid performance improvements, but the understanding of its internal mechanisms remains superficial. The authors seek to demystify why adaLN-Zero outperforms adaLN, and use these insights to further improve the architecture.

Method: The paper (1) decomposes the adaLN-Zero mechanism into three potential elements: SE-like structure, zero-initialization, and gradual update order; (2) identifies zero-initialization as the most influential through analysis; (3) proposes adaLN-Gaussian, an analysis-guided initialization strategy; (4) introduces SE-adaLN-Zero, an improved conditioning mechanism inspired by the SE-like structure.

Result: Extensive experiments following DiT on four datasets, particularly ImageNet1K, demonstrate effectiveness and generalization of both adaLN-Gaussian and SE-adaLN-Zero. The methods also generalize to text-to-image generation beyond class-to-image generation.

Conclusion: The paper provides a deeper understanding of the adaLN-Zero conditioning mechanism, proves zero-initialization as its key factor, and contributes two practical improvements (adaLN-Gaussian and SE-adaLN-Zero) that enhance optimization efficiency and generation performance across multiple datasets and conditioning types.

Abstract: Diffusion transformer (DiT), a rapidly emerging architecture for image generation, has gained much attention. However, despite ongoing efforts to improve its performance, the understanding of DiT remains superficial. In this work, we delve into and investigate a critical conditioning mechanism within DiT, adaLN-Zero, which achieves superior performance compared to adaLN. Our work studies three potential elements driving this performance, including an SE-like structure, zero-initialization, and a "gradual" update order, among which zero-initialization is proved to be the most influential. Building on this understanding, we propose an analysis-guided initialization strategy, termed adaLN-Gaussian, which serves both as an empirical validation of our analysis and as a practical initialization method that consistently improves optimization efficiency. On the other hand, inspired by the SE-like structure, we introduce an improved conditioning mechanism called SE-adaLN-Zero. Extensive experiments following DiT on four datasets, especially on ImageNet1K demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization of adaLN-Gaussian and SE-adaLN-Zero. Beyond class-to-image generation, we also evaluate the generalization of the two improved methods on text-to-image generation.

</details>


### [214] [Sekai2: From World Exploration to Interactive World Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09449)
*Kang He,Wenshuo Peng,Zihui Gao,Jiaming Tan,Kaipeng Zhang,Yongtao Ge*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Sekai2 is a multi-source real-world video dataset of 128,892 clips (2,826 hours) with camera trajectories, hierarchical annotations, and 982 panoramic sequences with loops/revisits, designed for long-horizon video generation, camera control, and interactive world modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video corpora rarely combine long duration, camera trajectories, and temporally grounded text annotations together. Large-scale web video lacks trajectories and time-aligned text, while pose-annotated datasets are short-range or reconstruction-oriented, limiting progress in long-horizon generation and camera control.

Method: The dataset collects world-exploration footage from 10,428 source videos across 113 countries, decomposes them into clips with released camera trajectories, provides hierarchical annotations disentangling subject motion, environment dynamics, static scene content, and camera behavior (649,597 grounded segments), and introduces 982 panoramic sequences with non-linear trajectories, loops, and revisits.

Result: 128,892 clips totaling 2,826 hours; 43,594 segments reach full two minutes (51.4% of footage); 649,597 temporally grounded segments; 982 panoramic sequences with revisits; full pose-and-caption coverage with broad geographic and semantic diversity, varied camera trajectories, and non-redundant temporal descriptions.

Conclusion: Sekai2 provides a scalable resource for long-horizon video generation, camera-controllable synthesis, and interactive world-model pre-training, with revisits enabling supervision for persistent scene representations and geometrically consistent world models.

Abstract: Video world models must capture how scenes evolve over time and across viewpoints. Training them for long-horizon generation and camera control therefore benefits from long videos paired with camera trajectories and temporally grounded semantics. Existing corpora rarely offer the three together: large-scale web video provides broad visual diversity but no trajectories or time-aligned text, while pose-annotated datasets are typically short-range or reconstruction-oriented. We introduce Sekai2, a multi-source real-world video dataset that carries the world-exploration footage of Sekai toward interactive world modeling. The release contains 128,892 clips totaling 2,826 hours from 10,428 source videos across 113 countries or regions, and is deliberately weighted toward sustained observation: under a common 120-second decomposition, 43,594 segments reach the full two minutes and account for 51.4% of all footage. Every clip includes a released camera trajectory and hierarchical annotations disentangling subject motion, environment dynamics, static scene content, and camera behavior, resulting in 649,597 temporally grounded segments. Crucially, we further introduce 982 panoramic sequences captured along non-linear trajectories with loops and revisits. These revisits provide repeated observations of the same locations across time and viewpoints, offering essential supervision for learning persistent scene representations, long-term spatial memory, and geometrically consistent world models. Corpus-scale analyses demonstrate complete pose-and-caption coverage, broad geographic and semantic diversity, varied camera trajectories, and highly non-redundant temporal descriptions. Together, these properties make Sekai2 a scalable resource for long-horizon video generation, camera-controllable synthesis, and interactive world-model pre-training.

</details>


### [215] [A Content-Aware Pure Permutation with Intrinsic Avalanche Effect: Breaking the Diffusion-Permutation Dichotomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09452)
*Zahra Ghoraeian,Mohammad-Reza Sadeghi,Samaneh Mashhadi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Triangular Content-Aware Permutation (TCA) algorithm, which uses Canny edge detection and Delaunay Triangulation to create content-sensitive pixel permutations. TCA achieves differential sensitivity (NPCR=97.10%, UACI=20.06%) through pure pixel relocation alone—something previously assumed impossible—with an average of 14.81 iterations across 50 test images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper challenges the long-held assumption in image processing and cryptography that pixel permutation alone cannot create differential sensitivity (the avalanche effect). Classical permutation methods and even advanced content-aware methods from 2025-2026 maintain near-zero NPCR because changing one pixel merely relocates it rather than producing global changes. The motivation is to prove that permutation alone can achieve significant avalanche effects.

Method: TCA extracts edge points using the Canny edge detector and applies Delaunay Triangulation to both edges and corners, creating a unique geometric partition of the image. Because Delaunay Triangulation is highly sensitive to image geometry, altering a single pixel changes the edge map, which in turn produces a completely different triangulation and thus a globally different permutation pattern. This content-aware, geometry-driven approach is what enables differential sensitivity.

Result: On 50 images, TCA achieves NPCR=97.10% and UACI=20.06% with an average of 14.81 iterations, compared to near-zero NPCR for classical methods. The iteration threshold varies from 6.4 to 30.7 depending on content complexity. Statistical performance confirms low PSNR (11.93 dB) and near-zero correlation (~10^-3). The method is slower than classical approaches due to triangulation cost, but this is a deliberate security-performance trade-off.

Conclusion: Pure pixel permutation can create significant differential sensitivity when the permutation is driven by content-aware geometric analysis rather than dimension-based rules. The non-analytic, content-dependent nature of TCA's permutation pattern makes it well-suited for reference-based encryption, fragile watermarking, and non-blind steganography applications.

Abstract: Pixel permutation is a fundamental tool in image processing, image encryption, and data hiding (including watermarking and steganography) that rearranges pixels without changing their values. A common assumption in the literature is that permutation alone cannot create differential sensitivity; changing one pixel merely relocates that pixel in the output, producing no avalanche effect. This paper challenges this by introducing the Triangular Content-Aware Permutation (TCA) algorithm.
  The method extracts edge points using Canny and applies Delaunay Triangulation to edges and corners, creating a unique partition. Since triangulation is highly sensitive to image geometry, changing a single pixel alters the edge map, resulting in a completely different triangulation and global permutation pattern. Unlike classical dimension-based permutations and advanced content-aware methods (2025-2026), which lack differential sensitivity, TCA increases NPCR from near-zero to 97.10% solely through pixel relocation.
  Experiments on 50 images show that TCA, with an average of 14.81 iterations, achieves NPCR = 97.10% and UACI = 20.06%, proving pure permutation can create significant differential sensitivity. Conventional methods maintain near-zero NPCR. The iteration threshold varies from 6.4 to 30.7 based on content complexity. Low PSNR (11.93 dB) and near-zero correlation (~10^-3) confirm superior statistical performance. Although slower than classical methods due to triangulation, this is a deliberate trade-off for stronger security. Given the non-analytic, content-dependent nature of the pattern, TCA is ideal for reference-based encryption, fragile watermarking, and non-blind steganography.

</details>


### [216] [RecoverFly: A Failure-Aware Reinforcement Learning Post-Training Framework for Aerial Vision-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09467)
*Boxiong Wang,Hui Kang,Geng Sun,Jiahui Li,Chao Yu,Daxin Tian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RecoverFly is a failure-aware reinforcement learning post-training framework for end-to-end UAV vision-language-action policies that uses token-level RL, revisits failure cases for corrective learning, and employs a two-stage long-tail scene curriculum with reference-policy regularization to improve closed-loop navigation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: End-to-end UAV-VLA policies trained with behavior cloning lack corrective supervision for interactive closed-loop execution. While RL offers a solution, it suffers from sample inefficiency, long-tailed scene distributions, and policy distribution shift during optimization.

Method: RecoverFly adapts token-level RL for grammar-constrained autoregressive UAV actions, revisits unresolved failure cases to strengthen corrective learning and sample utilization, and combines a two-stage long-tail scene curriculum with reference-policy regularization for scene adaptation while preserving acquired capabilities.

Result: On the TravelUAV benchmark, RecoverFly achieves best performance on seen, unseen-map, and unseen-object splits, improving success rate by 3.12 to 8.37 percentage points over the AerialVLA initialization with a rollout budget of about 30% of the training-set size.

Conclusion: RecoverFly validates that failure-aware RL post-training is effective, robust, and generalizable for UAV vision-language navigation, addressing key limitations of behavior-cloned policies in closed-loop interactive environments.

Abstract: Unmanned aerial vehicle vision-language navigation (UAV-VLN) requires agents to translate visual observations and language instructions into reliable flight actions in complex environments. Although recent end-to-end UAV vision-language-action (UAV-VLA) policies reduce reliance on separately designed perception, planning, and control modules, their behavior-cloning objectives provide limited corrective supervision for interactive closed-loop execution. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers a promising solution, while its effectiveness is constrained by inefficient use of samples, long-tailed scene distributions, and policy distribution shift during optimization. To this end, we propose RecoverFly, a failure-aware RL post-training framework for end-to-end UAV-VLA policies. Specifically, RecoverFly adapts token-level RL for stable optimization of grammar-constrained autoregressive UAV actions, revisits unresolved failure cases to strengthen corrective learning and sample utilization, and combines a two-stage long-tail scene curriculum with reference-policy regularization to improve scene adaptation while preserving acquired capabilities. Experiments on the TravelUAV benchmark demonstrate that RecoverFly achieves the best performance on the seen, unseen-map, and unseen-object splits. Moreover, compared to the AerialVLA initialization, RecoverFly improves success rate by 3.12 to 8.37 percentage points under a total rollout budget of about 30\% of the training-set size, validating its effectiveness, robustness, and generalization capabilities.

</details>


### [217] [FaLCon: Facet-Anchored Retrieval with Late Consensus for Sim2Real Text-Based Person Anomaly Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09474)
*Hieu Dinh Trung Pham,Phuong Huu Vu Tran,Thuan Duc Mai,Son Nguyen Minh Le,Khang Le Minh,Hoang Vo,Minh-Chi Phung,Huy Minh Nhat Nguyen,Cuong Tuan Nguyen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes an anchor-constrained coarse-to-fine retrieval framework for text-based person anomaly search in a Sim2Real setting, combining global semantic matching with fine-grained verification. The framework achieves 95.41% mAP@10 on the PAB benchmark, significantly outperforming individual retrieval backbones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-based person anomaly search is challenging in Sim2Real settings where models trained on synthetic data must retrieve real-world images, and visually similar candidates differ only in subtle actions, object interactions, or appearance attributes. Applying multimodal LLMs to the entire gallery is computationally prohibitive.

Method: The framework consists of: (1) representing queries via original caption, structured concatenation, and semantic facets; (2) integrating heterogeneous vision-language retrievers through per-query score calibration and soft claim-aware fusion; (3) using full/concatenated captions as anchors for recall preservation while appearance/action/object facets provide corrective evidence; (4) refining candidates with a Qwen3 reranker and two semantic verification modules (anomaly-aware cloze completion and multi-agent evidence reasoning); (5) an uncertainty-gated consensus module for adaptive expert reweighting on ambiguous queries.

Result: On the PAB benchmark, soft claim-aware retrieval achieves 86.44% mAP@10, outperforming individual retrieval backbones. The complete framework achieves 95.41% mAP@10, 94.44% R@1, and 99.09% R@5.

Conclusion: Preserving strong global retrieval while restricting expensive semantic reasoning to a small candidate pool is effective for fine-grained Sim2Real person anomaly search. Code will be released on GitHub.

Abstract: Text-based person anomaly search requires retrieving real-world pedestrian images from detailed natural-language descriptions using models trained primarily on synthetic data. This Sim2Real setting is particularly challenging because visually similar candidates may differ only in subtle actions, object interactions, or appearance attributes, while applying multimodal large language models to the entire gallery is computationally expensive. We propose an anchor-constrained coarse-to-fine retrieval framework that combines global semantic matching with fine-grained verification. First, each query is represented by its original caption, a structured concatenation, and several semantic facets. Heterogeneous vision-language retrievers are then integrated through robust per-query score calibration and soft claim-aware fusion. Full and concatenated captions serve as anchors to preserve candidate recall, whereas appearance, action, and object facets provide bounded corrective evidence. The resulting candidate pool is further refined by a discriminative Qwen3 reranker and two complementary semantic verification modules based on anomaly-aware cloze completion and multi-agent evidence reasoning. Finally, an uncertainty-gated consensus module adaptively reweights the three experts on ambiguous queries. Experiments on the PAB benchmark show that the proposed soft claim-aware retrieval achieves 86.44% mAP@10, substantially outperforming individual retrieval backbones. The complete framework further improves performance to 95.41% mAP@10, 94.44% R@1, and 99.09% R@5. These results demonstrate that preserving strong global retrieval while restricting expensive semantic reasoning to a small candidate pool is effective for fine-grained Sim2Real person anomaly search. Our code will be available on Github.

</details>


### [218] [Agreement-Based Audio-Visual Segmentation:Champion Report for the MeViS-Audio Track in the 8th LSVOS Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09475)
*Yiwen Ren,Jianing Liu,Yingxin Wang,Kexin Zhang,Licheng Jiao,Lingling Li,Xu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a staged solution for the MeViS-Audio track that segments objects described by spoken motion expressions in video. The system uses Qwen3-ASR for speech-to-text, generates multiple mask tracks from complementary grounding and segmentation models, selects the best track via average mask agreement voting, applies correction rules for direction/count/plural queries, and uses a video-level classifier to determine target presence. The system achieved first place with 0.5952 J&F and a final score of 0.769589.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The MeViS-Audio task requires not only segmenting objects described by spoken motion expressions but also handling cases where the described target is absent (returning empty masks). A single model prediction is unreliable, so a more robust approach is needed to handle diverse motion expressions and absence detection.

Method: The solution is a staged pipeline: (1) Qwen3-ASR converts speech to text; (2) multiple complementary grounding and segmentation models generate several mask tracks; (3) the track with the highest average mask agreement with other candidates is selected; (4) explicit rules for direction, count, and plural queries correct special cases; (5) a video-level classifier combining visual, audio-visual, and within-video query scores decides target presence.

Result: The system achieved 0.5952 J&F, 0.7931 no-target accuracy, 0.9205 target accuracy, and a final score of 0.769589. The system ranked first in the MeViS-Audio track.

Conclusion: A simple staged approach with multiple complementary models, mask agreement-based selection, rule-based corrections, and absence detection can outperform more complex single-model solutions for audio-guided video object segmentation.

Abstract: The MeViS-Audio track asks a system to segment the objects described by a spoken motion expression throughout a video and to return empty masks when the described target is absent. We present a simple staged solution. Qwen3-ASR first converts speech into text. Several video mask tracks are then produced with complementary grounding and segmentation models. Instead of trusting a single prediction, we select the track that has the highest average mask agreement with the other candidates. A small set of explicit direction, count, and plural rules corrects queries that require more than ordinary single-object tracking. Finally, a video-level classifier combines visual, audio-visual, and within-video query scores to decide whether any target is present. The submitted system obtains 0.5952 J &F, 0.7931 no-target accuracy, 0.9205 target accuracy, and a final score of 0.769589. The challenge organizers notified our team that this result ranked first in the track.

</details>


### [219] [Beyond Uniform Restoration: Empowering All-in-One Restoration with Pixel-Level Multimodal Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09482)
*Chunxiao Liu,Wei Liu,Anbin Xiong,Erli Meng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MGN-AIR is a novel pixel-level all-in-one image restoration framework that estimates pixel-level visual prompts and combines textual and visual prompts to provide global and local degradation cues, enabling fine-grained restoration across diverse corruption types and severity levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing all-in-one image restoration methods apply a uniform restoration strategy across the entire image, ignoring that different regions may suffer from distinct degradation types and varying severity. This motivates the need for pixel-level, fine-grained restoration control.

Method: The approach first learns to estimate a pixel-level visual prompt, then leverages both textual and visual prompts to provide global and local degradation cues, guiding the model on where to look and how to restore at each pixel.

Result: Extensive experiments on multiple all-in-one image restoration benchmarks covering denoising, deraining, deblurring, dehazing, desnowing, and low-light enhancement demonstrate that the proposed MGN-AIR consistently and significantly outperforms existing approaches.

Conclusion: Pixel-level restoration with multimodal prompts (textual and visual) enables more fine-grained and precise control, leading to state-of-the-art performance on all-in-one image restoration tasks.

Abstract: All-in-one image restoration is a unified low-level vision task that aims to effectively recover high-quality images from inputs degraded by various types and levels of corruption using a single model. Recent works have achieved remarkable progress by learning degradation-adaptive prompts or network architectures. However, these methods typically apply a uniform restoration strategy across the entire image, neglecting the fact that different regions may suffer from distinct degradation types and varying degrees of severity. In contrast, we propose to perform restoration at the pixel level, thereby enabling more fine-grained and precise control over the restoration process. Specifically, we present MGN-AIR, a novel pixel-level restoration framework for all-in-one image restoration. Our approach first learns to estimate a pixel-level visual prompt. Then, it leverages both textual and visual prompts to provide global and local degradation cues, guiding the model on where to look and how to restore at each pixel. We conduct extensive experiments on multiple all-in-one image restoration benchmarks, covering a wide range of tasks including denoising, deraining, deblurring, dehazing, desnowing, and low-light enhancement. Experimental results demonstrate that our proposed method consistently and significantly outperforms existing approaches.

</details>


### [220] [GeoRoute: Geometry-Aware Hybrid Inference for Traffic Future-Frame Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09493)
*Khang Minh Le,Hieu Dinh Trung Pham,Luu Thanh Danh,Nam-Tien Le,Hieu Anh Ngo,Phuong Huu Vu Tran,Son Nguyen Minh Le,Nguyen Trong Nghia,Tu Tran Thi Cam,Huy Minh Nhat Nguyen,Cuong Tuan Nguyen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free inference framework that stabilizes static geometry in latent video diffusion predictions for traffic scenes by using multi-frame depth-layered rendering and view-conditioned routing, achieving competitive performance on the AI City Challenge Track 5 benchmark without retraining the underlying video model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long-horizon future-frame prediction for traffic scenes remains challenging due to temporal ghosting, geometry drift, and inconsistent object motion. Directly applying latent video diffusion models to structured traffic scenes leads to unstable geometry and degraded temporal coherence over extended horizons.

Method: A training-free inference framework with two components: (1) For front-camera videos, multi-frame depth-layered rendering projects static geometry from observed history frames while preserving dynamic regions from the generative base model; (2) For heterogeneous traffic views, a frozen vision-language model infers coarse camera groups and selects specialized motion-based predictors. The framework requires no retraining or fine-tuning.

Result: Validated on the AI City Challenge Track 5 benchmark, achieving competitive performance among top-ranked teams.

Conclusion: Geometry-aware inference-time refinement and view-conditioned hybrid inference can improve static-geometry stability and low-level structural fidelity without changing the original model architecture.

Abstract: Long-horizon future-frame prediction is important for autonomous driving, traffic surveillance, and intelligent transportation systems, yet remains challenging due to temporal ghosting, geometry drift, and inconsistent object motion. Recent latent video diffusion models have achieved impressive visual quality, but directly applying them to structured traffic scenes often leads to unstable geometry and degraded temporal coherence over extended horizons. We present a training-free inference framework that stabilizes reliable static structure in pretrained video predictions through multi-frame temporal context and view-conditioned routing. For front-camera videos, our method refines generated futures with a multi-frame depth-layered renderer that projects static geometry from observed history frames while preserving dynamic regions from the generative base model. For heterogeneous traffic views, a frozen vision-language model infers a coarse camera group from the observed clip and selects a specialized motion-based predictor. The framework requires neither retraining nor fine-tuning of the underlying video model and can be applied directly to pretrained generators. We validate the proposed framework on the AI City Challenge Track 5 benchmark, where our final system achieves competitive performance among the top-ranked teams. These results demonstrate that geometry-aware inference-time refinement and view-conditioned hybrid inference can improve static-geometry stability and low-level structural fidelity without changing the original model architecture.

</details>


### [221] [SwissCrop25: A National Multi-Year Benchmark for Operational Crop Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09497)
*Thomas Lauber,Mehmet Ozgur Turkoglu,Sélène Ledain,Helge Aasen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SwissCrop25 is a national-scale crop mapping benchmark covering seven growing seasons (2019-2025) with Sentinel-2 time series, temperature data, a fine-grained 73-class crop taxonomy, and 5 non-crop classes. It introduces a leave-one-year-out protocol for joint cropland delineation and crop classification, revealing that domain-specific temporal models (TSViT) outperform a foundation model (Galileo), with TSViT achieving the best overall performance and a 12 pp macro-mIoU advantage over U-TAE. The dataset also exposes interannual distribution shifts and a seasonality trade-off between models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing crop mapping datasets evaluate model generalisation across years, fine-grained taxonomy resolution, and cropland versus non-cropland discrimination only in isolation, failing to reflect realistic operational conditions where these requirements must be met simultaneously.

Method: The authors construct SwissCrop25 from Sentinel-2 time series and daily temperature observations over 2019-2025, defining a 73-class crop taxonomy with grassland management types and 5 non-crop classes. They propose a leave-one-year-out evaluation protocol for joint cropland delineation and crop classification, benchmarking U-TAE, TSViT, and Galileo models, including in-season and interannual robustness analyses.

Result: TSViT achieves the best overall performance with a 12 pp macro-mIoU advantage over U-TAE. Domain-specific models outperform the Galileo foundation model. SwissCrop25 reveals substantial interannual distribution shifts, temperature-derived phenology improves robustness, and in-season evaluation shows U-TAE performs better early while TSViT gains advantage later through improved rare-class discrimination.

Conclusion: SwissCrop25 serves as a challenging public benchmark for evaluating crop mapping systems under realistic operational conditions, revealing architecture differences hidden by conventional benchmarks and providing a testbed for future research on cross-year generalisation and fine-grained crop mapping.

Abstract: Operational crop mapping requires models that generalise across years, resolve fine-grained crop taxonomies, and distinguish cropland from surrounding landscapes. However, existing crop mapping datasets enable evaluation of these requirements only in isolation. We therefore introduce SwissCrop25, a national-scale crop mapping benchmark dataset spanning seven growing seasons (2019-2025). SwissCrop25 combines Sentinel-2 time series, daily temperature observations, a fine-grained 73 crop taxonomy including grassland management types, and 5 explicit non-crop land cover classes. To evaluate realistic deployment conditions, we define a leave-one-year-out protocol with joint cropland delineation and crop classification for benchmarking representative crop mapping architectures. Evaluating U-TAE (convolutional temporal-attention model), TSViT (transformer-based spatio-temporal model), and Galileo (EO foundation model) reveals differences between architectures hidden by conventional benchmarks. In this setting, domain-specific models outperform Galileo, with TSViT achieving the best overall performance and a 12 pp macro-mIoU advantage over U-TAE. SwissCrop25 also exposes substantial interannual distribution shifts and shows that incorporating temperature-derived phenological information improves robustness. Finally, in-season evaluation reveals a trade-off between models, with U-TAE performing better early in the season and TSViT gaining an advantage later through improved rare-class discrimination. SwissCrop25 provides a challenging testbed for evaluating crop mapping systems under realistic operational conditions and is publicly released at https://huggingface.co/datasets/EOA-team/SwissCrop25 .

</details>


### [222] [XFeat Revisited: Reproducibility and Evaluation of a Lightweight Image Matcher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09519)
*Lazar Đoković,Aimee Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a reproducibility study of XFeat, a lightweight local feature extractor and matcher. The authors re-implemented the architecture from the paper, re-evaluated the original checkpoint alongside their re-implementation, conducted ablations on under-justified design choices, reproduced downstream evaluations, and extended testing to zero-shot out-of-distribution and cross-modal matching scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The original XFeat paper, supplement, and public code differ in several implementation details (backbone layout, fusion block, training losses), and certain architectural design choices were not fully justified. This study aims to clarify these discrepancies, verify reproducibility, and provide a nuanced assessment of the original claims.

Method: The authors re-implement XFeat based on the paper and supplementary material, re-evaluate the authors' released checkpoint alongside their own re-implementation, perform architectural ablations on the parallel keypoint branch and skip-connection placement, reproduce downstream evaluations (homography estimation and Aachen visual localization), and extend testing to zero-shot out-of-distribution and cross-modal matching (retinal, thermal-visible, remote-sensing imagery).

Result: Reproduced models closely match and sometimes outperform the original checkpoint on MegaDepth-1500 and ScanNet-1500. The parallel keypoint branch is important for semi-dense matching but less beneficial than originally claimed; evidence for the specific skip-connection placement is inconclusive. Homography estimation reproduced closely, while Aachen visual localization fell below reported results even for the released checkpoint. XFeat remains effective in some cross-modal settings but degrades sharply under severe modality shifts.

Conclusion: The main claim of XFeat's strong accuracy-efficiency trade-off is supported, but several architectural justifications are weaker than originally stated, and performance can be sensitive to underspecified evaluation details and severe modality shifts.

Abstract: We present a reproducibility study of XFeat, a lightweight local feature extractor and matcher designed to identify corresponding points across images efficiently on resource-constrained hardware. We re-implement the architecture based on the paper and supplementary material, re-evaluate the authors' released checkpoint alongside our re-implementation, and conduct additional architectural ablations to examine design choices that were not fully justified in the original work. This distinction between re-evaluation and reproduction is important, as the paper, supplement, and public code differ in several implementation details, including the backbone layout, fusion block, and training losses. Empirically, our reproduced models closely match and, in some cases, outperform the re-evaluated original checkpoint on MegaDepth-1500 and ScanNet-1500, supporting the main claim that XFeat provides a strong accuracy-efficiency trade-off for standard image-matching benchmarks. Our ablations provide a more nuanced view of two architectural arguments from the original paper. In particular, the parallel keypoint branch is important for semi-dense matching, but its benefit is less pronounced than originally claimed, while the evidence for the specific placement of the single skip-connection remains inconclusive. Finally, we reproduce the original downstream evaluations and find close agreement for homography estimation, while Aachen visual localization remains below the reported results, even for the released checkpoint, suggesting sensitivity to underspecified evaluation details. We then extend the analysis to zero-shot out-of-distribution and cross-modal matching across retinal, thermal-visible, and multimodal remote-sensing imagery, where XFeat remains effective in some settings but degrades sharply under severe modality shifts.

</details>


### [223] [A Height-Constrained 2-Point Minimal Solver for Pose Estimation from Active LED Markers with Event Cameras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09520)
*Runze Yuan,Alexander Kappler,Jun Zhang,Kuangyi Chen,Fabio Morbidi,Pascal Vasseur,Cédric Demonceaux,Friedrich Fraundorfer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper derives a minimal solver for camera pose estimation using only two LED markers with event cameras, incorporating known tilt angle and camera height from onboard sensors (IMU/altimeter). The method provides both closed-form and linear least-squares solutions, analyzes degenerate configurations, and demonstrates improved accuracy over state-of-the-art P2P solvers and competitive performance with P3P.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing event-camera-based pose estimation methods with active LED markers rely on PnP solvers that typically require three or more markers, and structured marker layouts are difficult to deploy in space-constrained scenarios. Meanwhile, partial self-motion information (gravity direction and altitude) is readily available from onboard sensors but underutilized.

Method: The authors formulate a minimal pose estimation problem using only two LED markers, incorporating known tilt angle (gravity direction) and camera height from onboard sensors. They derive both a closed-form solution and a linear least-squares solution for uniquely determining camera pose, and analyze degenerate configurations to characterize when height information does not contribute to rotation estimation.

Result: Experiments on synthetic and real data (collected with an event-based active marker system and motion capture ground truth) demonstrate improved accuracy over the state-of-the-art P2P solver and competitive performance relative to P3P.

Conclusion: The proposed two-marker minimal solver leverages onboard sensor information to achieve robust and accurate pose estimation, reducing marker requirements while maintaining performance comparable to three-point methods.

Abstract: In many autonomous applications requiring real-time localization, active marker-based systems are preferred due to their low latency and ease of deployment compared to computationally demanding feature-based methods. Event~\mbox{cameras} offer high temporal resolution and minimal delay and are commonly used with active LED markers for robust real-time localization. Existing methods typically rely on Perspective-n-Point (PnP) solvers for pose estimation. However, structured marker layouts can be challenging to deploy in space-constrained scenarios, while partial self-motion information (e.g., gravity direction and altitude) is readily available from onboard sensors. We derive a robust and accurate minimal solver that estimates camera pose from only two LED markers by incorporating known tilt angle and camera height measured by an onboard sensor, such as an IMU or an altimeter. The proposed formulation uniquely determines the camera pose through both a closed-form and a linear least-squares solution. We further analyze degenerate configurations and characterize the conditions under which height information does not contribute to rotation estimation. For evaluation, we developed an event-based active marker system to collect real-world data with ground truth from a motion capture system. Experiments on both synthetic and real data demonstrate improved accuracy over the state-of-the-art P2P solver and competitive performance relative to P3P.

</details>


### [224] [TriView-YOLO: Early Multi-View Fusion for Ground Penetrating Radar Cavity Detection in Soft, High-Water-Content Soils](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09522)
*Suphawut Thawinutchokaudom,Sompote Youwai,Warat Kongkitkul,Mitsumasa Yamashina,Jose M. D. S. Rodrigues Neto,Jun Shinohara*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TriView-YOLO is a multi-view YOLOv12 detector for road cavity screening using GPR in soft, high-water-content ground. It fuses three co-registered views (longitudinal B-scan, horizontal C-scan, cross-section B-scan) into a 9-channel input via a TripleInputConv layer. Trained on 1,600 field samples from Bangkok and Japan, it achieves mAP50 of 0.558 on a Bangkok-only test set over soft marine clay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Detecting subsurface cavities from GPR is most difficult in soft, high-water-content ground where conductive water-saturated soil attenuates signals and degrades cavity reflections, yet this is precisely the condition where cavities form most readily. No dedicated deep learning cavity-detection evaluation has been reported for this ground condition.

Method: TriView-YOLO replaces the YOLOv12 stem with a TripleInputConv layer that fuses three co-registered views (longitudinal B-scan, horizontal C-scan, and cross-section B-scan) into a 9-channel input. The rest of the network is unchanged, and bounding boxes are required only on the longitudinal view. Training used 1,600 expert-verified field samples from Bangkok (principal) and Japan (training/validation only), collected with a vehicle-mounted multichannel 3D GPR system.

Result: On the unaugmented Bangkok test set over soft marine clay (80-140% water content, water table at 1-2m), the model attains mAP50 of 0.558 +/- 0.028 over three seeds at 23.6 GFLOPs and 3.1 ms per image. Ablations show auxiliary views help (removing them lowers mAP50 and recall), while public/synthetic images, DINOv3 features, larger models, and COCO pretraining provide no benefit.

Conclusion: The multi-view fusion approach is effective for GPR cavity detection in challenging soft, water-saturated ground conditions, while the ablation results suggest that sophisticated additions (larger models, fancy features, external pretraining) do not help and field data are what matter most.

Abstract: Automated detection of subsurface cavities from Ground Penetrating Radar (GPR) is most difficult in soft, high-water-content ground, where conductive, water-saturated soil attenuates the signal and degrades cavity reflections, yet this is also the condition under which cavities most readily form. This paper proposes TriView-YOLO, a multi-view YOLOv12 detector for road cavity screening in such ground. Three co-registered views (longitudinal B-scan, horizontal C-scan, and cross-section B-scan) form a 9-channel input fused by a TripleInputConv layer that replaces the YOLOv12 stem; the rest of the network is unchanged, and bounding boxes are required on the longitudinal view only. Training used 1,600 expert-verified field samples, principally metropolitan road surveys of Bangkok, Thailand, acquired with a vehicle-mounted multichannel three-dimensional GPR mobile mapping system, with surveys over the firmer subgrades of Japan added to training and validation only. The test set comes exclusively from the Bangkok surveys, over soft marine clay with 80-140% water content and a water table at 1-2 m depth, a ground condition for which no dedicated deep learning cavity-detection evaluation has been reported. On this unaugmented, field-only test set, split randomly within surveys, the proposed model attains mAP50 of 0.558 +/- 0.028 over three seeds at 23.6 GFLOPs and 3.1 ms per image. Ablations show that removing the auxiliary views lowers mAP50 and recall, whereas public and synthetic training images, DINOv3 features, larger model scale, and COCO pretraining bring no gain.

</details>


### [225] [Towards Expressive and Faithful Audio-to-Image Generation: A Unified Multimodal Dataset and Synthesis Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09529)
*Dongxu Ge,Shansong Liu,Cheng Gong,Xiao-Lei Zhang,Chi Zhang,Xuelong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces A2I-Set, a large-scale tri-modal dataset (323K audio-image-text pairs) and AudioCanvas, a model fine-tuned on it for audio-to-image generation, achieving superior visual quality and cross-modal alignment over existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Audio-to-image generation is limited by traditional datasets lacking high-fidelity images and precise cross-modal alignment, which prevents fine-tuning strong T2I models for high-quality A2I generation.

Method: The authors construct A2I-Set, a unified tri-modal dataset of 323K paired audio, images, and detailed text captions; develop a human-supervised mixed-source test set; and propose AudioCanvas, an A2I model fine-tuned on this dataset.

Result: AudioCanvas achieves more visually expressive outputs and better cross-modal alignment, generally outperforming existing A2I approaches.

Conclusion: The proposed dataset and model advance audio-to-image generation by addressing data quality and alignment gaps, with public releases of dataset and code for future research.

Abstract: As an important subfield of cross-modal generation, synthesizing static visual content in the form of images from audio, namely audio-to-image (A2I) generation, has attracted increasing research attention in recent years. Nevertheless, despite the remarkable visual quality of modern text-to-image (T2I) models, the performance of A2I remains fundamentally limited by traditional datasets, which often lack both high-fidelity images and precise cross-modal alignment. As a result, existing methods still struggle to achieve high-quality audio-to-image generation through finetuning strong T2I models, thereby constraining practical applications in this area. Motivated by this gap, we introduce A2I-Set, a unified, high-quality tri-modal dataset consisting of 323K paired audio, images, and detailed text captions, specifically designed for audio-visual research, including audio-conditioned image generation. Besides, we developed a new mixed-source test set for the A2I task through human supervision. We further propose an A2I model, AudioCanvas, fine-tuned on our A2I-Set. Experiments show that AudioCanvas achieves more visually expressive as well as cross-modal alignment results that generally outperforming existing approaches. Our dataset and source code are available at https://github.com/gdx012/A2I-Generation.

</details>


### [226] [DocPure: Prompt-Free Unified Document Restoration via Degradation-Aware Structure-Guided Wavelet Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09536)
*Lingming Su,Wanglong Lu,Tao Wang,Kaihao Zhang,Nan Zhang,Liyan An,Hanli Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DocPure is a prompt-free unified framework for degradation-aware document image restoration that handles multiple degradations (deblurring, denoising, compression artifact reduction, deshadowing) using a degradation-aware structure auto-encoder with routing regularization and a structure-guided wavelet interaction mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Document images suffer from diverse degradations during acquisition and transmission. Existing unified restoration methods struggle with training multiple degradation-specific models, relying on manual task-specific prompts, or requiring cross-task data pairing. A prompt-free unified solution is needed.

Method: DocPure consists of: (1) a degradation-aware structure auto-encoder with degradation-informed routing regularization that predicts clean structural priors, using degradation labels only as auxiliary supervision during training (prompt-free at inference); (2) a structure-guided wavelet interaction mechanism that bridges frequency-domain features and spatial semantics, with cross-frequency adaptive modulation using low-frequency sub-bands to modulate high-frequency recovery for structural consistency.

Result: Extensive experiments show DocPure achieves strong performance compared with state-of-the-art methods across multiple document restoration tasks including deblurring, denoising, compression artifact reduction, and deshadowing.

Conclusion: DocPure provides an effective prompt-free unified solution for degradation-aware document restoration, overcoming limitations of prior approaches while achieving competitive performance across diverse degradation types.

Abstract: High-quality document images are pivotal for information archiving and downstream automatic processing. However, they are frequently compromised by diverse degradations during uncontrolled acquisition and transmission. While unified document restoration techniques have been proposed to restore images from multiple degradations, they often struggle with training multiple degradation-specific models, reliance on manual task-specific prompts, or cross-task data pairing. To address these limitations, we propose DocPure, a prompt-free unified framework that achieves degradation-aware document restoration.
  We design a degradation-aware structure auto-encoder with degradation-informed routing regularization to predict clean structural priors from degraded inputs. The model is prompt-free at inference, and degradation labels are only used as auxiliary supervision for the routing regularization during training.
  Furthermore, we introduce a structure-guided wavelet interaction mechanism to bridge frequency-domain features and spatial semantics. Within the structure-guided wavelet interaction mechanism, a cross-frequency adaptive modulation utilizes low-frequency sub-bands to modulate high-frequency recovery, ensuring structural consistency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DocPure achieves strong performance compared with state-of-the-art methods across various tasks, including deblurring, denoising, compression artifact reduction, and deshadowing.

</details>


### [227] [Towards Collaborative Joint Perception and Prediction: Framework, Baseline Evaluation, and Deployment Perspectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09541)
*Lei Wan,Hannan Ejaz Keen,Alexey Vinel*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper extends collaborative perception to a new paradigm called Collaborative Joint Perception and Prediction (Co-P&P), which unifies perception with motion prediction to address perception error accumulation and occlusions. It compares fusion strategies (finding prediction-level fusion inferior to detection/tracking-level fusion), establishes baselines for modular design, and implements an end-to-end prototype using RENO neural codec and FutureDet that shows collaboration improves forecasting while neural compression achieves ~34x bandwidth reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CAVs using V2X communication enable collaborative perception, but persistent challenges remain: accumulated perception errors and visual occlusions degrade motion prediction of surrounding road users in dynamic traffic environments. The authors aim to unify CP with prediction to overcome these issues.

Method: Presents a conceptual Co-P&P framework; compares fusion strategies (prediction-level, detection-level, tracking-level); establishes baseline performance for modular perception-prediction design; implements a minimal end-to-end prototype coupling collaborative point-cloud sharing via RENO neural codec with joint detection-forecasting via FutureDet.

Result: Prediction-level fusion degrades overall system performance compared to detection-level or tracking-level fusion. The end-to-end prototype shows collaboration improves forecasting accuracy, and neural compression preserves these benefits at roughly 34x lower communication bandwidth.

Conclusion: Co-P&P is a viable paradigm that improves situational awareness and motion prediction; detection/tracking-level fusion are superior to prediction-level fusion; collaborative forecasting benefits can be retained with substantial bandwidth savings through neural compression.

Abstract: Connected Autonomous Vehicles (CAVs) increasingly exploit Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) communication to exchange multi-source sensor information, enabling advanced Collaborative Perception (CP) capabilities. Extending beyond these capabilities, this work focuses on Collaborative Joint Perception and Prediction (Co-P&P), a paradigm that unifies CP with motion prediction to mitigate two persistent challenges: the accumulation of perception errors and visual occlusions. We present a conceptual framework for Collaborative Joint Perception and Prediction (Co-P&P) that improves motion prediction of surrounding road users, thereby enhancing situational awareness in complex and dynamic traffic environments. Building upon our preliminary study, this extended version compares the performance of different fusion strategies and establishes baseline performance for a modular design of perception and prediction. Experimental results show that prediction-level fusion leads to a decline in overall system performance compared to detection-level or tracking-level fusion. We further implement a minimal end-to-end Co-P&P prototype that couples collaborative point-cloud sharing via the RENO neural codec with joint detection-forecasting via FutureDet, showing that collaboration improves forecasting accuracy while neural compression preserves this benefit at roughly 34x lower communication bandwidth.

</details>


### [228] [PressureMesh: 3D Human Mesh Estimation from Multi-Device Pressure Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09550)
*Changhai Ma,Ziyu Wu,Yunkang Zhang,Fangting Xie,Mengting Niu,Heyu Ding,Quan Wan,Jiayue Yuan,Boyan Liu,Yi Ke,Xiaohui Cai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MDP-Net is an end-to-end network that estimates human meshes from temporal pressure data across multiple devices, using a Mixture of Experts-based multimodal fusion mechanism, supported by a new multi-device temporal pressure dataset (MDP) with pose labels including 2D/3D joints and human meshes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing pressure-based human pose monitoring methods are limited to a single device, which restricts the effective monitoring range for applications like rehabilitation assessment and human-computer interaction.

Method: Proposes MDP-Net, an end-to-end network that directly estimates human meshes from temporal pressure data across multiple devices, with a multimodal fusion mechanism inspired by Mixture of Experts (MoE) to achieve effective complementarity and enhancement of cross-device pressure information. Also constructs the MDP dataset, a high-quality multi-device temporal pressure dataset with pose labels including 2D/3D joints and human meshes.

Result: MDP-Net achieves a joint position error of 12.6 cm on the MDP dataset.

Conclusion: Fusing multi-device pressure information is an effective and promising new solution for daily human pose monitoring.

Abstract: Human pose monitoring is crucial in fields such as rehabilitation assessment and human-computer interaction. Due to its privacy-preserving nature, pressure-based human pose monitoring has become a primary approach for unobtrusive sensing. However, existing methods are generally limited to a single device, which restricts the effective monitoring range. To address this limitation, we propose MDP-Net, an end-to-end network capable of directly estimating human meshes from temporal pressure data across multiple devices. We introduce a multimodal fusion mechanism inspired by the Mixture of Experts (MoE) framework to achieve effective complementarity and enhancement of cross-device pressure information. To support the training and evaluation of MDP-Net, we constructed MDP, a high-quality multi-device temporal pressure dataset that includes various pose labels such as 2D/3D joints and human meshes. Experimental results demonstrate that MDP-Net achieves a joint position error of 12.6 cm on the MDP dataset. These results prove that fusing multi-device pressure information is an effective and promising new solution for daily human pose monitoring.

</details>
