### [229] [From Semantic Grounding to Decision Optimization: A Unified Framework for Long-Horizon UAV Vision-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09564)
*Zeyuan Ma,Jiaxin Chen,Di Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified semantic-to-decision framework for UAV vision-language navigation (UAV-VLN) that addresses three coupled challenges: weak instruction-grounding of landmarks, insufficient exploitation of long-horizon history, and unstable decisions under local traps. The method comprises an instruction-grounded semantic enhancement module, a relevance-aware dynamic temporal aggregation strategy, and a topology-aware decision method, achieving state-of-the-art performance on AerialVLN and OpenFly benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current UAV-VLN approaches suffer from three coupled issues: (1) weak grounding of instruction-relevant landmarks in visual observations, (2) insufficient exploitation of long-horizon history, and (3) unstable decisions under local traps or repeated exploration.

Method: The proposed unified semantic-to-decision framework includes: (1) an instruction-grounded semantic enhancement module that injects object-level semantics and relative spatial cues into the current observation state; (2) a relevance-aware dynamic temporal aggregation strategy that reweights the full history buffer and converts high-relevance frames into structured landmark prompts for the decoder; (3) a topology-aware decision method combining local-optimum cognition with group-relative policy optimization under progress, goal, semantic, and path-compliance rewards.

Result: Experiments on the AerialVLN and OpenFly benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.

Conclusion: The unified semantic-to-decision framework effectively resolves the coupled grounding, history exploitation, and decision stability issues in UAV-VLN, as evidenced by superior benchmark results.

Abstract: UAV vision-language navigation (UAV-VLN) focuses on enabling an aerial agent to follow natural-language instructions in open 3D environments from egocentric visual observations. Current approaches suffer from three coupled issues: weak grounding of instruction-relevant landmarks in visual observations, insufficient exploitation of long-horizon history, and unstable decisions under local traps or repeated exploration. To address these issues, we propose a unified semantic-to-decision framework. First, we present an instruction-grounded semantic enhancement module that injects object-level semantics and relative spatial cues into the current observation state. Subsequently, we develop a relevance-aware dynamic temporal aggregation strategy that reweights the full history buffer while converting a few high-relevance frames into structured landmark prompts for the decoder. Finally, we devise a topology-aware decision method that combines local-optimum cognition with group-relative policy optimization under progress, goal, semantic, and path-compliance rewards. Experiments on the widely used AerialVLN and OpenFly benchmarks clearly demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance.

</details>


### [230] [VideoVIBE: A Video-Grounded Diagnostic Benchmark for One-Shot Interactive Website Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09573)
*Jiajun Xu,Yanghao Zhou,Jingyun Liao,Yu Bai,Jinxing Zhou,Chengliang Liu,Changsen Yuan,Bo Wang,Qian Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces VideoVIBE, a video-grounded benchmark with ~1.7K diagnostic Video QA instances derived from 6,338 verified failures in generated webpages, spanning four failure types. It also proposes V2Lens, a training-free multi-agent system that refines initial video-based diagnoses through targeted verification. V2Lens improves over the strongest standalone model (Gemini-2.5-Flash, 64.54) by 7.18 points to reach 71.72.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing evaluations of vibe-coded web apps score isolated artifacts or final outcomes, offering limited evidence about which failures occur and why. There is a need for behaviorally faithful and diagnostically informative assessment of generated application quality.

Method: The paper builds VideoVIBE, transforming human-operated webpage recordings into fine-grained diagnostic tasks across semantic-logical, visual-motion, structural-temporal, and functional failure categories. It also proposes V2Lens, a training-free multi-agent system that challenges and selectively refines video-based diagnoses using targeted visual and source-code verification.

Result: Across thirteen Video MLLMs, Gemini-2.5-Flash is the strongest standalone model at 64.54, while V2Lens achieves 71.72, a 7.18-point improvement.

Conclusion: Video-grounded evaluation can move beyond isolated artifacts and aggregate outcomes toward a behaviorally faithful and diagnostically informative account of generated application quality.

Abstract: Natural-language-driven "vibe coding" enables the one-shot generation of visually rich and interactive web applications, yet reliable assessment of their quality has not kept pace. Existing evaluations often score isolated artifacts or final task outcomes, offering limited evidence about which failures occur and why. We introduce VideoVIBE, a video-grounded benchmark that transforms human-operated webpage recordings into fine-grained diagnostic tasks. It contains approximately 1.7K diagnostic Video QA instances derived from 6,338 verified failures across generated webpages, spanning semantic-logical, visual-motion, structural-temporal, and functional failures. Diagnoses are grounded primarily in recorded presentation and behavior, with webpage source code used as complementary context. We further propose V2Lens, a training-free, evidence-grounded multi-agent system that challenges and selectively refines initial video-based diagnoses through targeted visual and source-code verification. Across thirteen closed-source and open-weight Video MLLMs, Gemini-2.5-Flash is the strongest standalone model with a score of 64.54, while V2Lens reaches 71.72, an improvement of 7.18 points. Together, our results show that video-grounded evaluation can move beyond isolated artifacts and aggregate outcomes toward a behaviorally faithful and diagnostically informative account of generated application quality.

</details>


### [231] [MSP-Net: Manifold-Guided Spectral Prompt Network for Hyperspectral Object Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09575)
*Juliu Li,Hanlin Qin,Shuowen Yang,Jingjing Li,Yuedong Tan,Shuai Yuan,Huixin Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MSP-Net is a Manifold-Guided Spectral Prompt Network for hyperspectral object tracking that reconstructs band relationships via graph-driven manifold routing, creates adaptive spectral groupings, and builds dynamic conditional prompts that adapt in real-time to target appearance and scene changes, achieving AUC >0.80 and Precision >0.96 on HOT2020 and HOT2023 benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing hyperspectral tracking methods treat HSI as multi-channel extensions of RGB images with fixed band-order fusion, which causes sensor-specific model dependence, neglects band manifold relationships (hurting cross-sensor generalization), and fails to capture dynamically changing discriminative band contributions across targets and scenes.

Method: MSP-Net (1) reconstructs band relationships and forms adaptive spectral grouping via graph-driven manifold routing, (2) jointly integrates grouped spectral statistics with template appearance to construct target-related dynamic conditional prompts, (3) continuously evolves spectral conditions based on intermediate target representations during tracking, and (4) uses reliable historical states to constrain target localization and scale fluctuations for temporal stability.

Result: Experiments on HOT2020 and HOT2023 show MSP-Net achieves AUC exceeding 0.80 and Precision exceeding 0.96, demonstrating robust performance under heterogeneous sensors, target deformation, and complex backgrounds.

Conclusion: The manifold-guided adaptive spectral prompt approach effectively overcomes sensor heterogeneity and dynamic scene variations in hyperspectral tracking, setting a new performance benchmark while maintaining temporal stability.

Abstract: Hyperspectral object tracking leverages abundant spectral information to provide unique advantages for target discrimination in complex scenes. However, existing methods typically treat hyperspectral images as multi-channel extensions of RGB images, performing feature fusion in fixed band order. This approach leads to models dependent on specific sensor configurations while neglecting manifold relationships between bands, making generalization to heterogeneous sensors difficult. Moreover, the discriminative contribution of bands dynamically changes with target attributes and scene variations, further limiting the representational capacity of static fusion strategies. To address this, we propose the Manifold-Guided Spectral Prompt Network (MSP-Net). This network first reconstructs band relationships and forms adaptive spectral grouping through graph-driven manifold routing, then jointly integrates grouped spectral statistics with template appearance to construct target-related dynamic conditional prompts, enhancing target features while suppressing background interference. Furthermore, as tracking progresses, spectral conditions continuously evolve based on intermediate target representations, enabling target prompts to adapt in real-time to appearance and scene changes. Meanwhile, reliable historical states are used to constrain target localization and scale fluctuations, significantly improving temporal stability in cross-sensor tracking. Experiments on HOT2020 and HOT2023 demonstrate that MSP-Net achieves AUC and Precision exceeding 0.80 and 0.96, respectively, exhibiting exceptional robustness under heterogeneous sensors, target deformation, and complex background conditions. The code will be released at https://github.com/GGML668897/MSP-Net.

</details>


### [232] [You Only Flow Once: Calibrated and Real-Time Radar Pose Estimation with Multi-Hypothesis Normalizing Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09579)
*Jonas Leo Mueller,Sebastian Hoefler,Dario Zanca,Naga Venkata Sai Jitin Jami,Thomas Altstidl,Bjoern M. Eskofier*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes MH-NFPG, a conditional normalizing flow model for multi-hypothesis human pose estimation from sparse, noisy millimeter-wave radar point clouds. It generates diverse pose distributions in a single parallel forward pass, outperforming diffusion-based methods in calibration and accuracy while being over 20x faster.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radar-based pose estimation is fundamentally ill-posed due to sparse and noisy point clouds that map to multiple plausible human poses. Existing methods are deterministic and collapse this ambiguity, while diffusion-based approaches model multi-hypothesis distributions but are computationally costly and lack calibrated uncertainty.

Method: MH-NFPG combines a spatiotemporal transformer backbone with a conditional normalizing flow that transforms a Laplace base distribution into an expressive posterior distribution, enabling parallel generation of multiple pose hypotheses through a single forward pass.

Result: The method outperforms diffusion-based alternatives in calibration across three radar benchmarks (MM-Fi, mmRadPose, mRI), improves pose accuracy on two benchmarks and matches on the third, achieves over 20x faster inference, and reduces calibration error by up to 85%. Diffusion models showed substantially degraded calibration, while the flow-based approach maintained reliable coverage including cross-environment settings.

Conclusion: Normalizing flows are a practical and efficient alternative to diffusion models for real-time, uncertainty-aware radar pose estimation, providing well-calibrated multi-hypothesis predictions without costly sequential sampling.

Abstract: Sparse and noisy millimeter-wave radar point cloud observations often correspond to multiple plausible human poses, making deterministic pose estimation fundamentally ill-posed. Yet existing radar methods remain deterministic, collapsing this ambiguity into a single estimate. Diffusion-based alternatives can model multi-hypothesis distributions but require costly sequential denoising for each distribution sample and lack calibrated uncertainty. We propose Multi-Hypothesis Normalizing Flow Pose Generator (MH-NFPG), which models pose distributions from radar point clouds using a conditional normalizing flow. Specifically, we combine a spatiotemporal transformer backbone with a normalizing flow that transforms a Laplace base distribution into an expressive posterior, generated in parallel through a single forward pass. Leveraging this efficiency, we outperform diffusion-based alternatives in calibration across three radar benchmarks (MM-Fi, mmRadPose, mRI), improve pose accuracy on two, and match it on the third, while achieving over 20x faster inference for applications and reducing calibration error by up to 85%. We find that calibration degrades substantially for diffusion models, whereas our flow-based approach maintains reliable coverage, also in cross-environment settings. These results demonstrate normalizing flows as a practical alternative to diffusion models for real-time, uncertainty-aware radar pose estimation. Our code will be made publicly available.

</details>


### [233] [GenTrack3: Hybrid Stochastic-Deterministic Online Multi-Object Tracking with Cluster-Aware Association](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09581)
*Toan Van Nguyen,Rasmus G. K. Christiansen,Dirk Kraft,Leon Bodenhagen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents an online multi-object tracking (MOT) framework that integrates deterministic tracking-by-detection principles with stochastic particle-based methods to achieve robust tracking under uncertainty. It introduces a novel track-to-detection matching approach for improved scalability and group tracking, with each target maintained as a tracklet with identifiers, states, velocities, track penalties, and track ages, and matched to detections via stochastic particle sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deterministic MOT approaches (e.g., tracking-by-detection with data association) are reproducible and efficient but rely heavily on motion models and are sensitive to noisy detections. Stochastic methods handle uncertainty and complex non-linear dynamics better but suffer from high computational cost and variability from random sampling. The paper aims to combine the strengths of both paradigms for robust tracking under uncertainty.

Method: The proposed online MOT framework integrates deterministic and stochastic principles. It introduces a novel track-to-detection matching approach for scalability with increasing target numbers and supports group tracking. Each target is represented as a tracklet containing identifiers, states, velocities, track penalties, and track ages. A stochastic particle set is associated with each target to compute matching costs to detections, enabling a systematic tracking pipeline.

Result: The paper provides reference implementations of the proposed approach and baseline trackers, available on GitHub (https://github.com/SDU-VelKoTek/GenTrack3), though specific quantitative performance metrics are not detailed in the abstract.

Conclusion: The hybrid deterministic-stochastic framework aims to achieve robust multi-object tracking under uncertainty while addressing scalability and group tracking needs through a novel matching approach and particle-based tracklet representation.

Abstract: Multi-object tracking (MOT) involves maintaining consistent target identities as objects dynamically enter and leave a scene. Deterministic approaches, such as tracking-by-detection with data association, produce reproducible results and are computationally efficient, but they rely heavily on motion models and are sensitive to noisy detections that can lead to association errors. In contrast, stochastic methods explicitly model uncertainty and can better handle complex non-linear dynamics, albeit at the cost of increased computational complexity and variability arising from random sampling. This paper presents an online MOT framework that integrates deterministic and stochastic principles to achieve robust tracking under uncertainty. Furthermore, a novel track-to-detection matching approach is introduced to enhance scalability with increasing target numbers while supporting group tracking. The tracking inference mechanism employs a tracklet that includes identifiers, states, velocities, track penalties and track ages of targets, supporting a systematic tracking pipeline. Each target is associated with a stochastic particle set to compute the matching cost to detections. Reference implementations of the proposed approach and baseline trackers can be found on GitHub: https://github.com/SDU-VelKoTek/GenTrack3.

</details>


### [234] [TeaMatch: Teachable Cross-Modal Representation Learning for 2D-3D Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09590)
*Chongjian Wang,Junjie Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TeaMatch is a novel framework for 2D-3D matching that introduces "teachability" as a criterion for cross-modal representation learning. It trains task-specific weak students to simulate failure modes, optimizes the teacher to improve students' recoverability of reliable correspondences, and achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging benchmarks without additional inference cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing detection-free methods for 2D-3D matching primarily optimize matching within a single model and struggle to maintain reliable correspondences under challenging conditions such as noisy inputs, low overlap, and ambiguous structures.

Method: TeaMatch introduces teachability as a criterion for representation learning. It constructs task-specific weak students simulating common failure modes, trains them to imitate the teacher on a training split, evaluates their recoverability on a disjoint meta split, and optimizes the teacher to improve students' ability to recover reliable correspondences using correspondence-level and geometry-aware constraints. It integrates into existing coarse-to-fine matching pipelines.

Result: TeaMatch improves matching robustness and achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging 2D-3D matching benchmarks.

Conclusion: Teachability serves as an effective learning criterion that yields more robust and structurally consistent cross-modal representations, improving 2D-3D matching performance without adding inference overhead.

Abstract: Learning reliable correspondences between images and point clouds is fundamental for 2D-3D matching. Despite recent progress in detection-free methods, existing approaches primarily optimize matching within a single model and often struggle to maintain reliable correspondences under challenging conditions such as noisy inputs, low overlap, and ambiguous structures. In this work, we propose TeaMatch, a novel framework that introduces teachability as a criterion for cross-modal representation learning. We define teachability as the ability of a representation to be effectively recovered by weak learners under degraded inputs, reflecting its structural consistency and robustness. To this end, we construct a set of task-specific weak students that simulate common failure modes and train them to imitate the teacher on a training split while evaluating their recoverability on a disjoint meta split. The teacher is then optimized to improve the students' ability to recover reliable correspondences, guided by correspondence-level and geometry-aware constraints. Our framework can be seamlessly integrated into existing coarse-to-fine matching pipelines without additional inference cost. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TeaMatch improves matching robustness and achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging 2D-3D matching benchmarks.

</details>


### [235] [Illusion or Integrity? Geometrical Consistency Metric for AIGC Video Quality Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09594)
*Yifei Xue,Yuanchen Fei,Hao Zhang,Chenzhi Nie,Tie ji,Yizhen Lao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoCon-Bench is a novel benchmark for evaluating AI-generated video quality by measuring geometric consistency across frames, serving as a proxy for physical law compliance. It captures global motion via translation estimation, fits homography/fundamental matrix models, and reports inlier ratio and geometric error metrics across 20 scenes in six motion categories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VQA metrics for AI-generated videos assess visual harmony, video-text consistency, and domain-specific alignment, but lack quantitative metrics for measuring fidelity to physical laws. This gap motivates the need for a benchmark that evaluates physical plausibility in generated videos.

Method: GeoCon-Bench quantitatively measures geometric consistency across frames extracted from generated sequences. It captures global motion through translation estimation, fits homography or fundamental matrix models using background correspondences, and computes complementary metrics including inlier ratio and geometric error.

Result: A dataset of 20 scenes across six motion categories is released, and experiments on state-of-the-art AIGC models demonstrate the reliability of GeoCon-Bench as a video quality assessment metric.

Conclusion: GeoCon-Bench provides a novel quantitative approach to evaluating physical law compliance in AI-generated videos through geometric consistency, addressing a critical gap in existing VQA frameworks.

Abstract: Recently, AI-driven video generation has attracted considerable attention. This surge increases the demand for reliable video quality assessment (VQA) metrics to evaluate AI-generated content (AIGC) videos and guide model optimization. Existing studies assess video quality through visual harmony, video-text consistency, and domain-specific alignment, yet lack quantitative metrics for measuring fidelity to physical laws. To address this limitation, we present a novel benchmark that evaluates the quality of AIGC videos based on their compliance with physical principles by quantitatively measuring geometric consistency across frames extracted from generated sequences. This serves as a proxy for estimating the extent to which generated videos conform to real-world physical rules. Specifically, GeoCon-Bench captures global motion through translation estimation, fits homography or fundamental matrix models using background correspondences, and reports complementary metrics, including inlier ratio and geometric error. We also release a dataset containing 20 scenes across six motion categories. Experiments on state-of-the-art AIGC models demonstrate the reliability of GeoCon-Bench as a video quality assessment metric.

</details>


### [236] [ResemBrick: Brick Reconstruction from Photographs with Perceptual Fidelity and Buildability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09597)
*Xilun Chen,Hanwen Wan,Yusong Zhao,Zexin Lin,Ruixiang Liao,Xiaoqiang Ji*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ResemBrick generates buildable, colored brick models of 3D objects from casual photographs by coupling discretization (budgeted occupancy completion via a resolution-conditioned network) and assembly (support-aware greedy placement with provably terminating repair), achieving zero floating/unstable bricks and best perceptual fidelity among prior brick systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior brick-model pipelines treat voxelization as fixed preprocessing and address either visual resemblance or structural stability separately, failing to optimize the discretization itself under a hard budget-limited voxel grid where these two goals conflict.

Method: ResemBrick couples discretization and assembly: (1) budgeted occupancy completion recasts voxelization as allocation, using a single resolution-conditioned network spanning 13 resolutions to select surface voxels in one feed-forward pass under a target occupied-voxel count; (2) buildability by construction uses support- and look-ahead-aware greedy placement plus a deterministic, provably terminating repair step that grounds floating components.

Result: Under a matched budget, ResemBrick outperforms existing voxel selectors in perceptual fidelity and uniquely achieves zero floating and zero unstable bricks on unfiltered held-out objects. As a full pipeline, it attains the best perceptual fidelity among prior brick-construction systems.

Conclusion: Discretization and assembly should be treated as tightly coupled stages rather than independent ones; jointly optimizing them yields brick models that are both visually faithful and physically buildable.

Abstract: Producing a hand-buildable, colored brick model of a 3D object from a few casual photographs is a clean testbed for a broader challenge: generating 3D content that meets hard physical-assembly constraints under a discrete, budget-limited voxel grid. On a coarse lattice, visual resemblance and structural stability pull against each other, yet prior brick pipelines address only one side and treat voxelization as fixed preprocessing rather than a variable to optimize. We present ResemBrick, which couples the two. Budgeted occupancy completion reframes discretization as allocation: given a target occupied-voxel count, a single resolution-conditioned network decides in one feed-forward pass which surface voxels to fill for best appearance, one weight set spanning 13 resolutions. Buildability by construction then combines support- and look-ahead-aware greedy placement with a deterministic, provably terminating repair that grounds every floating component. Under a matched budget, ResemBrick surpasses existing voxel selectors in perceptual fidelity while uniquely reaching zero floating and zero unstable bricks on unfiltered held-out objects; as a complete pipeline, it attains the best perceptual fidelity among prior brick-construction systems. Our results point to treating discretization and assembly as tightly coupled stages rather than independent ones.

</details>


### [237] [Structure-Enhanced Features and Quality-Aware Dynamic Anchor Scoring for Robust Lane Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09610)
*Weize Cai,Yongqi Dong,Zhida Shao,Yichen Liu,Zixin Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A structure-enhanced and quality-aware lane detection framework built on ADNet that uses a Gated Horizontal-Vertical Token (GHVT) module to improve backbone feature continuity and Line-Quality-Aware Dynamic Anchor Scoring (LQAS) to calibrate classification confidence with localization quality, achieving F1@50 score improvements on VIL-100, CULane, and TuSimple datasets with minimal overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Anchor-based lane detectors suffer from two coupled issues: backbone features lose structural continuity along partially visible/occluded lanes, and classification confidence is decoupled from line-level localization quality, allowing inaccurate anchors to persist before NMS. Both issues limit detection performance despite efficient candidate generation.

Method: The framework preserves ADNet's inference pipeline while adding two components: (1) a GHVT module that enhances mid/high-level backbone features via lightweight directional token interactions with a learnable residual gate, and (2) an LQAS mechanism that calibrates classification logits using quality supervision, hard-negative suppression, and pairwise ranking without adding inference branches.

Result: On VIL-100, the method improves ADNet-R34 from 89.97 to 91.28 in F1@50, reducing both false positives and false negatives. Additional experiments on CULane and TuSimple, along with ablations, score-distribution diagnostics, and runtime analysis, confirm complementary improvements with minimal computational overhead.

Conclusion: Structural feature enhancement and quality-aware anchor scoring are complementary and can jointly improve lane detection performance, addressing both representation continuity and confidence calibration issues without altering the efficient inference pipeline or adding significant computational cost.

Abstract: Lane detection requires recovering thin, elongated, and frequently occluded lane structures under challenging driving conditions. While anchor-based detectors provide efficient candidate generation, their performance is limited by two coupled issues: backbone features often lose structural continuity along partially visible lanes, and classification confidence may decouple from line-level localization quality, allowing inaccurate anchors to persist before non-maximum suppression (NMS). We propose a structure-enhanced and quality-aware framework that improves lane representation and dynamic-anchor scoring while preserving the inference pipeline of the Anchor Decomposition Network (ADNet). Specifically, a Gated Horizontal-Vertical Token (GHVT) module enhances mid- and high-level backbone features via lightweight directional token interactions with a learnable residual gate. In parallel, Line-Quality-Aware Dynamic Anchor Scoring (LQAS) calibrates existing classification logits using quality supervision, hard-negative suppression, and pairwise ranking without adding inference branches. On the VIL-100 dataset, our method improves ADNet-R34 from 89.97 to 91.28 in F1 score at the 0.5 intersection-over-union threshold (F1@50), reducing both false positives and false negatives. Additional experiments on CULane and TuSimple datasets, extensive ablations, score-distribution diagnostics, and runtime analysis confirm complementary structural and ranking improvements with minimal computational overhead.

</details>


### [238] [Marrying Optimal Transport and ODEs for Unified Continuous-Time 4D Reconstruction and Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09613)
*Liying Yang,Hao Mo,Jialun Liu,Chen Liu,Xinxing Yu,Chenhao Guan,Hui Ma,Xiao Cao,Ajian Liu,Yanyan Liang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Uni4R is a unified framework for 4D reconstruction and point tracking that learns continuous velocity fields via Optimal Transport and Ordinary Differential Equations, enabling kinematic-coherent modeling at arbitrary timestamps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing unified 4D reconstruction and point tracking methods rely on heuristic interpolations or only predict at integer timestamps, lacking kinematic coherence and failing to model dynamics at arbitrary timestamps.

Method: The framework uses a Flow Matching Guided Decoder (FMGD) with a global velocity branch extracting anchor features, plus Flow Matching theory to formulate a probability path via OT on the anchor feature manifold. A point reconstruction branch provides geometric features, and a local velocity prediction module decodes velocities at arbitrary timestamps. An integral-consistency training strategy uses an ODE solver to integrate velocities to recover target pointmaps, enabling end-to-end supervision from integer timestamps.

Result: Uni4R achieves state-of-the-art performance in both 4D reconstruction and point tracking, and SOTA on a new kinematics-aware benchmark at continuous time.

Conclusion: The synergy of OT and ODE provides a continuous velocity field acting as a kinematic prior that mutually benefits both tasks, overcoming the limitations of heuristic interpolation approaches.

Abstract: Existing unified 4D reconstruction and point tracking approaches typically rely on heuristic interpolations or just predict at integer timestamps, lacking kinematic coherence and failing to model dynamics at any arbitrary timestamp. In this paper, we propose Uni4R, a framework that unifies these tasks by learning continuous velocity fields through the synergy of Optimal Transport (OT) and Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE). Importantly, this continuous velocity field acts as a kinematic prior that mutually benefits both 4D reconstruction and point tracking. Specifically, we propose the Flow Matching Guided Decoder (FMGD). A global velocity branch first extracts anchor features that capture the global dynamic state of the sequence. Then, FMGD leverages Flow Matching (FM) theory to formulate a probability path defined by OT on the anchor feature manifold, instantiating it as FM-guided velocity features for velocity prediction. This establishes a robust kinematic inductive bias. Meanwhile, a point reconstruction branch provides geometric features. The local velocity prediction module then joint above features and time embeddings, to decode velocities at arbitrary timestamps. To overcome the absence of high-quality ground-truth velocities in fractional frames, we propose an integral-consistency training strategy. This strategy uses an ODE solver to integrate velocities to recover target pointmaps, enabling the model to be supervised end-to-end directly from integer timestamps. Experimental results demonstrate that Uni4R achieves SOTA performance in both 4D reconstruction and point tracking, and achieves SOTA in our new kinematics-aware benchmark at continuous time.

</details>


### [239] [LoRA-based Adaptation Alone Is Not Enough: Understanding the Limits of Foundation Models for Face Presentation Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09633)
*Peter Lorenz,Anjith George,Marcel Sébastien*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper systematically evaluates 32 foundation models for face presentation attack detection (PAD). Zero-shot prompting performs near chance, while low-rank adaptation (LoRA) with less than 1% trainable weights achieves below 2% intra-dataset ACER but substantially higher cross-dataset ACER, suggesting that pretrained representations and adaptation data matter more than lightweight adaptation for generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PAD methods perform well within datasets but degrade significantly under cross-dataset evaluation due to variations in sensors and lighting. Existing PAD systems predominantly focus on CLIP-based foundation models, overlooking other FMs with different architectures and training procedures. This study addresses that gap by systematically evaluating a broad range of foundation models.

Method: The study systematically evaluates 32 foundation models across different architectures and training procedures. It tests zero-shot prompting and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) with fewer than 1% trainable weights on the MCIO benchmarks (MSU-MFSD, CASIA-FASD, Replay-Attack, and OULU-NPU), measuring both intra-dataset and cross-dataset ACER.

Result: Zero-shot prompting achieves near-chance performance across all model families and scales. With LoRA, most models achieve below 2% intra-dataset ACER, but cross-dataset ACER is substantially higher. The findings indicate that LoRA primarily refines within-dataset decision boundaries rather than improving cross-dataset generalization.

Conclusion: Pretrained representations and the adaptation dataset play a more significant role in cross-dataset generalization than the lightweight LoRA adaptation strategy evaluated. The performance gap between intra-dataset and cross-dataset evaluations highlights the ongoing challenge of generalizable PAD.

Abstract: Face presentation attack detection (PAD) aims to reliably detect a wide range of presentation attacks. While PAD methods achieve strong performance within individual datasets, their performance degrades under cross-dataset evaluation. Variations in sensors or lighting conditions can reduce the effectiveness of detectors from near-perfect to nearly random. Foundation models (FMs) have emerged as a promising alternative because typical PAD datasets, such as the MCIO benchmarks (MSU-MFSD, CASIA-FASD, Replay-Attack, and OULU-NPU), are small relative to the scale used for web-based pretraining. However, existing PAD systems primarily focus on CLIP-based foundation models, while overlooking other FMs with different architectures and training procedures. This study addresses this question by systematically evaluating 32 FMs. Zero-shot prompting achieves performance near chance across model families and scales. The vision encoders, when low-rankadapted (LoRA) with fewer than 1% trainable weights, achieve below 2% intra-dataset ACER in most cases, while cross-dataset ACER is substantially higher. LoRA primarily refines the decision boundary within a dataset, suggesting that pretrained representations and the adaptation dataset play a larger role in cross-dataset generalization than the evaluated lightweight adaptation strategy.

</details>


### [240] [NeuroRefiner: Morphology-Aware Multi-Agent Refinement for 3D Fluorescence Microscopy Neuron Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09636)
*Haiyang Yan,Jinyue Guo,Yanchao Zhang,Bingqing Wang,Zhenchen Li,Jing Liu,Jiazheng Liu,Linlin Li,Hua Han*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NeuroRefiner is a multi-agent system for accurate 3D neuron segmentation in fluorescence microscopy, combining three collaborative agents (diagnosis, instruction generation, validation) with TopoRefineNet, a 3D U-Net tool using cross-modality feature fusion. It formalizes human expert workflows through iterative global observation and local editing, achieving state-of-the-art results on BigNeuron, CWMBS, and ZBFWB datasets with a 3.02% F1 improvement on ZBFWB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3D neuron segmentation is critical for neuroscience but challenging due to sparse and elongated neuron morphology. Existing methods struggle to preserve both local details and global topology, resulting in fragmented segmentation outputs.

Method: NeuroRefiner is a multi-agent system with three collaborative agents: one for diagnosing topological errors, one for generating correction instructions, and one for validating refinement quality. It includes TopoRefineNet, a 3D U-Net-based tool leveraging cross-modality feature fusion for instruction-guided segmentation refinement. The system iteratively performs global observation and local editing, mimicking human expert workflows over multiple reasoning rounds.

Result: NeuroRefiner outperforms state-of-the-art methods on BigNeuron, CWMBS, and ZBFWB datasets, notably achieving a 3.02% improvement in F1 score on the challenging ZBFWB dataset, producing topologically more accurate segmentations with enhanced interpretability.

Conclusion: Through multi-round agent reasoning and voxel-level editing, NeuroRefiner effectively addresses the topological fragmentation problem in neuron segmentation, offering more accurate and interpretable results than existing approaches.

Abstract: Accurate 3D neuron segmentation in fluorescence microscopy is critical for neuroscience. However, the sparse and elongated morphology of neurons poses significant challenges to existing segmentation methods. These methods struggle to preserve both local details and global topology, leading to fragmented results. To address this, we propose NeuroRefiner, a multi-agent system that formalizes the human expert workflow involving iterative global observation and local editing. Specifically, NeuroRefiner comprises three collaborative agents dedicated to diagnosing topological errors, generating correction instructions, and validating refinement quality. To facilitate agent instruction-guided segmentation refinement, we propose TopoRefineNet, a dedicated 3D U-Net-based tool that leverages cross-modality feature fusion to generate refined masks. Through multi-round agent reasoning and voxel-level editing, NeuroRefiner produces topologically more accurate segmentations with enhanced interpretability. Experiments on the BigNeuron, CWMBS, and ZBFWB datasets demonstrate that NeuroRefiner outperforms state-of-the-art methods, notably achieving a 3.02% improvement in F1 score on the challenging ZBFWB dataset.

</details>


### [241] [DUET: A Diversity-Quality Duet of Distillation Experts for Two-Step Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09637)
*Zian Li,Litong Gong,Borui Liao,Pengfei Liu,Xinyu Wang,Xinyuan Wei,Yifan Gao,Tiezheng Ge,Muhan Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DUET reconciles the quality-diversity trade-off in two-step video generation by combining two distilling paradigms as a noise-level duet of experts: an sCM expert handles the high-noise step for diversity, and a DMD expert handles the low-noise step for quality. DUET+ further applies RL-guided expert adaptation to address relay interface bottlenecks, achieving DMD-level quality with nearly twice the structural diversity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion models enable high-quality video generation but are costly due to iterative sampling. Few-step distillation reduces this cost but exposes a quality-diversity trade-off between trajectory-level distillation (sCM favors diversity) and distribution-level distillation (DMD favors quality), which existing loss-level combinations fail to resolve.

Method: DUET decomposes two-step video generation by noise level: an sCM expert trained with its native objective takes the high-noise step to lay out diverse structure, while a DMD expert takes the low-noise step to refine appearance detail. Since the experts are trained independently with their native objectives, DUET sidesteps loss-level optimization difficulties. DUET+ introduces RL-guided expert adaptation to address the relay interface and high-noise stage bottlenecks.

Result: Using the Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B backbone, DUET lifts two-step quality of sCM close to DMD while retaining about twice the structural diversity of DMD. DUET+ further improves overall quality while preserving the diversity advantage.

Conclusion: Noise-level expert specialization is a simple and effective paradigm for reconciling diversity and quality in two-step video generation, eliminating the need to trade one for the other.

Abstract: Diffusion models have enabled high-quality video generation in recent years, but the high cost of iterative sampling hinders their practical deployment. Few-step distillation alleviates this cost, yet exposes a quality--diversity trade-off between its two dominant paradigms: trajectory-level distillation (e.g., sCM) favors diversity, whereas distribution-level distillation (e.g., DMD) favors quality. Targeting extreme two-step video generation, we introduce DUET, which reconciles the two paradigms through a noise-level duet of experts: an sCM expert takes the high-noise step to lay out diverse structure, and a DMD expert takes the low-noise step to refine appearance detail. Since the two experts are trained independently with their native objectives, DUET sidesteps the optimization difficulties of loss-level combinations and delivers quality and diversity jointly rather than trading one for the other. We further identify the relay interface and the high-noise stage as the remaining bottlenecks, and address them with RL-guided expert adaptation, yielding DUET+. With the Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B backbone, DUET lifts the two-step quality of sCM close to the level of DMD while retaining nearly all of its structural diversity---about twice that of DMD---and DUET+ further improves overall quality while preserving this diversity advantage. Together, these results establish noise-level expert specialization as a simple, effective paradigm for reconciling diversity and quality in two-step video generation.

</details>


### [242] [EgoHieraLoc: A Cortically Inspired Hierarchical Segmentation-Guided Framework for Egocentric Visual Query Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09656)
*Yifei Cao,Guolong Wang,Mingliang Hou,Xiya Bu,Daming Liu,Yu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes EgoHieraLoc, a unified framework for visual query localization (VQL) in 2D and 3D, inspired by hierarchical human vision, featuring a Discriminative Parsing Module, Query-Aware Module, Regional Adaptation Module, and Geometric-Semantic Joint Confidence (GSJC) for credible multi-view 3D estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VQL remains challenging due to ambiguous object boundaries and ineffective global context for fine-grained localization. Human vision handles this through hierarchical processing: foreground screening, selective attention, global-local feedback, and credibility-based multi-view integration.

Method: EgoHieraLoc consists of: (1) Discriminative Parsing Module using segmentation priors for foreground-aware query representations; (2) Query-Aware Module for robust target localization via discriminative correlation filtering with deformable modeling; (3) Regional Adaptation Module feeding multi-scale context back to local regions for precise boundaries; (4) GSJC coupling segmentation confidence with local depth consistency, multi-view back-projection consistency, and triangulation-baseline quality for credible 3D estimation.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on both VQL-2D and VQL-3D benchmarks.

Conclusion: The unified hierarchical framework inspired by human vision effectively addresses both 2D and 3D visual query localization challenges, with learned modules and credibility-aware multi-view fusion contributing to superior performance.

Abstract: Visual query localization (VQL) aims to retrieve and re-localize a queried object in egocentric videos, yet remains challenging when object boundaries are ambiguous and global context cannot effectively guide fine-grained localization. Human vision handles such ambiguity through a hierarchical process: it rapidly screens foreground candidates, selectively attends to the target despite distractors, refines perception via feedback between global context and local detail, and, when a single view is unreliable, integrates evidence across viewpoints according to its credibility. Inspired by these competencies, we propose \textbf{EgoHieraLoc}, a unified framework for VQL-2D and VQL-3D. A Discriminative Parsing Module first extracts foreground-aware query representations using segmentation priors; a Query-Aware Module then performs robust target localization through discriminative correlation filtering with deformable modeling; and a Regional Adaptation Module feeds multi-scale context back into local regions to recover precise object boundaries. To extend this perceptual hierarchy to 3D localization, we introduce Geometric-Semantic Joint Confidence (GSJC), which multiplicatively couples segmentation confidence with local depth consistency, multi-view back-projection consistency, and triangulation-baseline quality, so that a viewpoint contributes to the 3D estimate only when it is credible both semantically and geometrically. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on both VQL-2D and -3D benchmarks.

</details>


### [243] [CIFA: Contextual-Intersectional Fairness Auditing for Hidden Subgroup Discovery in Face Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09669)
*Nazia Aslam,Khalid Adnan Alsayed,Thomas B. Moeslund,Kamal Nasrollahi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes CIFA, a structured framework for auditing fairness in computer vision models by examining intersections of demographic and contextual attributes (e.g., illumination, blur, image quality). Evaluated on gender classification with ResNet-50 and ViT-B/16 across three face datasets, it shows that aggregate accuracy and demographic-only evaluations can mask significant contextual-intersectional disparities, and that no single mitigation strategy consistently eliminates worst-group disparities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing fairness evaluation in computer vision relies primarily on aggregate accuracy and demographic subgroup analysis, ignoring that contextual factors (illumination, blur, image quality, facial accessories, appearance) can interact with demographics to create hidden subgroups where model performance degrades substantially despite strong aggregate metrics and acceptable demographic fairness.

Method: CIFA is a three-stage framework: (1) demographic auditing, (2) contextual auditing, and (3) contextual-intersectional auditing, followed by worst-group discovery to identify and rank the most vulnerable attribute combinations. An audit-mitigate-reaudit protocol is used to assess mitigation strategies.

Result: Evaluations on gender classification using ResNet-50 and ViT-B/16 across FairFace, CelebA, and UTKFace reveal that aggregate accuracy and demographic-only evaluations can mask substantial contextual-intersectional disparities. Mitigation strategies reduce some worst-group disparities, but no single strategy consistently eliminates them across datasets and architectures.

Conclusion: Contextual-intersectional auditing is an important and necessary component of fairness evaluation for face analysis systems. The framework is reproducible and useful for discovering, prioritizing, and reassessing hidden subgroup risks that standard evaluation methods overlook.

Abstract: Fairness evaluation in computer vision commonly relies on aggregate accuracy and demographic subgroup analysis. However, visual models are also sensitive to contextual factors such as illumination, blur, image quality, facial accessories, and appearance attributes. These factors may interact with demographic characteristics, producing hidden subgroups in which performance degrades substantially despite strong aggregate accuracy and apparently acceptable demographic fairness. To address this, we propose the Contextual-Intersectional Fairness Auditing Framework (CIFA), a structured framework for identifying subgroup vulnerabilities arising from interactions between demographic and contextual attributes. CIFA performs demographic, contextual, and contextual-intersectional auditing, followed by worst-group discovery to identify and rank the most vulnerable attribute combinations. We evaluate CIFA on gender classification using ResNet-50 \cite{he2016deep} and ViT-B/16 \cite{dosovitskiy2020image} across FairFace \cite{Karkkainen2021}, CelebA \cite{Liu2015}, and UTKFace \cite{Zhang2017}. Our results show that aggregate accuracy and demographic-only evaluation can mask substantial contextual-intersectional disparities. We further assess several established mitigation strategies through an audit--mitigate--reaudit protocol and find that, although some worst-group disparities are reduced, no single strategy consistently eliminates them across datasets and architectures. These findings establish contextual-intersectional auditing as an important component of fairness evaluation and provide a reproducible framework for discovering, prioritizing, and reassessing hidden subgroup risks in face analysis systems.

</details>


### [244] [MPISuperRes-PnP: A Super-Resolution Zero-Shot Plug-and-Play Reconstruction Algorithm for Magnetic Particle Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09672)
*Vladyslav Gapyak,Thomas März,Andreas Weinmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a plug-and-play super-resolution (SR) approach for Magnetic Particle Imaging (MPI) based on energy minimization. The method incorporates SR directly into the reconstruction task using a splitting scheme, where the Gaussian denoising subproblem is handled by a pre-trained learned denoiser in a zero-shot fashion—requiring no training data for MPI. The method is evaluated on synthetic and real MPI data, showing applicability without hallucination artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MPI reconstruction produces very coarse spatial grids, making super-resolution techniques essential. Existing SR methods (system matrix upscaling, reconstruction interpolation) have limitations, and deep learning approaches suffer from the scarcity of MPI training data. The authors aim to combine benefits of deep learning with energy minimization while avoiding the need for training data.

Method: The proposed approach integrates super-resolution into the MPI reconstruction task via an energy minimization formulation. Following the plug-and-play framework, a splitting scheme is derived where the Gaussian denoising subtask is solved with a pre-trained learned Gaussian denoiser in a zero-shot fashion. Hyper-parameters are selected via an extended parameter search. The generic SR framework allows different regularizers or imaging tasks.

Result: The method was evaluated quantitatively and qualitatively on both synthetic and real data (MPIData: EquilibriumModelWithAnisotropy and 2D-OpenMPI Data). The pre-trained denoiser behaved conservatively—no hallucination artifacts were observed. The method demonstrates applicability without requiring MPI-specific training data.

Conclusion: The proposed plug-and-play SR method for MPI successfully leverages deep-learning denoising without training, avoiding the need for scarce MPI training data. The approach is generic and extensible to other regularizers and imaging tasks, making it a promising direction for future MPI super-resolution applications.

Abstract: Magnetic Particle Imaging (MPI) is an emerging medical imaging modality. MPI is based on the non-linear response of magnetic nanoparticles to an applied magnetic field and avoids ionizing radiation. The measured signal is the voltage induced in receive coils by the particles' response. Reconstructing the particle concentration from the signal constitutes the imaging task. Even using state-of-the-art measurement-based reconstruction, the associated spatial grid is very coarse, hence super-resolution (SR) techniques are important. In this work, we propose an approach for SR in MPI inspired by energy minimization. Different methods have been proposed for SR in MPI, ranging from upscaling of the associated system matrix to interpolation of the reconstruction. Here we incorporate SR into the reconstruction task via an energy minimization formulation. Following the plug-and-play approach to energy minimization we derive a splitting scheme and a SR method for MPI where the arising Gaussian denoising task is treated with a pre-trained learned Gaussian denoiser in a zero-shot fashion. This way, we incorporate benefits of deep learning without training and avoid the need of training data. Further, we provide a quantitative and qualitative evaluation of the proposed method. Hyper-parameter are selected via an extended parameter search. The found parameters are applied for reconstruction on real data. We show the applicability of our method on synthetic and on real data (MPIData: EquilibriumModelWithAnisotropy and 2D-OpenMPI Data). The proposed method employs a deep-learning denoiser without training -- thus it does not require presently scarcely available MPI training data. The denoiser behaves conservatively, i.e., no hallucination artifacts were observed. The SR approach is generic such that it can be applied in future MPI contexts involving different regularizers or different imaging tasks.

</details>


### [245] [Thinking With Tools, Not With Pixels: Tool Calls as Text Scaffolds for Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09682)
*Jiahao Shao,Yuanbo Yang,Yiyi Liao,Yujun Shen,Ceyuan Yang,Yinghao Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TextCall (call-but-no-return) is introduced to test whether returned images in tool-augmented vision-language models are actually load-bearing. Three studies show that removing the returned images (replacing them with a text placeholder) matches or exceeds full "thinking-with-images" performance across LoRA, full fine-tuning, and RL, while reducing latency by 29-46% and eliminating tool-execution API calls. The active signal is the structured text scaffold (tool name, coordinates, target description, intent) emitted at tool-call time, confirming the Tool-Call Scaffold Hypothesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent work using blind tests, gain decompositions, and attention analyses has shown that returned images in tool-augmented vision-language models contribute little to performance. This raises the question of what actually carries the gain if not the pixels, motivating an investigation into where the load-bearing signal truly resides.

Method: The paper introduces TextCall, which keeps the structured text scaffold (tool name, coordinates, target description, intent) emitted before returned pixels arrive, but replaces returned images with the placeholder "[Image output skipped]". Three studies are conducted: (i) non-necessity of returned pixels across LoRA, full fine-tuning, and RL; (ii) sufficiency of the scaffold on matched training queries; (iii) component specificity decomposing the scaffold into reasoning text and spatial code.

Result: TextCall matches or exceeds full thinking-with-images across all training paradigms. Under RL, it preserves tool use at the reported checkpoint, avoiding the failure mode where seeing returned images causes models to stop calling tools. Scaffold-only input yields equivalent accuracy to returned-image input on matched queries, and both reasoning text and spatial code contribute with task-dependent dominance. Latency is reduced by 29-46% and tool-execution API calls are eliminated.

Conclusion: The Tool-Call Scaffold Hypothesis is confirmed: in current thinking-with-images distributions, the active signal is the structured text emitted at tool-call time, and the returned image acts as a redundant carrier. TextCall provides an efficient alternative, though constructing tasks where pixels are genuinely load-bearing remains an open direction for future work.

Abstract: Tool-augmented vision-language models increasingly "think with images": they call crop, zoom, or code tools and reason over the returned pixels. However, recent work using blind tests, gain decompositions, and attention analyses has shown that returned images contribute little, raising the question: if pixels do not carry the gain, what does? We hypothesize that the load-bearing signal is the structured text emitted before any returned pixel arrives: tool name, coordinates, target description, and intent. This textual scaffold encodes where to look and what to find. We introduce TextCall (call-but-no-return) to test this: it keeps the scaffold but replaces returned images with the text placeholder [Image output skipped]. Three studies support the hypothesis. (i) Non-necessity of returned pixels: across LoRA, full fine-tuning, and RL, TextCall matches or exceeds full thinking-with-images; under RL it preserves tool use at the reported checkpoint, avoiding the failure mode where, under matched settings, seeing the returned image causes the model to stop calling tools and answer directly. (ii) Sufficiency of the scaffold: on matched training queries, scaffold-only input yields equivalent accuracy to returned-image input. (iii) Component specificity: decomposing the scaffold into reasoning text and spatial code shows both components contribute, with the dominant one varying by task. Together these results support the Tool-Call Scaffold Hypothesis: in current thinking-with-images distributions, the active signal is the structured text emitted at tool-call time; the returned image is a redundant carrier. TextCall preserves accuracy while reducing latency by 29-46% and eliminating tool-execution API calls. Our claims hold for current thinking-with-images benchmarks; constructing tasks where pixels are genuinely load-bearing remains an open direction.

</details>


### [246] [Diffuse the object, keep its label: curating detector training data from a few unlabeled photographs via VLM-built 3D vegetation scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09691)
*Mario Malizia,Marnix Enting,Rob Haelterman,Ken Hasselmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes synthesizing labeled training images for small-object detection in vegetation by generating coarse 3D vegetation scenes from unlabeled site photographs using a vision-language model, placing 3D object meshes to obtain annotations, and re-texturing renders via a diffusion pass with a graded mask-lock. A standard detector trained on these synthetic images matched or exceeded one trained on labeled data from another site on a humanitarian-demining benchmark, demonstrating effective unsupervised site adaptation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Labeled images of small objects hidden in vegetation are scarce, and detectors trained on them generalize poorly across sites. Rather than reusing labels from another site, the authors aim to synthesize training data from unlabeled photographs of the deployment site itself, avoiding manual annotation while achieving better domain adaptation.

Method: 1) A vision-language model generates a coarse 3D vegetation scene from a single unlabeled photograph. 2) 3D object meshes are placed in the scene, yielding bounding boxes, segmentation masks, and per-instance occlusion from scene geometry automatically. 3) A lightweight adapter fine-tuned on the site photographs conditions a diffusion pass that re-textures renders. 4) A graded mask-lock controls how much diffusion can modify the object itself. 5) A standard detector is trained on the synthetic images.

Result: The graded mask-lock was the most influential curation choice: lightly diffusing the object improves minority-class recall over fully protecting its pixels, while unrestricted diffusion dissolves it. The trained detector matched or exceeded a counterpart trained on a larger labeled dataset of real images from a different site, consistently across seeds on a humanitarian-demining benchmark. Gains were largely insensitive to photograph and crop budgets, and in-domain accuracy did not predict cross-site performance.

Conclusion: Unsupervised site adaptation via synthesized labeled images from a handful of unlabeled photographs can outperform conventional cross-site label reuse, offering a practical alternative when labeled data from the deployment site is unavailable.

Abstract: Labeled images of small objects hidden in vegetation are scarce, and detectors trained on them generalize poorly across sites. Rather than reusing labels collected at another site, we synthesize labeled training images from a handful of unlabeled photographs of the deployment site itself. A vision--language model generates a coarse 3D vegetation scene from one photograph; placing 3D object meshes in the scene yields bounding boxes, segmentation masks, and per-instance occlusion directly from the scene geometry, without manual annotation. A lightweight adapter fine-tuned on the photographs conditions a diffusion pass that re-textures the renders, and a graded mask-lock sets how much diffusion may touch the object itself. In our runs this grade was the most influential curation choice: lightly diffusing the object improves minority-class recall over fully protecting its pixels, while unrestricted diffusion dissolves it. Trained on these images, a standard detector matched or exceeded its counterpart trained on a larger labeled dataset of real images from a different site, consistently across seeds on a humanitarian-demining benchmark; the comparison is thus unsupervised site adaptation from a handful of photographs against conventional cross-site label reuse. In our ablations the gains were largely insensitive to the photograph and crop budgets, and in-domain accuracy did not predict cross-site performance.

</details>


### [247] [LookAgain: Closed-Loop GUI Grounding with Visually Grounded Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09723)
*Renshan Zhang,Haoyang Meng,Yixiao He,Rui Shao,April Hua Liu,Liqiang Nie*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LookAgain is a closed-loop GUI grounder that reformulates grounding as a multi-turn predict-look-again-refine process, allowing the model to reflect on and revise its initial coordinate predictions based on new visual evidence, achieving state-of-the-art results on GUI grounding benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing GUI grounders suffer from degraded performance on small targets, densely packed controls, and out-of-distribution interfaces. The authors attribute this to a paradigmatic limitation: no existing approach treats a produced coordinate as a hypothesis that can be reflected upon and revised under new visual evidence. This manifests in three coupled issues: lack of post-hoc reflection, visual evidence decoupled from the prediction, and refinement over views rather than over predictions.

Method: LookAgain reformulates GUI grounding as a multi-turn predict-look-again-refine process using two primitives: (1) "locate" which posts a coordinate hypothesis, renders a marker on the image, and appends a local patch of the predicted region, anchoring the next reasoning step to the previous prediction as a spatial prior; and (2) "confirm" which accepts or rejects the hypothesis and terminates the procedure. The model is trained with SFT on constructed reflective trajectories as a cold start, followed by GRPO with terminal grounding correctness as the sole reward.

Result: LookAgain consistently improves performance on both refusal-aware and general GUI grounding benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art results. Comprehensive ablations verify the effectiveness of the proposed framework.

Conclusion: The closed-loop reflection paradigm, where predictions are treated as revisable hypotheses with post-hoc visual evidence, effectively addresses the limitations of existing single-shot GUI grounding approaches and sets a new state-of-the-art benchmark.

Abstract: Recent graphical user interface (GUI) grounders have significantly advanced single-shot accuracy on standard benchmarks, yet their performance degrades sharply on small targets, densely packed controls and out-of-distribution interfaces. We attribute this gap to a paradigmatic limitation shared by existing approaches: none of them treats a produced coordinate as a hypothesis to be reflected upon and revised under new visual evidence. This manifests as three coupled issues: 1) Lack of post-hoc reflection. The prediction is frozen at the moment of emission, leaving no internal mechanism to challenge or refine it. 2) Visual evidence decoupled from the prediction. The auxiliary visual evidence is gathered to support the upcoming coordinate rather than to scrutinise the one already committed to. 3) Refinement over views, not over predictions. The iterative zoom-in refines the inspected region instead of inheriting a previous coordinate as a spatial prior to be corrected. In this paper, we propose LookAgain, a closed-loop GUI grounder driven by post-prediction visual reflection. LookAgain reformulates grounding as a multi-turn predict-look-again-refine process with two primitives: "locate" posts a coordinate hypothesis, renders a marker on the image and appends a local patch of the predicted region. It anchors the next reasoning step to the previous prediction as a spatial prior; "confirm" accepts or reject the hypothesis and terminates the procedure. We train the LookAgain grounder with SFT on constructed reflective trajectories as a cold start, followed by GRPO with terminal grounding correctness as the sole reward. Extensive experiments show that LookAgain consistently improves performance on both refusal-aware and general GUI grounding benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art results. Comprehensive ablations further verify the effectiveness of the proposed framework.

</details>


### [248] [World Tokens: Enhancing Embodied Policies with Training-Time World Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09730)
*Qu Tang,Benhui Zhuang,Bo Yuan,Xue Yu,Longteng Guo,Junlan Feng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: World Tokens is an embodied policy architecture using a World Adapter that bridges vision-language understanding, world-dynamics modeling, and action generation. It uses world modeling during training to improve the action policy while removing the world-model branch at deployment for efficient VLA-level latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLA models excel at closed-loop control but do not explicitly model physical scene evolution. World-action models (WAMs) capture spatiotemporal dynamics but incur substantial inference cost due to future generation or large video backbones in the control loop.

Method: The World Adapter transforms VLM features into a fixed set of world tokens that condition a jointly fine-tuned future-video denoiser and serve as the action expert's sole visual-language context. Shared conditioning lets denoising gradients shape action representations, while exclusive routing prevents policy bypassing. At deployment, the world-model branch is removed, leaving only VLM, World Adapter, and action expert.

Result: With a 2B backbone and no embodied action pretraining, World Tokens is highly competitive on LIBERO, attains the best reported averages on SIMPLER, substantially improves real-world R1 Pro success over a matched action-only baseline, and generates action chunks at VLA-level latency.

Conclusion: World Tokens demonstrates that world modeling during training can enhance action policies without sacrificing deployment efficiency, achieving competitive performance while maintaining VLA-level latency.

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are a widely adopted paradigm for embodied policies. They excel at efficient closed-loop control but do not explicitly model how physical scenes evolve as a task unfolds. Recently emerging world-action models (WAMs) leverage pretrained video world models to capture spatiotemporal evolution, yet retaining future generation or a large video backbone in the control loop substantially increases inference cost. We introduce World Tokens, an embodied policy architecture built around a World Adapter that bridges visual-language understanding, world-dynamics modeling, and action generation. It uses world modeling during training to enhance the action policy while preserving efficient deployment. Specifically, the World Adapter transforms VLM features into a fixed set of world tokens, which condition a jointly fine-tuned future-video denoiser and simultaneously serve as the action expert's sole visual-language context. This shared conditioning allows gradients from future-video denoising to directly shape the representation used for action prediction, while exclusive routing prevents the policy from bypassing that representation. At deployment, the world-model branch is removed, leaving only the VLM, World Adapter, and action expert, with no online video-model inference. With a 2B backbone and no embodied action pretraining, World Tokens is highly competitive on LIBERO, attains the best reported averages on SIMPLER, substantially improves real-world R1 Pro success over a matched action-only baseline, and generates each action chunk at VLA-level latency.

</details>


### [249] [HandSplatter: Automated Digital Goniometry from Neural Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09735)
*Emmett Chen,Neal Chen,Xiang Li,Quanzheng Li,Siyeop Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a novel 3D hand joint pose estimation pipeline using neural rendering that combines 2D feature extraction with view synthesis to accurately measure finger range of motion, along with a discrete density hill climbing algorithm for correcting projected landmarks in 3D space, providing a more precise and objective alternative to manual goniometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hand and finger disorders are leading causes of musculoskeletal disability, and precise joint motion quantification is clinically needed. The current standard—manual goniometry—is labor-intensive and has inconsistent inter-rater reliability. Existing software-based digital alternatives lack sufficient accuracy for clinical use.

Method: The authors propose a novel pipeline for 3D hand joint location and pose estimation using neural rendering. The approach integrates 2D feature extraction with view synthesis to enhance accuracy and clinical viability. Additionally, a discrete density hill climbing algorithm is introduced to enable meaningful correction of projected landmarks in 3D space.

Result: The proposed system significantly improves accuracy compared to previous software-based approaches and demonstrates clinical viability, overcoming the inefficiencies of manual measurement and inaccuracies of existing digital tools for objective functional assessment of hand joint motion.

Conclusion: The neural rendering-based pipeline with the density hill climbing landmark correction provides a robust, objective tool for assessing hand joint range of motion, addressing the limitations of both manual goniometry and existing digital alternatives.

Abstract: Hand and finger disorders are leading contributors to musculoskeletal disability, creating a clinical need for precise methods to quantify joint motion. Range of motion (ROM) serves as the metric for diagnosis, rehabilitation monitoring, and evaluating surgical outcomes. Currently, the goniometer is the standard tool for assessing finger flexion and extension. However, manual goniometry is labor-intensive and suffers from inconsistent inter-rater reliability due to variations in examiner technique. While digital alternatives exist, current software-based approaches often lack the necessary accuracy for clinical usage. To address these limitations, we present a novel pipeline for 3-D hand joint location and pose estimation using neural rendering. Unlike previous methods, our approach combines 2-D feature extraction with view synthesis to significantly improve accuracy and clinical viability. Furthermore, we introduce a discrete density hill climbing algorithm that facilitates the meaningful correction of projected landmarks in 3-D space. This system overcomes the inefficiencies of manual measurement and the inaccuracies of existing software, providing a robust tool for objective functional assessment.

</details>


### [250] [Disentangling Co-Occurring Retinal Pathologies with Saliency-Guided Sparse Expert Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09752)
*Nagur Shareef Shaik,Jeongwoo Park,Yeong-Jin Kim,Jaeuk Jung,Hyunjung Oh,Dong Hye Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a sparse conditional computation architecture for multi-disease retinal fundus image classification, combining a Guided Context Gating (GCG) spatial attention front-end with a sparsely-routed Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) block. The routing is interpretable and disease-dependent, achieving 0.912 macro AUC and 0.653 macro F1 on a five-class benchmark, with visualizations confirming expert alignment with lesions and co-occurring disease mapping.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard deep learning classifiers apply static, identical computation to every retinal fundus image, ignoring that images frequently exhibit multiple co-occurring pathologies with varying disease distributions. This motivates a dynamic, condition-aware architecture.

Method: The architecture pairs a Guided Context Gating (GCG) spatial attention front-end with a sparsely-routed Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) block operating over feature tokens, enabling sparse conditional computation and interpretable, data-driven expert routing.

Result: The model achieves 0.912 +/- 0.008 macro AUC and 0.653 +/- 0.014 macro F1 on a five-class, patient-disjoint 5-fold cross-validation benchmark. Expert allocation is significantly disease-dependent (p < 0.001), and Grad-CAM++ plus post-MoE t-SNE visualizations confirm routing aligns with localized lesions and maps co-occurring cases between constituent clusters.

Conclusion: Sparse Mixture-of-Experts serves as an interpretable approach for multi-disease retinal screening, with expert routing that is disease-dependent, geometrically meaningful, and aligned with clinically relevant features.

Abstract: Retinal fundus images frequently exhibit multiple co-occurring pathologies, yet standard deep learning classifiers apply static, identical computation to every image regardless of the underlying disease distribution. We propose a novel architecture that resolves this via sparse conditional computation, pairing a Guided Context Gating (GCG) spatial attention front-end with a sparsely-routed Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) block operating over feature tokens. Crucially, this routing yields an interpretable, data-driven decomposition. Expert allocation is significantly disease-dependent (p < 0.001), with the healthy Normal state and morphologically distinct pathologies (e.g., ERM, AMD) isolating to dedicated experts. On a five-class, patient-disjoint 5-fold cross-validation benchmark, our model achieves 0.912 +/- 0.008 macro AUC and 0.653 +/- 0.014 macro F1. Furthermore, Grad-CAM++ and post-MoE t-SNE visualizations confirm that expert routing aligns with localized lesions and geometrically maps co-occurring cases between their constituent clusters, positioning sparse MoE as an interpretable approach to multi-disease retinal screening.

</details>


### [251] [C$^2$A: Coupling Spatial Evidence with Clinical Priors via Co-occurrence Aware Class Attention for Multi-Label Chest X-Ray Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09774)
*Akash Gogineni,Nagur Shareef Shaik,Aasrith Mandava,Adnan Masood,Dong Hye Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: C²A is a co-occurrence aware class attention classification head for chest X-ray multi-label classification that couples spatial attention maps with learnable co-occurrence priors via a graph message-passing step, achieving 0.895 macro-mean AUROC on CheXpert with gains concentrated on classes with ambiguous spatial evidence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thoracic pathologies rarely occur in isolation, but standard multi-label classifiers use shared global descriptors that discard where findings are located and how they co-occur, limiting performance especially for classes with ambiguous spatial evidence.

Method: C²A casts pooling as an expectation over learned per-class spatial attention maps to obtain localized descriptors, then couples these via a learnable graph warm-started from empirical label co-occurrence, using a single residual message-passing step that acts as a bounded perturbation of identity with explicit bilinear co-occurrence interactions.

Result: C²A achieves 0.895 macro-mean AUROC on CheXpert, outperforming context-gating baselines, with gains concentrated on highly co-occurrent classes with ambiguous spatial evidence (e.g., +1.5 for Atelectasis over GCG).

Conclusion: Incorporating spatial localization and co-occurrence priors improves multi-label classification with negligible computational overhead, demonstrating that clinical co-occurrence priors regularize ambiguous classes effectively.

Abstract: Thoracic pathologies rarely occur in isolation, yet standard multi-label classifiers rely on shared global descriptors, discarding \emph{where} findings lie and \emph{how} they co-occur. We propose \textbf{C$\mathbf{^2}$A} (Co-occurrence Aware Class Attention), a classification head that explicitly couples spatial evidence with clinical priors. First, C$^2$A casts pooling as an expectation over learned per-class spatial attention maps, yielding localized descriptors for each disease. Second, it couples these descriptors via a learnable graph warm-started from empirical label co-occurrence.
